# Voyager II — The Butterfly Garden

**Protocol:** Open Seas (Home Waters plus web research)
**File created:** 2026-11-17 12:15:44 UTC
**Codebase reviewed:** `main` @ `4b51bad` (version 3.9.0, with the 4.0 plan of 2–3 November 2026)

> This is the second Voyager report. The first,
> [Voyager-2026-10-26_18-48-37-UTC.md](Voyager-2026-10-26_18-48-37-UTC.md),
> reviewed 3.0.0 and led to 3.1–3.9. This one starts from what shipped, scores
> it again, and plans what comes next. It builds on the owner's current plans
> ([4.0](plans/4.0-android-packaging.md), [future update](plans/future-update.md))
> and refers to their items by id (P-n, D-n, W-n) rather than repeating them.

## At a glance

- **Where the project stands.** The Project State Score is **85 %** today.
  It was 75 % at 3.0. It reaches 89 % once (a)'s 4.0 rows land, 92 % once
  every package does, and 93 % once D-7 lands too. The first Voyager's plan
  landed almost whole. What is left sits at the edges: midnight, a second
  tab, a restore in flight, an Android save screen, a night palette and an
  iPad held sideways. See [a.1](#a1-scorecard).
- **(a) Fixes & Integrity.** Twenty-eight readers covered every line and
  reported 432 findings. None were critical. After adversarial
  verification and measurement, 3 high and 29 medium remain, with about
  250 lows, written up as F-1 to F-46. The 3 high ones are navigation
  missing at iPad-landscape widths, no visible keyboard focus on buttons,
  and night colours at 1.05–3.0:1. Eight work packages (WP-A to WP-H) fix them all
  with no loss of capability. The data-integrity and Android ones go into
  4.0. See [a.6](#a6-order-effort-and-risk).
- **(b) Strengthening Existing Features.** 86 improvements across eight
  areas: 31 Now, 37 Next and 18 Later. The first ones are small. Support
  lines come in the gardener's own region first, with Text beside Call. The
  comfort plan is offered after a heavy check-in. A recovery sheet reads as
  it is printed. The Journal has no giant zero on day one. See
  [b.10](#b10-what-to-do-first).
- **(c) Major New Capabilities.** Six to build after 4.0:
  - Recently removed
  - backups that keep themselves on Android and under the lock
  - a letter to open later
  - a garden check
  - fingerprint or face unlock in the Android app
  - sharing a photo into the field book

  Three pilots: two gardens one journal, a quiet widget, and on-device
  speaking on desktop. Six ideas are not recommended, each with its reason.
  See [c.0](#c0-how-these-were-chosen-and-what-they-cost) and
  [c.9](#c9-what-to-build-and-when).
- **For the owner.** 45 new questions, D-47 to D-91, each with a
  recommendation: [a.9](#a9-new-questions-for-the-owner),
  [b.11](#b11-questions-for-the-owner) and
  [c.10](#c10-questions-for-the-owner).

## (a) Fixes & Integrity — the Zero-Loss Plan

**Scope.** The whole product this time: the PWA and the Android app that 4.0
takes to Google Play. Every fix keeps every capability. Where a fix needs the
owner, it is marked **[owner]** and listed in [a.9](#a9-new-questions-for-the-owner).
Items the owner has already decided are cited by id and built around, not
re-argued.

### a.1 Scorecard

Each figure answers one question: *for an app of this scope, how much material
work is left on this axis?* 100 % would mean none. The Project State Score is
the plain average of the six. "After" is what the plan below should reach if
every work package lands. It is a target, not a promise.

| Metric | 3.0 (Voyager I) | **Today (3.9.0)** | After plan | Why it sits where it does |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| **Project State Score** | 75 % | **85 %** | 92 % (93 % with D-7) | The first plan landed almost whole. What is left is at the edges: midnight, a second tab, a restore in flight, an Android save screen, a night palette. |
| Health | 78 % | **87 %** | 95 % | Every check is green: 3,241 unit tests and 208 end-to-end tests pass. Twenty-eight readers and 55 adversarial verifications found **nothing critical, and nothing high in the data path**. The three high defects are in presentation, each measured: no navigation on screen at iPad-landscape widths, no visible keyboard focus on buttons, and night colours down to 1.05:1. The confirmed medium ones are narrow: a save that started before a restore and lands after it, words typed before midnight left out of reach after it, an Android overwrite that keeps an old file's tail. |
| Security | 82 % | **86 %** | 93 % (95 with D-7) | The cryptography holds up under a line-by-line read: one sealed key pair per way in, AAD on every seal, non-extractable keys, 600,000 PBKDF2 rounds (still OWASP's figure). The CSP allows only `'self'`, nothing that ships has a known vulnerability, and Capacitor's critical CVE-2026-103922 is patched. Held back by the shared `github.io` origin (D-7), which no code can fix; a 6-character passcode with no strength reading, guessable offline in under a minute when it is six digits; the Android Recents card showing the unlocked journal; and two hardening steps measured free but not taken (`'unsafe-inline'` and Trusted Types). |
| Architecture | 74 % | **79 %** | 86 % | Pure reducers, one repository, invariants written down and pinned. But `SettingsView` is 1,661 lines, `App.tsx` 1,213, and the repository is one ~1,230-line closure holding nine pieces of mutable state. That closure is where the save-ordering defects live. The CSS is still 10.5 k lines in three overriding sheets (D-29). Twenty-three files read `VITE_PLATFORM` directly, 18 pages are bare `lazy()` imports with no retry, and one test harness is copied into 17 files. |
| Documentation | 80 % | **87 %** | 93 % | ARCHITECTURE, the plans with their D/P/W ids, and the comments explaining *why* are unusually good. Drift is small but visible to gardeners: the open-source notices name stale versions and are shown in the app. The README says every check runs on every push (P-17 paused some), one doc says `platform.ts` is the only build switch, and six status documents overlap. |
| Tests | 72 % | **87 %** | 94 % | 95 % of statements and 92 % of branches covered, plus axe sweeps, computed-style baselines and a frame guard. But nothing in the e2e suite checks invariant 8 (nothing leaves the device), axe's "incomplete" results are dropped, and the sweeps run only by day at the default text size, which is how night prices at 1.71:1 got through. No test checks the 1,081–1,180 px band where navigation disappears. The suite also depends on the host's locale and on the day not changing mid-run. |
| Performance | 68 % | **86 %** | 92 % | Measured: the garden holds 60 fps at 4× slower CPU with zero layouts (it was 22 fps), and five years of journal costs under 100 ms per action. Held back by an invisible button animation that takes 88 % of a busy page's main thread, a flight loop that keeps a throttled phone's main thread 39 % busy, an Android first load with 245 B to spare, a precache 2.2× larger than at 3.0, and audio that does not recover after an interruption. |

### a.2 How this was measured

Every number below comes from running the code at `main` @ `4b51bad`, in a 4-CPU Linux container with Node 24.22.1 (what CI uses), npm 11, Chromium 153.0.8010.12 and Playwright 1.63. Readers and probes shared the CPU, so durations are upper bounds.

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `npm run typecheck` (`tsc -b`: app, node, e2e) | Pass, 17 s |
| `npm run lint` (type-aware ESLint) | Pass, 0 warnings, 44 s |
| `npm run knip` | Clean |
| `npm test` (Vitest, coverage) | **3,241 / 3,241 pass** in 306 files, 285 s. Statements 95.33 %, branches 92.25 %, functions 94.01 %, lines 96.67 % (floors: 84 / 78 / 80 / 85). 93 "Not implemented: Window's scrollTo()" lines of jsdom noise. |
| `npm run test:e2e` | **208 pass, 2 skipped by design**, 0 minor axe warnings, 19.0 min on 2 workers (desktop, Pixel 7, Android bundle) |
| `npm audit --omit=dev` | 0 vulnerabilities in what ships |
| `npm audit` (dev too) | 13 moderate, all one `minimatch` advisory reached through ESLint and typescript-eslint |
| `npm outdated` | Within range: Vite 8.3.8, typescript-eslint 8.73, eslint 10.15, knip 6.45, fake-indexeddb 6.2.8. Held majors (ARCHITECTURE's Toolchain table): TypeScript 7.0.2, Vitest 5.1, vite-plugin-pwa 2.0, jsdom 31, Playwright 1.65, `@axe-core/playwright` 4.14. Capacitor 8.6.2 is out (the app is on 8.5.3). |
| `npm run build` | Entry JS 345.1 kB (108.4 kB gzip), `repository` chunk 85.8 kB (28.4 kB gzip), one first-load stylesheet 102.2 kB (22.5 kB gzip). Precache: **156 entries, 2,536 KiB** (3.0 had 50 entries, 1,176 KiB). Four icons are listed twice. |
| `npm run budgets` | Web: entry 107.2 kB, CSS 22.5 kB, first load 168.4 kB of a 169.2 kB limit. **Android: first load 169.0 kB: about 245 B to spare.** |
| First-load composition (sourcemap attribution) | React DOM 45 %, app 51 %. Largest app parts: `App.tsx` 19.6 k chars, `validateGarden` 16.5 k, `FlowerSprite` 15.7 k, `gardenRepository` 14.7 k, `ButterflySprite` 13.3 k, `GardenView` 13.0 k, `OutfitOverlay` 10.2 k, `data/content` 9.1 k, `data/shopItems` 7.6 k. |
| CSP probe (production build, 17 pages × light and dark) | With `style-src 'self'` (no `'unsafe-inline'`): **0 violations**. With Trusted Types enforced: **exactly 1 sink**, the service worker's `register()`. |
| Scale probe | Pixel 7 profile at 4× slower CPU, a garden with 5 daily goals and a check-in, reflection and recap every day. Longest main-thread task per action: none (empty), 61–72 ms (1 year, 0.8 M characters), 51–93 ms (3 years), 60–96 ms (5 years, 4.1 M characters); first action of a day, which also writes the daily copy, up to 280 ms in all at 5 years. Longest task at launch: 56 ms (empty) to 173 ms (5 years). |
| Scene probe | Pixel 7, 12 dressed butterflies and 8 plants, 4 s window: **60 fps at 4× slower CPU, 0 layouts**, p95 frame 16.8 ms; main thread busy 39 % of the window (241 style recalculations, one a frame); 9.5 % at normal speed; 5,500 DOM nodes. (3.0: 22 fps before its fix, 51–53 after.) |

**How the code was read.** Every tracked text file was assigned to one of 28
partitions: production code by concern (shell and state, repository, the lock's
cryptography and its screens, domain rules, content, platform, data, views,
sprites, both stylesheets), the unit tests in six parts, the e2e suite, Android
with CI and config, the docs, and the generated material. Each partition went
to a reader who read **every line** of it, wrote dense per-file notes, and
returned findings with evidence, a failure scenario and a fix. The readers
covered about 198,000 lines between them, counting the code each opened to
check a claim, and each listed anything it could not read in full. Only `package-lock.json` and the style baselines were read by
script rather than by eye (every one of the 722 lockfile entries was parsed).
Then, independently:

- **Adversarial verification.** Every finding a reader rated medium or higher
  went to a separate verifier told to *refute* it. The verifier read the code
  and its guards, and reproduced the finding with a throwaway test where it
  could. Findings rated partly confirmed or uncertain got a second verifier.
  Severities below are the verified ones, which are often lower than the
  readers' (the `adopt()` race and the empty-PRF case both dropped to low).
- **Measured in a browser.** Layout, tap targets, contrast, CSP and regex
  findings were measured directly, as the table shows.
- **Lead reading.** The shell, entry, build config, stored types, the lock's
  key hierarchy, sealing, the recovery and move codes, the save path and the
  persistence hook were read line by line again by the author, independently
  of the readers.

### a.3 Where the first Voyager's plan went

Of about 95 named recommendations in the first report, **about 78 are done,
12 partly done, 5 parked and 3 deliberately declined**:

- **Declined:** backdrop images as Blobs, a React context with selectors, and
  a Web Lock around delete. Each has its reason recorded.
- **Still open:**
  - D-7: the PWA's own origin, and with it real security headers.
  - D-29: the CSS consolidation (cascade layers, no `!important`).
  - P-7 to P-10: the four Android items that a.9 parked.
  - D-28: other languages.
  - The entry chunk's 95 kB target, overtaken when React 19.3 raised the cap.

The data-loss findings that led that report (D1 to D4: one tab overwriting
another, a damaged backup crashing every launch, no persistent storage, an
unreadable garden greeted as a newcomer) are all fixed and pinned by tests.
So is its headline performance finding: the garden moved to the compositor.

### a.4 Findings

**At a glance.** The readers reported 432 findings: none critical,
6 high, 72 medium, 279 low and 75 info. The 6 high reports are 4 distinct
defects: the two stylesheet readers each found the same two (navigation and
focus), one found the night colours, and the fourth (`card-rise`, F-45) was
measured and lowered to medium, leaving 3 high. Every
medium or higher finding was then verified, and the lead measured the layout,
contrast and timing ones in a browser. Verification confirmed most mediums but
lowered about a third of them, and the high ones were all confirmed by
measurement. The table counts only what survived. Each line
names where the problem is, how it was confirmed (**R** means reproduced by a
test, **M** means measured in a browser, **T** means traced in the code), and
the work package that fixes it.

| Severity | Count | Where most of them live |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| High | 3 | Navigation missing at iPad-landscape widths; no visible keyboard focus on buttons; night colours at 1.05–3.0:1 |
| Medium | 29 | Save ordering around a restore or a second tab; words left behind at midnight or by a lock; Android save screens; audio recovery; focus after actions; the 24 MP photo limit; crisis lines missing for Australia and New Zealand |
| Low | ~250 | Edges, copy and test quality, tracked in the readers' notes and summarised under each work package |

#### Data integrity and durability

| Id | Finding | Where | How confirmed | Fix in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F-1 | **A save that began before a restore, a put-back or a conflict reload can land after it and write the old garden back.** `save()` reads its baseline when its queued job starts, not when it was asked for. `load()`, `adopt()` and `replace()` change that baseline outside the queue. The single-tab form (a state change committed while a restore is being written) affects the Android app too; the cross-tab forms need two tabs and millisecond timing. The related `adopt()` race (an earlier write's parts merged under a later revision) is the same root cause. | `repository/gardenRepository.ts` (`save`, `adopt`, `replace`), `hooks/usePersistence.ts:289` | R (repository and real hook), two verifiers | WP-B |
| F-2 | **"Start fresh" can overwrite a garden whose set-aside copy never landed.** The copy just written can be pruned in the same transaction after a clock rewind, and a swallowed quota error leaves only an older copy, which still satisfies the guard. The download then offers the wrong copy. | `repository/setAside.ts:67`, `gardenRepository.ts:1151` | R (4 tests), two readers independently | WP-A |
| F-3 | **Words typed on Today before midnight are left where no screen can reach them.** The check-in note, feelings, reflection and prompt are drafted under the day they began. Once the date moves on (midnight, a tab left open overnight, a morning reload) nothing offers them back, and they are pruned a week later. The weather does carry over, so a Save after midnight files yesterday's mood under today. | `components/TodayView.tsx:185` | R | WP-A |
| F-4 | **An edit to a past check-in, reflection or recap is lost to a lock, a reload or leaving the page**, and the lock screen does not name it, though it names other forms. | `components/JournalView.tsx:117` | R | WP-A |
| F-5 | **Settings' name, garden name and motion form writes stale values back after a restore, a put-back or another tab's change.** It is seeded once at mount and never follows the garden. | `components/SettingsView.tsx:394` | R | WP-A |
| F-6 | **A downloaded earlier copy can never be restored.** No import path reads the `the-butterfly-garden-snapshot` envelope, and the refusal says the file is from a newer version. | `components/backup/EarlierCopies.tsx:82` | R | WP-A |
| F-7 | **The lock screen's "delete and start again" deletes for good without first offering a sealed copy**, though safe mode refuses its own delete until one is saved. | `components/lock/LockScreen.tsx:237` | R | WP-A |
| F-8 | **On Android an auto-lock during the system save screen destroys a move file's one-time code, a new recovery code's confirmation, or a ready protected backup**, and the lock screen does not say so. | `components/move/MoveDialog.tsx:136`, `lock/LockWatch.tsx` | R | WP-E |
| F-9 | **Android: overwriting an existing backup through the save screen can leave a corrupt file reported as saved.** The plugin writes with mode `"w"`, which Android 10+ providers need not truncate. A shorter backup then keeps the old file's tail, and a later restore refuses it. | `android/.../GardenFilesPlugin.java:167` | T, and Android's own javadoc, AOSP and DuckDuckGo's identical fix | WP-E |
| F-10 | **Android: a whole backup crosses the bridge as one escaped string.** Capacitor's `org.json` parse peaks at about 4–7× the file, so a large field book can exhaust the app's heap during Save. | `lib/native.ts`, `GardenFilesPlugin.java:169` | T (traced into Capacitor 8.5.3) | WP-E |
| F-11 | **The sighting form can vanish while its photo is preparing and leave an invisible dialog behind.** The third Escape closes it natively, after which "Log a sighting" does nothing and the hidden form holds the auto-lock. `CustomBackdropEditor` shares the pattern. | `components/sightings/SightingForm.tsx:187` | R (Chromium probe and unit test) | WP-A |
| F-12 | Lows of the same family: a restored key spelled like the sealing format's internal marker (`\u0000bytes`) makes a locked garden unreadable; `flushDrafts` does not wait for a write already under way; ids minted on read are never written back; duplicate ids are accepted; several limits cut text mid-emoji (a lone surrogate that the lock turns into U+FFFD); daily copies are pruned by their day string, so a reset clock deletes the copy just made. | various | R / T | WP-A |

#### Security and privacy

| Id | Finding | Where | How confirmed | Fix in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F-13 | **The PWA shares its origin with every other page at `dionnedeh.github.io` (D-7).** Any of them can read the garden's IndexedDB (in plain text unless the lock is on), post `garden-clearing` or an update reload on the garden's own channels, and is offered the garden's passkey. Password managers also save the lock passcode, the protected-backup passphrase and a locked copy's passcode as interchangeable secrets for one site. GitHub Pages still cannot send headers, so `frame-ancestors`, COOP and the rest wait on the move. This is the largest structural risk left, and code alone cannot remove it. | `index.html`, `lib/appUpdate.ts:101`, `hooks/usePersistence.ts` | T | WP-C (owner: D-7) |
| F-14 | **The passcode is the weakest way in to every copy of a locked garden, and nothing says how weak.** The only rule is 6 characters. One RTX 5090 runs about 18,600 guesses a second against PBKDF2-SHA-256 at 600,000 rounds, so every 6-digit code falls in under a minute, 8 digits in about 1.5 hours, and 6 lowercase letters in about 4.6 hours, for anyone holding a safe-mode copy, a moved-address file or the device's storage. Four unrelated words would take about 30 GPU-years. | `lib/gardenLock.ts:454`, `components/lock/LockSetup.tsx:85` | T, with a measured hashcat figure (11,157 kH/s at 999 rounds) | WP-C |
| F-15 | **The Android Recents card shows the unlocked journal, or a move file's 12 words, for as long as the garden then sits locked in the background.** Capacitor reports going to the background only at `onStop`, after the snapshot, and the shortest auto-lock is a minute. | `android/.../MainActivity.java:19` | R (static scan and unit test) | WP-E |
| F-16 | **Turning the lock on leaves the desktop's kept-up-to-date backup file in plain text, and the moment of locking does not say so.** The pause is correct. Only the wording is missing. | `lib/autoBackupFile.ts:159`, `lock/LockSetup.tsx` | R | WP-C |
| F-17 | **Two free hardening steps are not taken.** The production build does not need `style-src 'unsafe-inline'`: removing it caused 0 violations. Enforcing Trusted Types touches exactly one sink, the service worker's `register()`. | `index.html:26` | M (17 pages, both themes) | WP-C |
| F-18 | **A crafted link can freeze the app at launch.** The trailing-slash regex in `routeFromHash` is quadratic: 80,000 slashes and one more character take 6.0 s here, about 24 s on a slow phone, again on every load of that link. | `lib/routes.ts:166` | M | WP-C |
| F-19 | **The moved-address carry-over ships a locked garden as the raw locked copy**, guarded only by the passcode (F-14), although a 128-bit move file already exists for the same job. | `components/MovedBanner.tsx:106` | T | WP-C (P-15) |
| F-20 | Lows: an empty or malformed PRF answer seals the passkey slot under a constant key (needs a misbehaving authenticator); `passkeysOffered` hides passkeys when a browser leaves `extension:prf` out of its capabilities; the stored lock record's iteration count is unbounded on unlock; the recovery list includes "cutting" and "rope"; the CSV guard misses full-width formula characters (＝ ＋ － ＠); a new photo is briefly written unsealed to a check database while the lock is on; routes kept in `history.state` are trusted unchecked. | various | R / T | WP-C |

#### Accessibility integrity (measured)

| Id | Finding | Where | How confirmed | Fix in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F-21 | **High. Between 1,081 and 1,180 px wide the PWA has no navigation on screen.** The header's tabs are hidden and the phone dock turns `position: static` at the very end of the page. 1,180 px is the landscape width of the iPad (10th generation) and iPad Air 11". | `App.css:2157` | M (and screenshot) | WP-D |
| F-22 | **High. Primary and secondary buttons show no keyboard focus.** `:focus-visible` gives them a transparent 2 px outline and nothing else (WCAG 2.4.7). Where a control is also *selected* (a plant, a chip), the selected style overwrites the focus ring. | `garden-3.css:106`, `App.css:3396` | M | WP-D |
| F-23 | **High. Five day colours have no night twin**: the goal editor's weekday picker at **1.05:1**, shop prices at 1.71–1.90:1 (49 on one tab), the rhythm card's month line at 1.61:1, the capacity note at 2.61:1, and the planner's Sunlight note at 3.00:1. All pass by day. Axe reported none of them. It files text it cannot measure as "incomplete" (25–379 elements per page at night), and the suite drops those. | `App.css:1166, 1438, 1620, 1185, 3470` | M (pixel sweep of 3,152 text elements; a second reader's figures match to the hundredth) | WP-D |
| F-24 | **On phones each plant is a 22 px target over an 84 px sprite**: only 28 % of a sprite's middle row reaches its own button, below WCAG 2.2's 24 px minimum. | `App.css:746` | M | WP-D |
| F-25 | **Between 621 and about 725 px wide the outer plant is clipped** (by 102 px at 621 px), breaking CLAUDE.md's own layout invariant. | `App.css:716` | M | WP-D |
| F-26 | **Focus is lost after actions**: the Journal timeline (Edit, Save, Delete), Undo in Today's toast, closing a shop "Try on", the seed tray, Settings' delete, and the recovery and safe-mode confirmations. Focus falls to `<body>`, the ring disappears and a screen reader hears nothing. | `JournalView.tsx:564`, `UndoToast.tsx:54`, `shop/ItemPreview.tsx`, `GardenView.tsx:679` | R | WP-D |
| F-27 | Lows: Care's companion picker claims the tabs pattern without a panel or arrow keys; petting shows no hearts with reduced motion; holding Enter on a butterfly opens its card and then presses "Care for…"; one `document.title` for every page; every "Star this line" has the same name; no forced-colours support; reduced-motion rules miss transitions and pseudo-elements; the splash ignores in-app motion and night settings; W-1's label-in-name work. | various | R / M | WP-D |
| F-45 | **Every card keeps an identity transform after its entrance animation** (`card-rise … both`), which makes each card the containing block for any `position: fixed` child. The field book's Undo toast is then placed against its card, often below the screen, instead of against the viewport. A probe placed a fixed element 157 px below a 839 px screen. | `theme-aurora.css:421` | M | WP-D |
| F-46 | Presentation mediums from the stylesheets: the phone dock is 100 % wide but offset 11 px, so its right edge and corner are cut off by the screen; gold gradient "eyebrow" text outside the app shell (the lock screen by day, onboarding) is 1.4–2.7:1; the header's "Install app" spills out of a 36 px circle on phones; no `color-scheme` follows the chosen theme, so native controls stay light at night; text fields in dialogs are 12.8 px, so iOS zooms the page on focus (the passphrase and move words); italics render upright for want of an italic face (scientific names among them); some shop and Guide text is under 10 px; and the PWA applies no left or right safe-area insets in landscape. | `garden-3.css:881, 943, 1149`, `theme-aurora.css:121`, `index.css:5, 30`, `android.css:18` | M / T | WP-D |

#### Performance

| Id | Finding | Where | How confirmed | Fix in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F-28 | **An infinite `gradient-drift` animates an invisible background on every primary button**: 88 % of the Shop's main-thread time on a 4× slower phone (1.26 s busy per 4 s and 240 style recalculations, against 0.15 s and 27 without it). | `App.css:3056` | M | WP-F |
| F-29 | **The garden's flight loop keeps a throttled phone's main thread 39 % busy** with one style recalculation a frame (9.5 % at normal speed). The frame rate is fine; the battery cost is not nothing for a garden left open. | `hooks/useFlightMotion.ts`, `lib/flightMotion.ts` | M | WP-F |
| F-30 | **The first load is at its limit.** Android has about 245 B to spare. About 2.4 kB gzip is reclaimable with no change on screen: catalogue description text (2.2 kB) that only lazy pages show, the other platform's Pass provider (~190 B, not folded), and ~146 lines of dead CSS. | `data/shopItems.ts`, `lib/gardenPass.ts:93`, `App.css:1824` | M | WP-F |
| F-31 | **The precache is 2.2× its 3.0 size** (156 entries, 2.5 MB). Four icons are listed twice and `icon-512` is cached though no page draws it. The Android bundle carries 93 kB of web-only icons, and its launcher PNGs would be 35 % smaller as lossless WebP. | `vite.config.ts:133` | M | WP-F |
| F-32 | **The breath tone and the soundscape never recover from an audio interruption** (a call, Siri, an alarm on iOS). The tone also queues every missed turn on the frozen clock: 51 live nodes after two minutes, sounding together as a loud chord if WebKit resumes. | `lib/breathTone.ts:51`, `hooks/useAmbientSound.ts:262` | R | WP-F |
| F-33 | **The 24-megapixel decode limit refuses every default photo from an iPhone 15 or later** (5,712 × 4,284 = 24.47 MP), for field-book photos and personal backdrops alike. The limit's own comment calls it an untested guardrail. | `lib/imageImport.ts:68`, `lib/customBackdrops.ts:24` | T (arithmetic) | WP-F |
| F-34 | Lows: the build id hashes the whole lockfile, so a dev-only bump offers every gardener an empty update; no-op reducers return a new garden, which writes and broadcasts; a personal image's `data:` URL is rebuilt on every render; the Guide's deferred search defers nothing; Safari encodes a full-size PNG before every image; a timed-out scene download is thrown away and started again; the aurora is a page-sized layer that never stops, with blurred chrome re-filtered every frame; `.garden-stats` blurs 18 px beneath an opaque background; fonts swap in with no preload or metric-matched fallback, so first paint reflows. Scale is fine as measured (a.2). | various | M / T | WP-F |

#### Safety and correctness of what the garden says

| Id | Finding | Where | How confirmed | Fix in |

| F-35 | **"If you need support now" has no number for Australia or New Zealand**, both regions the app offers. Verified on 17 November 2026 on the services' own sites: Lifeline Australia, call 13 11 14 or text 0477 13 11 14 (emergency 000); New Zealand's 1737, call or text, free, 24/7 (emergency 111). The support numbers' 30-day freshness test is also a release behind: it can never catch the release being shipped. | `data/support.ts:22`, `data/support.release.test.ts:14` | T, numbers verified at source | WP-A |
| F-36 | **The comfort plan's call links misdial the mixed form "+cc (0area)" for dozens of trunk-zero countries**, refuse two-digit emergency numbers (France's 15, 17 and 18) as "too short to call", and refuse full-width or Arabic-Indic digits. | `lib/comfortPlanEdits.ts:131–171` | R | WP-A |
| F-37 | **Care tells a gardener whose Pass is resting** (lapsed, on hold, or just "checking" for the first seconds of every launch) that a Pass piece is "waiting for a fitting stage" and invites them to take it off, against the Pass panel's promise that it is "resting, not deleted". | `components/CareView.tsx:495` | R | WP-A |
| F-38 | Lows: the Journal's "Delete this check-in for good?" is not true while daily copies keep the entry for up to seven days; onboarding says care activities earn Sunlight (they never do); a third of the garden's day and night lines can never be shown; a page whose code fails to load once stays broken for the session; the cyclic sigh refuses a shorter breath out after a slow-breathing preference of 5–6 s; a calendar file with a new UID adds a second daily event; "Back to the garden" on the error screen does nothing when the garden page itself is what failed (its reset key does not change), and its copy calls a failed save a failed read. | various | R / T | WP-A, WP-G |

#### Tests, CI and docs

| Id | Finding | Where | How confirmed | Fix in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F-39 | **Invariant 8 (nothing leaves the device) has no test.** No e2e listens for an off-origin request, a CSP violation or a page error, and no unit test pins the CSP. | `e2e/a11y.ts`, `src/indexHtml.test.ts` | T | WP-H |
| F-40 | **Axe's "incomplete" results are dropped, and every sweep runs by day at the default text size.** That is how F-23 reached production. | `e2e/a11y.ts:20`, `e2e/app.spec.ts:1413` | M | WP-H |
| F-41 | **The suite depends on the host.** Under a German locale at least 14 tests in 7 files fail; the 24-hour-step tests fail in the fall-back hour (W-5, reproduced); several e2e tests compare dates across a possible midnight (W-15); and the move-file test is flaky because "garden" is both a recovery word and part of the sealed envelope's format string. | `vite.config.ts:218` and others | R | WP-H |
| F-42 | Coverage floors sit 10–14 points under the suite's figures (D-30); the test helpers diverge from browsers (synchronous BroadcastChannel, no "interrupted" AudioContext, a dialog polyfill); there is no e2e of a real service-worker update or of two tabs; the App harness is copied into 17 files and the e2e garden openers into 14; the lock-change and lock-screen failure paths have no test, though their copy promises the old secret still works; the month planner's core job (planning a goal onto a day) has no component test. | various | T | WP-H |
| F-43 | Docs: the in-app open-source notices name stale versions and omit Workbox's runtime; the README says every check runs on every push; one doc says `platform.ts` is the only build switch; six status documents overlap; CLAUDE.md's checks still omit knip and the budgets, which D-10 decided to add. | `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md:17`, `README.md:140`, `CLAUDE.md:14` | T | WP-H |
| F-44 | CI and Android build: CodeQL scans no Java; the signing job restores caches from ordinary runs and runs npm install scripts with the upload key's passwords in its environment; `versionCode` is the run number, and the signature check is not tied to the expected certificate; Dependabot splits Capacitor packages that must move together. | `.github/workflows/android.yml:112–144`, `security.yml:37`, `dependabot.yml` | T | WP-H |

**Checked and found sound.** A heavy audit should also say what held up:

- **The lock's key design.** One sealed P-256 key pair per way in, the data key
  sealed to each public half with ECDH and HKDF, AAD binding every seal to its
  slot and lock, a new data key whenever a secret changes, and raw key bytes
  wiped after use.
- **Recovery and move codes.** The recovery code carries 264 bits. The move
  code carries 128 bits with a check that catches a typo before the slow
  unlock.
- **Iteration caps on untrusted files.** Protected backups and locked copies
  refuse more than 10 M PBKDF2 rounds, so a crafted file cannot hang the
  unlock.
- **Prototype pollution.** Untrusted backup JSON cannot change
  `Object.prototype`, because parsing and object spread only ever create own
  properties.
- **Scale.** Five years of daily use costs under 100 ms per action on a slow
  phone.
- **The scene.** The garden holds 60 fps at 4× slower CPU with zero layouts.
- **Android hardening.** No cleartext traffic, system CAs only, cloud backup
  off with device-to-device transfer kept, WebView metrics opted out, file
  access and geolocation off.
- **Play compliance.** The app targets API 36 (required since 31 August 2026)
  and uses Play Billing 9.1.0 (version 7 has been refused for new apps and
  updates since that day).
- **Dependencies.** Nothing that ships has a known vulnerability. Capacitor's
  CVE-2026-103922 (CVSS 9.3) is fixed in 8.5.1, the app is on 8.5.3, and the
  app renders no link that would reach it.
- **The e2e suite.** It is green on desktop, on a phone and on the Android
  bundle. The two skips are by design.

### a.5 The plan

Eight work packages. Each says what it fixes, how (with the files), what
pins it, and why nothing is lost. Effort is S (under a day), M (a few days)
or L (a week or more) for one engineer working in this codebase's style:
tests with every change, and the four checks before pushing.

#### WP-A — Nothing a gardener wrote is left behind *(F-2 to F-7, F-11, F-12, F-35 to F-38 · M–L)*

1. **Today keeps the day its words began** (F-3).
   - In `TodayView.tsx`, pin the check-in and reflection form to a `formDay`. It
     moves to the new day only when that day's drafts are empty.
   - While `formDay` is earlier than today, show one quiet line, "Begun on
     Monday", with **Keep for Monday** (an earlier-day save, so
     `addEarlierMood` takes feelings and energy and `addEarlierReflection` takes
     the prompt) and **Start today fresh**.
   - Goals, Sunlight and a gentle day still follow today.
   - Pin it with a test that crosses midnight mid-sentence and one that reloads
     the next morning.
2. **Journal edits become drafts** (F-4).
   - Add `lib/journalEditDraft.ts`, in the style of `goalEditDraft.ts`: one
     draft per kind, keyed by the day the edit began.
   - Save merges the edited fields onto the *current* entry, so another tab's
     newer change to that entry survives.
   - Each form shows `<DraftKept>`. Forms that are still not drafts mark
     themselves `data-lock-loses`.
3. **Settings follows the garden** (F-5). Seed name, garden name and motion
   with the existing `useSyncedDraft`, as Today does. Untouched fields then
   follow a restore or another tab, and half-typed ones are kept.
4. **Start fresh only when this garden's copy is safe** (F-2).
   - In `setAside.ts`, never prune the record just written, order copies by a
     monotonic `seq` read in the same transaction, and return the id written.
   - In `gardenRepository.ts`, `prepareFreshStart` refuses unless *that* id is
     stored.
   - `quarantinedCopy` offers the current garden's copy first.
5. **An earlier copy you downloaded opens again** (F-6).
   - `payloadOf` and `readLockedCopy` learn the `the-butterfly-garden-snapshot`
     envelope they already write. `sealedKind` routes a locked one to the
     locked-copy prompt.
   - No written shape changes.
6. **The lock screen offers a sealed copy before it deletes** (F-7). Add a first
   choice, "Keep a sealed copy first", which calls the existing `storedCopy()`
   lazily. Delete stays enabled, so a shared phone can still clear it.
7. **The sighting form and the image editor survive a native close** (F-11).
   Add a `close` listener in the pattern `LockDialog` and `useModal` already
   use, give each a remount key, and make `LockWatch` count only *open* dialogs
   as holding the auto-lock.
8. **Support lines for Australia and New Zealand** (F-35).
   - Add Lifeline Australia (13 11 14; text 0477 13 11 14) and New Zealand's
     1737 (call or text) to `data/support.ts`, each with its emergency number,
     dated by `SUPPORT_CHECKED_ON`.
   - Make the freshness test read the release *being* shipped (P-12), not the
     last one dated.
9. **Comfort-plan numbers that dial** (F-36).
   - In `comfortPlanEdits.ts`, invert the bracketed-zero rule to a short
     keep-the-zero list (Italy and five others).
   - Accept two- and three-digit emergency numbers.
   - Fold full-width and Arabic-Indic digits to ASCII before checking.
   - Pin it with a table of real numbers per country.
10. **Words that tell the truth** (F-37, F-38).
    - Care says a Pass piece is "resting until the Garden Pass is open", with a
      quieter "Take it off". The reason comes from one helper in
      `lib/wardrobe.ts` shared with saved looks.
    - The Journal's delete prompt says that earlier copies keep the entry for up
      to seven days.
    - Onboarding stops saying that care earns Sunlight.
11. **Lows of the same family** (F-12, F-38): escape `\u0000`-prefixed keys in
    `sealValue`; make `flushDrafts` wait for a write under way; write repaired
    ids back once; re-id duplicate ids on restore; cut text by code point
    everywhere (one `clipText` helper); prune daily copies by `takenAt` and
    never the copy just made; fix the garden-line index so every line can show.

**Zero loss.** No stored shape changes, and every addition is optional or
lives in drafts. Each item turns a loss into a choice offered to the
gardener.

#### WP-B — One baseline, one queue *(F-1 · M)*

The defects in F-1 share one cause: the repository's baseline (`persisted`,
`persistedRevision`) is replaced outside the save queue, and `save()` reads it
when its job starts. The fix makes the baseline's lineage explicit.

1. In `gardenRepository.ts`, keep a **baseline epoch**. It moves whenever the
   baseline is replaced from outside: a `load()` or `adopt()` that lands a
   revision other than the one held, `replace()`, `forget()`,
   `prepareFreshStart()` and `clear()`. A tab's own successful saves do not move
   it, so its quick successive saves still chain.
2. `save(state, epoch)` throws `GardenConflictError` at job start when its epoch
   is stale. The existing "not kept" path then reloads and says so. A
   superseded save during a restore stays silent.
3. In `usePersistence.ts`, tag each garden state with its epoch through a
   `WeakMap<AppState, number>`, copied from current to next by the `setState`
   the facade uses, so the tag follows the state's *lineage*, not the moment of
   the call.
4. `adopt()` runs through `enqueue()`. The `garden-saved` message carries the
   writer's `revision`, and an adoption that is not exactly one revision ahead
   falls back to a full `load()`. Older tabs' messages without a revision use
   the +1 rule.
5. `importGarden` and `putBackCopy` adopt the replacement *before* awaiting
   `snapshots()`.
6. Pin each ordering in `repository/twoTabs.test.ts` and
   `useGardenState.conflict.test.tsx`: adopt in flight, three queued saves with
   a reload, and a save queued behind `replace()`.

**Zero loss.** Only stale writes are refused, and those already reach a
"not kept" path whose reload shows the true garden. This also closes **D-32**
the way its recommendation reads (refuse the queued write, say nothing more), and
narrows **D-33**.

#### WP-C — Privacy and security hardening *(F-13 to F-20 · M)*

1. **Passcode strength, as advice, never a rule** (F-14).
   - Show a reading under the passcode in `LockSetup`, `LockChange` and the
     lock screen's new-passcode step. Reuse `passphrase.ts`'s checks, with
     lock-specific words. For an all-digit or under-8 passcode: "A short code
     like this can be guessed in minutes by anyone holding a copy of your
     garden. A few unrelated words are far harder."
   - Offer **Suggest a passphrase**: four or five words from the existing
     2,048-word list, 44–55 bits.
   - Say in one line that a locked copy opens with this passcode.
   - No existing lock changes.
2. **A tighter CSP, measured free** (F-17).
   - A build-only `transformIndexHtml` drops `'unsafe-inline'` from `style-src`.
     Dev keeps it for Vite's injected styles.
   - Add `require-trusted-types-for 'script'; trusted-types default`, with a
     `default` policy in the web build only that allows exactly
     `${BASE}sw.js`. The Android build registers no service worker and needs
     none.
   - WP-H's e2e guard fails any test on a violation.
3. **No quadratic route parsing** (F-18). Cap the hash at 256 characters before
   parsing, and strip trailing slashes with a loop. Validate a route read from
   `history.state` as one read from the address is.
4. **The moved-address carry-over uses the move file** (F-19, with P-15). Carry
   a locked garden as a move file sealed under a fresh 128-bit move code, which
   the old address shows once, instead of the raw locked copy.
5. **The kept file under the lock** (F-16). At lock-on, and in the backup
   section, name the file that still holds the garden unlocked and offer to
   stop updating it. (Keeping it written as a sealed copy is in (c).)
6. **Lows** (F-20):
   - Accept a PRF answer only when it is exactly 32 bytes.
   - `passkeysOffered` falls back to `isUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable`
     unless the browser gives a definite answer.
   - Cap the stored iteration count, sharing the 10 M constant.
   - New recovery codes avoid "cutting" and "rope", and old codes still read.
   - The CSV guard folds full-width characters.
   - Skip `photoCheck`'s unsealed trial under the lock.
   - Add `autocomplete="new-password"` plus a hidden username per secret, so
     password managers stop offering one for another.
7. **The shared origin** (F-13). This needs the owner (**D-7**). When it moves:
   - Serve `Content-Security-Policy` as a header with `frame-ancestors 'none'`,
     plus `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`,
     `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, a `Permissions-Policy` that
     keeps `publickey-credentials-get/create` and `web-share`, and HSTS.
   - Set `rp.id` explicitly. Keep `pwaAddress.json` as the single address.
   - Until then, the privacy policy's same-address passage (approved under D-6)
     stays the honest statement.

**Zero loss.** The CSP probe proved the stricter policy breaks nothing on
17 pages in both themes. Every other item is advice, wording, or validation of
input that can only have come from a bug or a hostile file.

#### WP-D — Accessibility integrity *(F-21 to F-27, F-45, F-46 · M)*

1. **Navigation at every width** (F-21). Close the 1,081–1,180 px gap. Either
   show the header's tabs from 1,081 px (condensing the wallet pills if they do
   not fit; measure it) or keep the dock fixed, as it is below 1,081 px. Pin 1,024, 1,100,
   1,180 and 1,194 px in `e2e/visual.spec.ts` and the axe sweeps.
2. **A focus ring that shows** (F-22). Give `.primary-button` and
   `.secondary-button` a visible `:focus-visible` outline: 3 px, offset 2 px,
   in a colour at least 3:1 against both the button and the page, in both
   themes. Keep the transparent outline only under `forced-colors: active`,
   where the system draws its own. Make selected-state rules add to the focus
   ring rather than replace it.
3. **Night twins for five colours** (F-23). Add `.theme-night` values for the
   weekday picker, `.pattern-cost`, `.streak-count small`, `.capacity-note` and
   `.calendar-sunlight-note`, each checked at 4.5:1 or better against its real
   background.
4. **Plants you can tap** (F-24, F-25).
   - On phones, make each plant's hit area cover its visible sprite. Give the
     button the sprite's width and resolve overlaps by stacking order, keeping
     CLAUDE.md's centring rule.
   - Fix the 621–725 px band by letting the row's end reservation, not the slot,
     set the spacing there too (CLAUDE.md's second invariant).
   - Add a 360 px and a 700 px tap-coverage check to the e2e suite.
5. **Focus goes somewhere** (F-26). Add one small `useReturnFocus` helper (the
   pattern `FilterSheet` and `useModal` already use), applied to the Journal
   timeline, the Undo toast, Try on, the seed tray, Settings' delete, and the
   recovery and safe-mode confirmations. Each action names where focus lands,
   with the safe choice first.
6. **Lows** (F-27): a real tabs pattern on Care (`tabpanel`, roving tabindex,
   arrow keys); reduced-motion hearts that fade in place; ignore repeated
   Enter/Space keydowns in the butterfly card; a `document.title` per page;
   names that say *which* line a star toggle stars; `forced-colors` styles for
   progress dots and the bond bar; reduced-motion rules that cover transitions
   and pseudo-elements; the splash inside the theme.
7. **The stylesheets' traps** (F-45, F-46).
   - `card-rise` fills `backwards`, not `both`, so it still holds the start pose
     during the stagger delay but leaves no transform behind. Pin a fixed-toast
     position check in e2e.
   - The dock spans the screen with its offset counted in.
   - `color-scheme` follows the theme on screen.
   - Inputs are 16 px or larger where iOS would zoom.
   - Provide the italic faces (or allow synthesis for italics only).
   - No text under 10 px.
   - Left and right `env(safe-area-inset-*)` in landscape.
8. **W-1** (axe 4.14's label-in-name): the header pills and "Try on" first, then
   lift the hold.

**Zero loss.** All of it is presentation and focus. Nothing changes what a
control does.

#### WP-E — Android correctness before 4.0 *(F-8 to F-10, F-15, billing · M)*

1. **Truncate on overwrite** (F-9). In `GardenFilesPlugin.documentChosen`, open
   with `"wt"`, falling back to `"rwt"` and then `"w"` with an explicit
   `truncate(length)`. Then read the length back where the provider allows,
   and reject on a mismatch, so the page never says "Backup saved." over a
   broken file. Pin the mode choice in a JVM test beside `DailyEventTest`.
2. **Save in pieces** (F-10). Replace the single escaped bridge string with a
   document session: `openDocument` (the save screen first, with a tiny
   message), then `writeChunk` (512 KiB of base64 at a time), then
   `closeDocument`. On cancel or error, delete the partial document. The Java
   heap stays flat whatever the garden's size.
3. **Recents shows the garden, not the journal** (F-15).
   - Add `setPrivateRecents(on)` to `GardenFilesPlugin`. On Android 13 and
     later it calls `setRecentsScreenshotEnabled(false)` while the lock is on,
     which blanks only the Recents card and keeps the gardener's own
     screenshots.
   - On older phones, set `FLAG_SECURE` only while the move code or a
     recovery sheet is on screen.
   - Correct MoveDialog's "kept nowhere" line.
4. **The auto-lock waits for the save screen** (F-8). `native.ts` counts
   document saves in flight, and `LockWatch.waiting()` includes them, so a save
   screen holds the lock as a file chooser already does. It is bounded by
   D-5's twice-the-chosen-time limit once P-13 lands.
5. **Billing that never refunds a paying gardener by mistake** (two billing findings, both low
   after verification).
   - A non-empty licence key that does not parse as the app's RSA key makes the
     Pass "unconfigured" (no offers shown), not "inactive".
   - Write a small "purchase in flight" flag before `launchBillingFlow`. At the
     next launch the plugin queries purchases only when the flag is set, and
     acknowledges them, then clears it. A gardener who owns nothing still never
     wakes Google's billing code, as the privacy policy promises.
6. **The 4.0 items already planned**: P-7 (a resting Pass), P-8 (the
   provisional Pass at launch), P-9 (`minWebViewVersion` and the error page)
   and P-10 (Gradle syntax) stand as written.

**Zero loss.** Every change is native behaviour the page already assumes:
that a save is whole, that the lock means the journal is out of sight.

#### WP-F — Performance and size *(F-28 to F-34 · S–M)*

1. **Stop the invisible animation** (F-28). Remove `gradient-drift` from
   `.primary-button`, or run it only on `:hover` with full motion. The
   gradient it moves is not visible. The computed-style baselines change only
   in the animation properties: regenerate them with
   `UPDATE_STYLE_BASELINES=1` and read the diff. Re-measure the Shop: the
   target is under 0.2 s busy per 4 s at 4×.
2. **A lighter flight loop** (F-29). Move each butterfly's lane onto the
   compositor (a Web Animations path per lane, or `offset-path` keyed by the
   existing lane maths), so frames need no style recalculation. Or halve the
   update rate while nothing is interacted with. Keep `perf.spec.ts`'s
   zero-layout guard, and add a style-recalculation and main-thread budget to
   it.
3. **Reclaim the first load** (F-30). Move catalogue descriptions out of the
   first-load chunk into the lazy pages that show them (−2.2 kB gzip), fold
   the Pass provider per platform with a literal `VITE_PLATFORM` check (about
   −190 B on Android), and delete the ~146 lines of dead CSS. Android's
   245 B of room becomes about 2.6 kB, which D-31's accessible loading heading
   and 4.0's own needs can spend on purpose (P-18).
4. **A smaller install** (F-31). Drop `includeAssets` (the glob already finds
   those icons) and keep `icon-512` out of the precache. Strip the 93 kB of
   web-only icons from the Android bundle, as the screenshots are stripped
   today. Make the Android launcher icons lossless WebP in
   `generate-android-icons.py` (−35 %).
5. **Audio that recovers** (F-32).
   - `breathTone.play()` queues nothing on a context that is not running, and
     resumes on `visibilitychange` and `pageshow`.
   - `useAmbientSound` gets an always-on recovery effect (`statechange`,
     `visibilitychange`, `pageshow`) that resumes, or re-arms the first-gesture
     resume.
   - Teach `fakeAudio` the "interrupted" state.
6. **Photos from today's phones** (F-33). Raise the decode limit to 50 MP,
   which covers 48 MP sensors. Where `createImageBitmap` accepts resize
   options, decode straight to the 2,048 px target, so memory stays bounded.
   Keep the 10 MB byte limit and the check before decoding.
7. **Lows** (F-34):
   - Hash only the production dependency tree into the build id.
   - No-op reducers return the same garden.
   - Memoise a personal image's URL.
   - Make the Guide's deferred search real.
   - Skip Safari's throwaway PNG.
   - Keep a timed-out scene download.
   - Pause the aurora when the page is hidden or motion is calm.
   - Drop the blur that sits under an opaque background.
   - Preload the two first-paint faces and add metric-matched fallbacks
     (`size-adjust`, `ascent-override`) so first paint does not reflow.

**Zero loss.** Nothing a gardener can see changes, except photos that used to
be refused and sound that used to stay silent.

#### WP-G — Architecture that pays its rent *(M–L, after 4.0)*

1. **Split `SettingsView`** (1,661 lines) into one component per card, with no
   change in behaviour. (b)'s Settings contents (B-H-1) do not wait for it, but it makes them easier
   to keep.
2. **Turn the repository closure into an explicit state object**: one place for
   `persisted`, `persistedRevision`, the queue, the epoch (WP-B) and the
   withheld/deleted/lock flags. Split `validateGarden` by collection.
3. **Add `lazyPage()`**, one helper for the 18 bare `lazy()` imports, which
   retries a failed chunk once. `ViewProblem` gets **Try again**, which
   re-creates the page's lazy component. The error boundary also takes a
   reset counter, so "Back to the garden" works when the garden page itself
   failed.
4. **Name the platform switch**: one `IS_ANDROID` constant that the bundler
   folds (`import.meta.env.VITE_PLATFORM === 'android'`). The 23 direct reads
   use it, and CLAUDE.md's Android-check trigger names it.
5. **D-29, the CSS consolidation**, as its own run now that the computed-style
   suite exists. The reader of `App.css` mapped a zero-visual-change path into
   `@layer`.
6. **One test harness**: the App harness copied into 17 files and the 14 e2e
   garden openers become shared helpers with a revision-settled wait.

#### WP-H — Tests, CI and docs that keep the promises *(F-39 to F-44 · M)*

1. **Invariant 8 as a fixture.** A Playwright `test.extend` used by every spec:
   - fail on any request whose origin is not the app's own;
   - fail on any `securitypolicyviolation`;
   - fail on any `pageerror`, and on any `console.error` not on a short
     allowlist.

   Add a unit test that pins the CSP string in `index.html`.
2. **Accessibility that sees the night.**
   - Record axe's "incomplete" colour-contrast nodes.
   - Add a pixel-sampling contrast check (the method of a.2's sweep) for text
     over paintings and gradients.
   - Run the view and shop-tab sweeps at night and at Largest text, plus a
     `forced-colors` pass.
3. **Tests independent of the host.**
   - Set `LANG` and `LC_ALL` to `en_US.UTF-8` in `test.env` beside `TZ`, with a
     setup guard that explains a non-US Windows locale.
   - Step days with `addDaysToLocalDate` (W-5).
   - Fix the e2e clocks to a time of day (W-15).
4. **Coverage and gaps.**
   - Raise the floors to about two points under today's figures (D-30):
     statements 93, branches 90, functions 92, lines 94.
   - Add the missing tests: the month planner's planning, a real
     service-worker update, two tabs in a browser, the reducers' "replace,
     never mutate" invariant (deep-freeze the input), and `useLocalDate`.
   - Stub `scrollTo` in `setup.ts` to remove 93 lines of noise.
5. **Docs that cannot drift.**
   - Generate the open-source notices per build from the module graph, and
     test them against `package-lock.json` and `variables.gradle`.
   - Add a docs-truth test: ARCHITECTURE's e2e list, README's catalogue
     counts and the version numbers.
   - Correct the README's CI sentence and the `platform.ts` claim.
   - Add knip and the budgets to CLAUDE.md (D-10).
   - Add `docs/plans/INDEX.md`, which names the current document and the status
     of every D/P/W id.
6. **CI.**
   - Add Java to CodeQL.
   - Give the signing job no shared caches and `npm ci --ignore-scripts`.
   - Check the bundle's signing certificate against the expected SHA-256.
   - Group Capacitor's packages in Dependabot.
   - Add an `engines` field and an `.nvmrc`.

### a.6 Order, effort and risk

4.0 is the first Android release and is under way, so the order follows it.
Items that protect an Android gardener's data or privacy, or that a Play
reviewer or a first iPad gardener would meet, go into 4.0. Architecture waits
until after.

| When | What | Effort | Risk | Why then |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Now, with P-9 and P-10** (low risk, first) | WP-E 1 (truncate), WP-E 3 (Recents), WP-C 3 (route cap), WP-A 8 (Australia and New Zealand lines), WP-D 2 and 3 (focus ring, night colours), WP-F 1 (gradient drift), WP-F 4 (install size) | S each | Low: isolated, each with its own test | They protect data and privacy, fix what a reviewer or a gardener meets on day one, and two of them free main-thread time and bytes |
| **4.0, beside P-7, P-8, P-13 and P-23** | WP-B (one baseline), WP-E 2 and 4 (save in pieces, lock waits for the save screen), WP-E 5 (billing hardening, in the same code as P-23), WP-A 1–7 (words left behind), WP-D 1 (navigation band), WP-D 4, 5 and 7 (taps, focus, the stylesheets' traps), WP-F 3 (first load), WP-F 5 and 6 (audio, photos), WP-C 1 and 2 (passcode advice, CSP) | M each | Medium for WP-B, which touches the save path. It is pinned by the two-tab suite and reviewed alone. | Data integrity before the audience grows. WP-F 3 frees the bytes 4.0's own items need. |
| **4.0's release checks** | WP-H 1 and 2 (privacy fixture, night accessibility), with P-17 bringing the paused checks back | M | Low | Proves the release keeps invariant 8 and its contrast |
| **4.1** | WP-F 2 (flight loop), WP-C 4–6, WP-A 9–11, WP-D 6 and 8 (lows, label-in-name), WP-F 7 (lows), WP-H 3–6 | S–M | Low | Valuable, not urgent |
| **After 4.1** | WP-G (architecture), WP-C 7 when D-7 is settled | L | Medium for D-29, guarded by the computed-style suite | Changes of shape, each as its own run |

Every package keeps CLAUDE.md's four checks green, and anything under
`android/` also runs the full Android checks.

### a.7 What stays exactly as it is

- **The nine invariants in ARCHITECTURE.** WP-B *strengthens* invariant 2 and
  closes one of invariant 9's two recorded gaps.
- **The backup format (invariant 6).** No item changes what a backup holds.
  Earlier-copy downloads become readable *as they are*.
- **The owner's decisions** (answered on 3 November 2026):
  - No new photo format for browsers that refuse Blobs (D-9).
  - The browsers job not required (D-8).
  - Playwright held (D-11).
  - The entry and CSS caps until 5.6 (D-10).
- **Open questions, built around as their recommendations stand.** If the
  owner answers one otherwise, the items that cite it change. None is
  re-argued here.
  - PBKDF2 at 600,000 rounds, not Argon2id (D-23). The passcode advice is the
    lever this plan pulls instead.
  - No QR code or camera (D-21; QR scanning was deferred on 28 October).
  - One drafts record for every tab (D-34).
  - The counts kept on a gentle day (D-35).
- **The lock design.** Slots, data-key rotation, the lock record and its
  stamps are untouched. Every change is to the words around the passcode, or
  to validating what is read.
- **What the garden never does.** No account, no server, no analytics, and no
  request beyond its own address except Play Billing, which still starts only
  at the moments the approved policy names.

### a.9 New questions for the owner

Numbered on from the future-update plan's D-46, for the owner to answer in
the same way:

| Id | Question | Recommendation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-47 | Show a passcode strength reading and offer "Suggest a passphrase" (advice only, no new rule)? | Yes, in 4.0 (WP-C 1). |
| D-48 | In the Android app with the lock on, hide the garden from the Recents card on Android 13 and later, and use `FLAG_SECURE` only on the move-code and recovery-sheet screens on older phones? | Yes (WP-E 3). Offer an opt-in "Hide the garden in recent apps" for older phones. |
| D-49 | Add Lifeline Australia and New Zealand's 1737 to the support page, and accept two-digit emergency numbers in the comfort plan? | Yes. The lines before 4.0 is dated (WP-A 8, P-12); the comfort-plan numbers in 4.1 (WP-A 9). |
| D-50 | Raise the image decode limit from 24 to 50 megapixels, so a current iPhone's photos are accepted? | Yes (WP-F 6). |
| D-51 | Remove the primary buttons' invisible gradient drift? | Yes. It is invisible and costs most of a busy page's main thread. |
| D-52 | Carry a locked garden from the old address as a move file with its own 12 words, rather than as the raw locked copy (P-15)? | Yes, in 4.1 (WP-C 4), so the carry-over is ready when D-7 is settled. |

---

## (b) Strengthening Existing Features

Section (a) makes what exists correct. This section makes it kinder and easier
to use. Nothing here is a bug fix (those are in (a), and are referred to by
F-n and WP-n) or a new capability (those are in (c)).

### b.1 How these were chosen

Each of eight areas was studied from four sources:

- **How it behaves now**, from the code readers' notes on every feature.
- **How it feels**, from a walk through the production build at phone size,
  by day and by night, with page lengths and the position of each part
  measured.
- **The code**, for each proposal, so every row names real files.
- **Current evidence** from wellbeing research, HCI and accessibility
  guidance, and how respected apps handle the same thing. Each claim links its
  source, and the evidence is described as strong only where it is.

Every improvement keeps to the rules section (a) keeps:

- **Nothing is lost.** No capability is removed. Stored data only gains
  optional fields, and the backup format does not change. Each proposal
  that adds something an older build would drop (B-T-10, B-J-11, B-C-10 and
  B-N's sighting fields) is put to the owner, not assumed.
- **Gentle.** No counts that read as scores, no streaks, no countdowns, and no
  badges for what was not done. Several proposals remove things that already
  break this (Care's daily "needs care" dot, the Journal's giant zero on day
  one).
- **Private.** Nothing new leaves the device. Words the gardener wrote are never
  quoted anywhere they did not choose.
- **Lazy.** The Android first load has about 245 B to spare until WP-F 3 frees
  about 2.4 kB, so additions live in lazy chunks. The few that touch the first
  load say so.
- **The owner's decisions stand.** Only D-1 to D-12 are answered (on
  3 November 2026); the rest are open, each with a recommendation. A proposal
  built on an open D-n says so and follows its recommendation. A decided one
  is built around.

The ids run B-T (Today), B-J (Journal), B-C (calm: breathing, sound, the
comfort plan and support), B-G (garden, care and shop), B-N (nature), B-S
(keeping the garden safe), B-H (the shell: Settings, navigation, onboarding and
updates) and B-A (accessibility and comfort), each numbered in order of value.

### b.2 Today: the check-in, goals and the reflection

Today is where the daily check-in lives, and its words and focus handling are
careful. The check-in earns the same Sunlight whatever the answer, unsaved
words are kept as drafts, one gentle next step follows a save, and a gentle day
shows two-minute versions with no counts. The gaps are in where things sit on a
phone and in what can be taken back. In a sweep of the production build at
412 × 839 px, the check-in question starts 644 px down and two of the five
weathers are off-screen. Several goal changes are silent or one-way, and a
gentle day picks its three goals by list order, not by what matters.

| Id | Improvement | Why it helps | How | Effort | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-T-1 | **Check in first.** On a phone, Today opens on "What is your inner weather?", not on an evening teaser. | On a Pixel 7 the question sits at y = 644 px and its weathers at about 720–840 px, under the dock. The first screen holds the meter, the seed line, the "Your next Sunlight tends" select and a Moonlight card that says "Moonlight opens this evening." from early morning, though the PWA's own "Check in" shortcut (`#/today/check-in`) already skips straight to the question. In [NN/g's eyetracking study](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/scrolling-and-attention/), 57 % of viewing time stayed above the fold. | In `TodayView.tsx`, while the recap is waiting, Moonlight becomes one quiet `.moonlight-later` line after the reflection that names the hour it opens (`recapOpensAt`), and the full card returns to the top once the recap opens or is collected. `.next-tend` moves into the Goals card and `<MonthPlanner>` below the reflection; the new rule gets its night twin (F-23), and an e2e at 412 × 839 px pins the first weather above the dock. No stored change. | S | Now |
| B-T-2 | **The comfort plan after a heavy check-in.** When a plan is written, "Open my comfort plan" joins the offer after Stormy or Rainy weather or low energy; with no plan, nothing new appears. | Today shows the plan only once the day is already gentle (`TodayView.tsx:637`), so it is missing at the moment the check-in says the day is hard. The plan follows safety planning's coping steps, and brief safety-planning interventions were associated with fewer suicidal behaviours ([Nuij et al. 2021](https://doi.org/10.1192/bjp.2021.50), RR 0.57). The just-in-time framework argues for support offered in a state of vulnerability ([Nahum-Shani et al. 2015](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4732268/)). | `lib/nextStep.ts` gains a `'comfort-plan'` step and `canOpenComfort`, second among the heavy steps; `NextStepOffer.tsx` names it, and `TodayView.tsx` passes it only when `profile.comfortPlan` exists. The plan is linked, never quoted, and the way back lands focus on the check-in's Save. No stored change. | S | Now |
| B-T-3 | **All five weathers in view.** On a phone the five skies sit side by side, a little smaller, and wrap at Larger text instead of running off the card. | At 412 px the row is a fixed five-column track that scrolls sideways with no sign that it does: Bright is cut by the card's edge (x = 375 px) and Radiant is off-screen (x = 475 px). The hidden two are the lightest, and options on screen are chosen more often than ones a person must scroll to reach ([Couper et al. 2004](https://www.scilit.net/publications/70380b88b54a4b9a4af3775bf60f4d63)), which matters for a check-in headed "No right answer". It is a design gap, not a failure: [WCAG's Reflow](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/reflow.html) allows scrolling in one direction. | In `App.css`'s ≤1,080 px and ≤620 px blocks, replace the fixed tracks and `overflow-x: auto` with `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 4rem), 1fr))` and size `.weather` like the 32 px small variant. Below 620 px the softer words stay in each button's name but are hidden visually, with the visible name first (W-1). An e2e at 360 and 412 px, at default and Largest text, checks every weather lies inside its card. No stored change. | S | Now |
| B-T-4 | **Every goal change can be taken back, and says what happened.** A done row's menu gains "Not done after all", and adding a goal, planning a day, Wake now and Restore each show the same Undo note as Done and Skip. | A mistaken completion (a 72 px swipe is enough) can be taken back only within the 6-second toast, which leans on the time limit [WCAG 2.2.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/timing-adjustable.html) asks designers to avoid; accidental activation is common with gestures ([Norman and Nielsen 2010](https://jnd.org/gestural-interfaces-a-step-backwards-in-usability/)), and people need a marked way out ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/user-control-and-freedom/)). Adding or planning says nothing, and Wake now and Restore move a goal silently and drop focus to the page body, where [WCAG 4.1.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/status-messages.html) asks for an announced status. | `GoalRow.tsx` gets an optional `onUncomplete`, wired in `TodayView.tsx` to the existing `onUncompleteGoal` (`uncompleteGoal` keeps the Sunlight, so nothing is won or lost), and `useSwipe`'s `disabled` pauses swipes while the row's menu is open. Add, plan, Wake now and Restore set an `UndoOffer` with existing actions only, and focus lands on the goal's own row, building on WP-D 5's `useReturnFocus` (F-26). No stored change. | M | Now |
| B-T-5 | **One "Add a goal" button once there are goals.** The add form folds behind "+ Add a goal" and opens, ideas first, with the cursor in the title. | The always-open form takes about 560 px on a phone (ideas at y = 1,402 px, "Add goal" at 1,923 px), between the day's goals and the reflection, though adding is occasional and caring and writing are daily. [NN/g's progressive disclosure](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/) shows only the most important options first. With B-T-1, Today at 412 px becomes roughly 600 px shorter for a gardener with a few goals. | A disclosure button in `TodayView.tsx` (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`). The form opens by itself when there are no goals or words are already typed, so a kept draft is never hidden, and "Done" or Escape returns focus to the button. No stored change; the new-goal drafts gain W-6's dating as planned. | S | Now |
| B-T-6 | **A gentle day keeps what matters.** The goal editor gains "Keep on gentle days", and those goals lead the short list. | A gentle day keeps the first three due goals in creation order (`TodayView.tsx:330`), and the list cannot be rearranged. So "Take prescribed medication", the idea `data/goalSuggestions.ts` says should never be made smaller, can sit behind "Show the other N goals" on the day a gardener is struggling. Resting most goals is sound, since one missed opportunity did not materially affect habit forming ([Lally et al. 2010](https://doi.org/10.1002/ejsp.674)), but medication or feeding a pet is not a habit to rest. | One `gentleChoice()` in `TodayView.tsx`, used by `setGentle` and the `gentleGoals` fallback: kept goals, then goals with a two-minute version, then the rest, three unless more are kept, still with no counts. `GoalEditor.tsx` and `goalEditDraft` carry the flag, `updateGoal` already spreads it, and `checkGoal` keeps unknown fields, so an older build carries it and picks the first three as before. Adds the optional `Goal.keepOnGentleDays`. | M | Next |
| B-T-7 | **A reflection that meets the day.** Saving says "Kept in your journal." with "Kept at 9:14", "Previous prompt" sits beside "Another prompt", and after heavy weather the day's prompt comes from the rest and self-compassion families. | The reflection's save is silent while the check-in's is announced ([WCAG 4.1.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/status-messages.html)), and a prompt passed by mistake comes back only after more than 60 others. After a Stormy check-in the day can open on "Name a tiny win that deserves to be remembered." Positive activities work best when they fit the person and the moment ([Lyubomirsky and Layous 2013](https://doi.org/10.1177/0963721412469809)), and self-compassion softens reactions to bad events without excusing them ([Leary et al. 2007](https://doi.org/10.1037/0022-3514.92.5.887)). | `lib/prompts.ts` gains `promptBefore()` and a family filter on `promptForDay`, still chosen by `getDailyPromptIndex`, so a reload never reshuffles. `TodayView.tsx` applies it with `isHeavyCheckIn` only while no prompt is chosen and nothing is typed, and writes `chosenPromptId` on the first keystroke, so the question never changes under written words. No stored change; prompt ids are unchanged. | S | Next |
| B-T-8 | **Schedules in plain days, in the gardener's own week.** Rows read "Mon, Wed, Fri", "Weekdays" or "Weekends" instead of "Selected days", and the day picker and calendar start on the local first day of the week. | `scheduleLabel` returns "Selected days" (`GoalRow.tsx:27`), and the picker and planner start on Sunday with English names whatever the locale, though the UK, Europe, Australia and New Zealand mostly start on Monday. [`Intl.Locale.getWeekInfo`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Intl/Locale/getWeekInfo) is Baseline only since July 2026, and older engines had a `weekInfo` getter, so it needs a Sunday fallback. | Move the existing `firstDayOfWeek()` (with its Sunday fallback) from `lib/weatherCalendar.ts` into a small shared `lib/week.ts` beside a new `shortWeekdays()`, imported only by lazy chunks, so Today and the Journal's calendar always start the week on the same day (and on the gardener's choice once B-J-10 adds one). It serves `scheduleLabel`, `WeekdayPicker` (still storing 0 = Sunday) and `MonthPlanner`'s headings and leading blanks. Tests pin `en-US` and `en-GB`, so the suite stays host-independent (F-41). No stored change. | S | Next |
| B-T-9 | **The month planner finds its way back.** "Back to today" appears once the calendar has moved, a past day names its weather and offers "Open this day in your journal", and a snoozed goal shows as "Resting". | The arrows are the only way back, and a past day ("Past days are for remembering, not planning") shows goals and Sunlight but no way to its check-in, though `#/journal/YYYY-MM-DD` already opens it (`lib/routes.ts:81`). The plan field caps titles at 80 characters (`MonthPlanner.tsx:339`) where every other goal field allows 120, and `isGoalPlannedOn` ignores `snoozedUntil`. | `MonthPlanner.tsx` gets the reset button, `maxLength={GOAL_TITLE_LENGTH}`, a "Resting" state while `snoozedUntil` is later than the day, and an optional `onOpenJournalDay` from `App.tsx` (about 30 B gzip of first load, taken from F-30's reclaim). Its tests also fill F-42's gap for the planner. No stored change. | S | Next |
| B-T-10 | **Words for anger and fear.** Angry, afraid and hurt join the feeling chips, with three comfortable words (for example connected, safe, playful) to keep the balance. | The 18 words hold nine difficult ones but not angry, afraid or hurt, so "frustrated" and "anxious" stand in for two of the commonest hard feelings. Telling difficult emotions apart is the kind of differentiation most consistently linked with wellbeing and regulation ([Kashdan, Barrett and McKnight 2015](https://www.affective-science.org/pubs/2015/kashdan-et-all-unpacking-emotion-differentiation-2015.pdf)). | Six rows in `data/feelings.ts` with new ids, in alphabetical order, the limit of five unchanged. Older builds drop unknown feeling ids on load (`cleanFeelings`), so ship it behind D-15's conditional state version, in the same release as the ♥ jar. New values in an existing field; the backup format is unchanged. | M | Later |
| B-T-11 | **"Ideas you'd rather not see".** A quiet link under the goal ideas lets a gardener tick ideas never to be offered on Today again. | Ideas such as "Take prescribed medication" or "Look at a photo of someone you love" can sting, after a loss for example, and they return every few presses of "Other ideas". Person-activity fit ([Lyubomirsky and Layous 2013](https://doi.org/10.1177/0963721412469809)) argues for letting people steer away from what does not suit them. | `openSuggestions` and `balancedSuggestions` in `lib/goalSuggestions.ts` take a hidden list, and `GoalIdeas.tsx` shows it in a small disclosure. The `setHiddenGoalIdeas` action costs about 40 B gzip of first load, so it waits for F-30. Adds the optional `Profile.hiddenGoalIdeas`, which `checkProfile` carries through older builds. | S | Later |

**Considered, and not recommended here.**

- **Removing the waiting Moonlight card.** It is there from the first day so
  the evening rhythm can be learned; B-T-1 keeps it as one line.
- **Making the day gentle by itself after a Stormy check-in.** It would take a
  choice away; the next step already offers it.
- **Streaks, "N of M days" or rates on goal rows.** D-39 recommends keeping the
  streak retired, and D-35 keeping only the two counts it names.
- **Drag-and-drop goal order.** It needs a single-pointer alternative anyway
  ([WCAG 2.5.7](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/dragging-movements.html));
  B-T-6 covers the case that matters, and "Move up" and "Move down" can follow
  F-30.
- **A label behind a swiping row, and a switch to turn swipes off.** The
  pattern is sound ([Flutter's Dismissible](https://docs.flutter.dev/cookbook/gestures/dismissible)),
  but after B-T-4 a mistaken swipe is one tap from undone; revisit then.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-53.** Should choosing the "Take prescribed medication" idea tick "Keep
  on gentle days" in plain sight, for the gardener to untick (B-T-6)?
  Recommended: yes, through an optional field on that one suggestion.
- **D-54.** D-26 recommends keeping the rotation as it is. May a heavy check-in choose
  where the day's rotation starts, adding no prompt (B-T-7's third part)?
  Recommended: yes. The other two parts need no decision.
- **D-55.** Add feeling words knowing that older builds drop unknown ids,
  or only behind D-15's conditional state version, so older builds set the
  garden aside instead (B-T-10)? Recommended: the D-15 rule, in the same
  release as the ♥ jar.

### b.3 The Journal, letters and the year

The Journal is the calmest part of the app, and on the whole it feels that
way. Marigold's letters are worked out when read and never stored, the rhythm
card counts days cared for with no streak, search folds accents, and the year
ends in a postcard drawn on the device. Nothing is scored. The rough edges fall
where feelings run closest: On this day can bring back a Stormy anniversary
unasked, every year and every month, with its first line already on screen,
and a newcomer's first sight of the rhythm card is a large "0 of the last 7".
Sightings, which D-25 recommends for search and copies, are missing from
search, copies, letters and the year, and a line quoted back in a letter leads
nowhere. The evidence is the code, the lead's walk on a Pixel 7 and the
readers' notes, with published research linked in each row.

| Id | Improvement | Why it helps | How | Effort | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-J-1 | **On this day the gardener steers.** A hard day waits on a gentle day or after a heavy check-in, a day can be kept from coming back, and a harder day shows its date and weather but not its words until the gardener asks. | The card offers the year-ago day, else the month-ago day, whatever its weather, and puts its first line on screen at once (`lib/onThisDay.ts:127`, `OnThisDayCard.tsx:41`). The only control is "Put away for today", so a Stormy anniversary comes back unasked every year and every month. Facebook added filters after complaints about painful reminders ([Engadget](https://www.engadget.com/2015/10/13/facebook-adds-on-this-day-preferences/), [NBC](https://www.nbcnews.com/tech/social-media/you-can-now-block-your-ex-facebooks-day-feature-n444336)), Google Photos has [Hide dates](https://support.google.com/photos/answer/9454489) and Apple Photos [Feature This Day Less](https://www.macrumors.com/2021/06/09/ios-15-memories-block-people/); looking back can help ([Isaacs et al. 2013](https://doi.org/10.1145/2470654.2466137)), but in a low mood even good memories turn "negatively tainted" ([Konrad 2016](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4n21504w)). | `onThisDay()` takes `{ mode, hidden, quiet }`. It skips hidden dates and, in gentle mode or when quiet (a gentle day, or `isHeavyCheckIn` today, `lib/nextStep.ts:16`), a Stormy or Rainy day or one of low energy, falling back to the month-ago twin or to nothing, and never says why; "harder" is read only from what the gardener chose, never their words. Adds optional `Profile.onThisDay` (`'gentle'` or `'off'`) and `onThisDayHidden` (at most 366 dates), set from a "More choices" disclosure on the card and "Days that come back" in Settings' "Your rhythm", through lazy reducers in the `changeComfortPlan` pattern; older builds carry both through `checkProfile`'s spread, and validating them there costs 60–90 B gzip of first load against Android's 245 B (a.2), or 0 B if the lazy reader guards them. | M | Now |
