| B-J-2 | **A rhythm card that welcomes a newcomer.** Before the first act of care it shows only words ("Your rhythm begins with your first act of care"), and after it the large figure is days of care so far, which can only grow, with "N of the last 7 · N this month" beneath and any zero part left out. | On day one the large figure is "0 of the last 7" beside "A missed day never sets this back" (`JournalView.tsx:384–407`), and that rolling figure does fall after quiet days, so the line is not true as read. 36 % of students felt their tracked data reflected badly on them, 56.5 % of those with a diagnosed mental illness ([Kelley et al. 2017](https://microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2017/02/Self-Tracking4MentalWellness-CHI2017.pdf)); people coming back after a lapse report guilt ([Epstein et al. 2016](https://homes.cs.washington.edu/~jfogarty/publications/ubicomp2016.pdf)); and counting an activity can take the pleasure out of it ([Etkin 2016](https://marketing.wharton.upenn.edu/wp-content/uploads/2016/10/Etkin-Jordan-11-12-15-Hidden-Cost.pdf)). An empty state should orient and show a way to start, not show an empty measure ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/empty-state-interface-design/)). | `careRhythm()` in `lib/rhythm.ts` also returns `total`, the size of the set it already builds. `JournalView.tsx` gets three branches (no care yet, counts, counts hidden), "A missed day never sets this back" becomes "Days are only ever added here", and the figure's meaning moves into visible text instead of an `aria-label` on a role-less `div`. Take F-23's night twin for `.streak-count small` (WP-D 3) in the same change; no stored change, 0 B of first load. | S | Now |
| B-J-3 | **Sightings in search, the timeline and readable copies (D-25).** A "Field book" chip finds a sighting's note and its common or scientific name, a day shows a quiet "Noticed in the wild" block, and copies gain the same block plus "Field book as CSV". | D-25, not yet answered, recommends "search and exports: yes", but neither is built: `JournalKind` has three kinds (`lib/journalSearch.ts:7`) and `journalDays()` reads only check-ins, reflections and recaps (`lib/journalExport.ts:62`). A garden holding only sightings is told "There is nothing in your journal yet". A sighting's note is the one thing a gardener writes that they can neither find nor take with them. | A `'sighting'` kind and `sightingsByDate` in `lib/journalSearch.ts`, names through `subjectName` (`lib/sightings.ts:104`), a `SightingsBlock` in `components/journal/`, and `ExportDay.sightings` with `sightingsCsv()` in `lib/journalExport.ts`, keeping the journal CSV's BOM, CRLF and formula guard ([RFC 4180](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4180), [OWASP](https://owasp.org/www-community/attacks/CSV_Injection)). Photos stay out of the timeline and the readable copies. No stored change; Journal and export chunks only, 0 B of first load. | L | Next |
| B-J-4 | **Letters and the year that tell the whole garden and lead back to the day.** Each gains one line of what was noticed ("You noticed a Red Admiral and milkweed in the wild."), and every quoted line gets "Read that day". | D-25 recommends one descriptive sightings line in each, but `countsIn`, `arrivalsIn` (`lib/summaries.ts`) and `gardenYear()` never read sightings, and `yearsToLookBack` cannot open a year of field-book entries alone. A line under "You wrote, on Tuesday:" or "Lines you starred" is a dead end, though `#/journal/YYYY-MM-DD` already opens a day (`lib/routes.ts:176`). Back must return focus to the link ([WCAG 2.4.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/focus-order.html)). | `sightingsIn(state, range)` in `lib/summaries.ts` names distinct subjects and never counts, so it reads the same with day counts hidden; `lib/gardenYear.ts` gains `noticed` and lets sighting dates open a year. `Letters.tsx` and `YearView.tsx` take an `onOpenDay` prop wired in `App.tsx`, as `YearLink` already is. No stored change: letters stay worked out when read, so an "Earlier letters" choice past the four weeks of `LETTER_WEEKS` can follow later; lazy chunks only, 0 B of first load. | M | Next |
| B-J-5 | **Delete with Undo in the timeline.** Delete happens at once with the shared toast, "Tuesday's check-in set aside. Undo", which puts the entry back exactly, star and "written later" mark included; editing a reflection also shows the prompt it answers. | Every timeline delete is "Delete this check-in for good?" with no way back (`JournalView.tsx:573`, `671`, `848`), while goals, the comfort plan and saved looks already use `UndoToast` ("Undo" in place of "Are you sure?"). When an action can be undone a confirmation is redundant, and confirms asked often get clicked through ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/confirmation-dialog/)); the toast waits while pointer or focus is on it ([WCAG 2.2.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/timing-adjustable.html)). It replaces WP-A 10's corrected delete wording, and focus follows WP-D 5 (F-26). | After WP-A 2's edit drafts, extract a memoised `TimelineDay` that owns its edit and pending-delete state, so a keystroke redraws one article, and add a lazy `lib/journalEdits.ts` with `restoreEntry()` behind a thin `changeJournal` action shaped like `changeComfortPlan` (`hooks/useGardenState.ts:559`). Sunlight and Moonlight are untouched either way, and daily copies still keep a deleted entry for seven days. No stored change; about 25–40 B gzip of first load, so measure it. | M | Next |
| B-J-6 | **Search that shows where it found the words.** Matched words are marked in the entries shown, "quoted words" match as a phrase, a "Starred" chip gathers every kept line, and an empty result has its own "Clear search". | Search only narrows which days show (`filterJournalDates`, `lib/journalSearch.ts:122`), so in a long reflection or a three-answer recap the gardener rereads to find why a day matched. Highlighting's effect on speed is mixed ([Zhang 2018](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC6305051/)), but in long personal text finding the sentence is the point. The mark needs an underline as well as a tint ([WCAG 1.4.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/use-of-color.html)), and the count should wait about half a second after typing stops before it is announced ([WCAG 4.1.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/status-messages.html)). | `normaliseWithMap()` in `lib/searchText.ts` folds code point by code point and maps folded positions back (ß becomes ss), so a range never splits a surrogate pair or a combining mark; `matchRanges()` and quoted phrases go in `lib/journalSearch.ts`, a small `Highlighted` component in `components/journal/`, and `JournalFilter` gains `starred`. Use `<mark>`, not the [CSS Custom Highlight API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/CSS_Custom_Highlight_API), which has been Baseline only since June 2025 and carries no semantics. No stored change (the filter is component state), 0 B of first load. | M | Next |
| B-J-7 | **Readable copies the gardener can shape.** A "Which days" choice (whole journal, this year, a month, or the days a search shows), stars marked "★ Starred", and the gardener's words laid out in their own script's direction. | Every copy is the whole journal (`lib/journalExport.ts:62`), when what a gardener wants to print or bring to a counsellor is often "this year" or "the days I just searched for", and stars appear in no copy. The printable page is `lang="en"` (`lib/journalExport.ts:328`) and nothing marks direction, so an Arabic or Hebrew note that starts or ends with Latin text or numbers is laid out in the wrong order ([W3C](https://www.w3.org/International/articles/inline-bidi-markup/index.en.html), [HTML `dir`](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/dom.html#the-dir-attribute)). D-28 skips translating the app, but a gardener's own words may be in any script. | `journalDays(state, include?)` filters by date, `ExportDay` gains star flags and `datedFileName` carries the range; `JournalExport.tsx` takes `searchDates` from `JournalView`. `dir="auto"` goes on every block of the gardener's words, in copies and on screen (timeline, letters, the year, On this day). No stored change; the export module already loads only when a copy is asked for. | M | Next |
| B-J-8 | **"Days when I…": a filter the gardener leads.** Beside "Weather that day", chips for breathed, finished a goal, cared for a companion, noticed nature and closed the evening narrow the timeline to those days, each with its own weather, and the app never says what they mean. | Behavioural activation rests on noticing for oneself how doing and feeling connect ([Kanter et al. 2012](https://www.cambridge.org/core/product/28347FA380E03B3066B4C8AFAC867B34/core-reader)), and people find their own mood data hard to interpret ([Schueller et al. 2021](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8387890/)), so the app shows days, not conclusions. Computed lines that pair an act with the weather reported a pattern that was not there in 38–60 % of simulated 30-day windows, and tracked data can turn into self-blame or rumination ([Kelley et al. 2017](https://microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2017/02/Self-Tracking4MentalWellness-CHI2017.pdf), [Faurholt-Jepsen et al. 2015](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26220802/), [Eikey et al. 2021](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00779-021-01573-w)). | `JournalFilter` gains `acts`, and a pure `actDates(state)` in `lib/journalSearch.ts` reads what the garden already holds: Sunlight sources such as `breath:<date>`, completions, companions' `careDates`, sightings, recaps and Moonlight. The results line says only what was found ("Showing 9 days when breathing counted as care", since a breath on a full-Sunlight day leaves no record) and drops its number with counts hidden or on a gentle day. No stored change, 0 B of first load. | M | Next |
| B-J-9 | **On this day for every year kept, and for field-book days.** When more than one earlier year has a day today, "A year ago · 2 years ago · 3 years ago" opens on the most recent, and a sighting day says "A year ago today you noticed a Monarch." | `onThisDay()` returns one day at most (`lib/onThisDay.ts:133`), so a garden kept three years never offers two or three years ago, and `hasEntry` (`:113`) never offers a day whose only record is a sighting. The year page comes once a year; On this day is the everyday way a long-kept journal gives something back ([Isaacs et al. 2013](https://doi.org/10.1145/2470654.2466137)). | An `onThisDays()` returns every exact calendar twin by year, keeping the 29 February rule, and the month-ago twin only when no year has one; `OnThisDayCard.tsx` renders `aria-pressed` choices as Letters' weeks do, and B-J-1's rules apply to each year. Sighting days follow B-J-3. No stored change, 0 B of first load. | S | Later |
| B-J-10 | **A weather calendar with one Tab stop, a way back, and a chosen week start.** The month is one Tab stop with arrow keys, Home, End, Page Up and Page Down, a "This month" button appears once the gardener has moved away, and "Days and numbers" in Settings gains "Week starts on". | Every written day is its own Tab stop, up to 31 a month, where the [APG grid](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/grid/) and [date picker](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/dialog-modal/examples/datepicker-dialog/) use one Tab stop and arrow keys, and after midnight crosses into a new month the calendar stays on the old one. The week's first day comes from `Intl.Locale` week info (`lib/weatherCalendar.ts:69–80`), which [Firefox has only from 153](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Intl/Locale/getWeekInfo), so on Firefox ESR 140 every gardener gets Sunday-first weeks, and a Saturday-first locale is drawn from Sunday. | A roving `tabindex` over the month table as `role="grid"` in `WeatherCalendar.tsx`, and `firstDayOfWeek(locale, chosen?)` in B-T-8's shared `lib/week.ts`, so Today's picker and planner follow the same choice, with Saturday starts taught to `monthWeeks` and the planner. Adds optional `Profile.weekStartsOn` (0, 1 or 6), which older builds carry whole; validating it in `checkProfile` costs 40–60 B gzip of first load, or 0 B if the lazy calendar ignores a bad value. | M | Later |
| B-J-11 | **Star a "What went well" line.** "Star this line" sits beside a recap's "What went well" only, so letters and the year can quote it under the same rules as other stars, and never when the gardener chose "never quote". | Letters and the year quote only starred check-in notes and reflections (`StarredKind`, `lib/letters.ts:22`), yet "What went well today?" is the garden's own good-things moment. Writing three good things a day raised happiness and lowered depressive symptoms for six months in a placebo-controlled study ([Seligman et al. 2005](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16045394/)); later replications are mixed, so the claim is modest. "Set down" is never starrable: quoting back what a gardener chose to put down would undo the act. | Adds optional `RecapEntry.starred` (exactly `true`): `StarredKind` gains `'recap'`, `migrateRecaps` (`repository/validateGarden.ts:448–460`) keeps it only when exactly `true`, and `starredLinesIn` in `lib/summaries.ts` reads `wentWell`. Because `migrateRecaps` rebuilds each recap field by field, builds 3.3 to 3.9 drop the star on their next save. About 30–50 B gzip of first load. | M | Later |

**Considered, and not recommended here.**

- **Computed lines that pair an act with the weather** ("on days you breathed, you felt brighter"): at a garden's scale they mostly report patterns that are not there, invite self-blame, and break the Journal's "without scores or analysis" promise. B-J-8 lets the gardener look instead.
- **Choosing what On this day brings back by reading the gardener's words, or hiding Stormy and Rainy days from the record**: B-J-1 reads only the weather they chose, and the timeline, calendar, search, year and copies stay whole as written.
- **A strip of seven suns, or any "current run" line**: each draws the gaps and works as a streak by another name. D-39 recommends keeping the streak retired; "longest run so far" stays because it can never fall.
- **A separate "Words waiting" card for earlier days' drafts**: F-3 (WP-A 1) already brings Today's words back across midnight, and a second surface would offer them twice.
- **Postcard shapes and a night postcard, for now**: a phone-sized card is pleasant but not urgent, and the night scene waits on D-20. The title cut that can split a flag (fix with [`Intl.Segmenter`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Intl/Segmenter)) and the brightness Safari loses (it ignores the [canvas `filter`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/CanvasRenderingContext2D/filter)) are fixes, best taken with WP-A 11.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-56.** On this day has no D-n yet. Should a Stormy or Rainy day wait by
  default on a gentle day or after a heavy check-in, and may the profile hold a
  short list of dates the gardener asked not to bring back (B-J-1)?
  Recommended: yes to both. Absent fields keep today's behaviour exactly.
- **D-57.** In the spirit of D-39, may the rhythm card's large figure move
  from "of the last 7" to days of care so far (B-J-2)? Recommended: yes, since
  it can only grow.
- **D-58.** If D-25 is answered as recommended (sightings in search and copies), should
  a day with only sightings also join the unfiltered timeline and the weather
  calendar, and may On this day offer one, which D-25 did not name (B-J-3,
  B-J-9)? Recommended: yes to both.
- **D-59.** For the wider question of insights, which section (c) takes up:
  is the gardener-led filter (B-J-8) the form to choose, so that the app never
  pairs an act with the weather? Recommended: yes.
- **D-60.** For recap stars (B-J-11), accept that builds 3.3 to 3.9 drop a
  recap's star on their next save (the trade D-14(d) asks about for outfits),
  or keep recap stars in the profile, which older builds carry whole?
  Recommended: answer it with D-14(d), and accept the drop: only the star is
  lost, and the words stay.

### b.4 Breathing, sound, the comfort plan and support

Breathing with a butterfly is calm and carefully built. The wings move on the
compositor, a count and a polite screen-reader cue keep time, an optional tone
marks each turn, and a session counts as care once a day. The soundscapes are
made on the device, and the comfort plan is a warm, private page with call
links, drafts and Undo. Two things most need work. Help is hard to reach when
it is needed: in the UX walk the support page sat 11,927 px down a 12,676 px
Settings page, it is not in the More sheet, and a locked garden puts the
passcode between the gardener and every helpline. And both the breathing page
and the plan are shaped for setting up rather than for a hard moment: the
screen sleeps partway through a session, and a ten-line plan is wrapped in
about 45 buttons and five open forms.

| Id | Improvement | Why it helps | How | Effort | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-C-1 | **Help that is never out of reach.** The More sheet gains a quiet "For harder days" group (breathing, the comfort plan, support), and the lock screen, recovery screen, safe mode and welcome screen gain "If you need support now", which shows the helplines and nothing else. | Support is reached from the foot of Settings, the foot of the Guide, the comfort plan, and Today only after a Stormy check-in (`TodayView.tsx:586`). With the lock on, `LockGate.tsx` replaces the whole app with the lock screen, so a gardener who has forgotten the passcode, or is too upset to type it, meets no helpline. [WCAG 2.2's Consistent Help](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/consistent-help.html) asks for help in the same place each time and names people facing "cognitive fatigue or cognitive shut down"; of 69 depression and suicide-prevention apps, 94 % gave emergency contacts and 67 % direct helpline access ([Martinengo et al.](https://bmcmedicine.biomedcentral.com/articles/10.1186/s12916-019-1461-z)). | Move the urgent card and the lines from `SupportView.tsx` into a new `SupportLines.tsx` that reads only `data/support.ts`, and render it in a `<details>` on the four lazy screens. Add a `HarderDays` block after `.more-sheet-pages` in `Navigation.tsx` (and at the foot of each page where More is hidden), a `'more'` `BreatheWay` in `lib/routes.ts`, and a support shortcut in `src/pwaManifest.ts`. The More-sheet block costs an estimated 120–160 B gzip of first load, so it lands after WP-F 3 (F-30) and is measured with `scripts/check-budgets.mjs`. No stored change. | M | Now |
| B-C-2 | **The screen stays awake while you breathe.** During a session the screen stays on, as it does for a video, with one quiet line saying so; Stop, the end or leaving the page releases it. | Nothing in `src` uses the Screen Wake Lock API, so a phone's display timeout (commonly 30 s to 2 minutes) darkens a 3- or 5-minute session that needs no touch. The timers are throttled, the tone stops, and with the lock on a 1-minute auto-lock can take the app down and lose the session uncounted. The API is Baseline 2025 ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Screen_Wake_Lock_API)): Chrome and Android WebView 84, Safari 16.4 and Firefox 126 ([compat data 8.1.9](https://unpkg.com/@mdn/browser-compat-data@8.1.9/data.json)), and Home Screen apps on iOS since [18.4](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/safari-release-notes/safari-18_4-release-notes) fixed [WebKit bug 254545](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=254545). | `holdScreenAwake()` in `lib/breathView.ts` requests `navigator.wakeLock.request('screen')` in try/catch and again on `visibilitychange` while a session runs, since the browser drops the lock on hide ([Chrome](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/capabilities/web-apis/wake-lock)); `BreathingView.tsx` calls it from an effect keyed on the run. Unit tests use a fake `navigator.wakeLock`; a real-device check goes in P-20, with native `FLAG_KEEP_SCREEN_ON` as the fallback if the WebView lacks it (which brings in CLAUDE.md's Android checks). No stored change; about 200–300 B in the lazy chunk. | S | Now |
| B-C-3 | **The comfort plan opens ready to read.** A plan with words opens as a calm page of the gardener's own lists, with Call (and Text, B-C-5) beside each person and one "Change my plan" button. It opens ready to write when it is empty or a draft is waiting, so nothing typed is hidden. | Each of the five lists ends in an always-open Add form, and every line carries up to five controls (`ComfortPlanView.tsx:369–414`), though the component's own comment calls it "the gardener's page, not a worksheet". Safety planning keeps the plan short, in the person's own words and quick to read ([Stanley and Brown 2012](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cbpra.2011.01.001)). In the [BeyondNow pilot](https://www.suicideinfo.ca/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/20190680-Evaluating-the-Feasibility-and-Effectiveness-of-an-Australian-Safety.pdf) about 82 % of participants used their plan while having suicidal thoughts, so it has to work at the moment it is read. | In `ComfortPlanView.tsx`, a `writing` state seeded at mount (true when the plan is empty or a `comfort-edit` or `comfort-new:{section}` draft holds text), a reading list that shares a new `LineWords` component with today's line, and focus and the live region ("Ready to change your plan.", "Back to reading.") through the existing `pendingFocus` and `say()`. Reading styles go in `components/comfort/comfort.css`; tests in `ComfortPlanView.test`, `e2e/comfort.spec` and `android.spec`, with axe in both modes. No stored change: the mode lasts one visit. | M | Now |
| B-C-4 | **The support page puts the gardener's region first.** Lines for the region chosen in Settings come first, with a note saying why, and the urgent card leads with that region's numbers. No line is hidden. | Lines appear in one fixed order (the US, Canada, the UK and Ireland, then everywhere else), and the urgent card names only 911, 999 and 112 (`SupportView.tsx:59–65`), whatever `profile.region` says. Once WP-A 8 adds [Lifeline Australia](https://www.lifeline.org.au/) and [1737](https://1737.org.nz/) (F-35, D-49), a gardener in Auckland would scroll past three countries to reach 1737. The generic Find A Helpline link also ignores the site's country pages ([Germany's](https://findahelpline.com/countries/de), for one). | `data/support.ts` gains optional `regions` and country codes per line, plus each region's emergency numbers (000 and 111 among them), dated by `SUPPORT_CHECKED_ON`. A pure, lazy `lib/support.ts` orders them with a stable `orderedLines()` that never drops a line, from `profile.region` or else the device's language region (`Intl.Locale`), and names a country link such as "Helplines in Germany" with `Intl.DisplayNames`. Nothing is looked up or sent. No stored change. | S | Now |
| B-C-5 | **Text as well as Call, and room for someone whose job is to help.** "Text Sam" sits beside "Call Sam" and opens messages with nothing written; the People example adds "someone whose job is to help, like your doctor or a counsellor". | A person with a number gets only "Call {name}" (`ComfortPlanView.tsx:369–373`), and speaking can be hardest on the day the plan is for: [Kids Help Phone found 71 % of young people would rather text](https://edsurge.com/news/2023-03-29-for-teens-text-based-crisis-lines-increase-accessibility-amid-mental-health-emergency), and [the Trevor Project](https://www.thetrevorproject.org/research-briefs/digital-support-for-youth-in-crisis/) reports young LGBTQ+ people choose text for confidentiality and comfort. The People example names only friends, family and neighbours, so [Stanley-Brown](https://suicidesafetyplan.com/)'s step for professionals has no obvious home. | `smsHref(phone)` in `lib/comfortPlanEdits.ts` is `telHref` with `sms:`, so it inherits WP-A 9's dialling fixes (F-36) and the digits-only href. It adds no message body: [RFC 5724](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5724) uses `?body=` where [iOS expects `&body=`](https://weblog.west-wind.com/posts/2013/Oct/09/Prefilling-an-SMS-on-Mobile-Devices-with-the-sms-Uri-Scheme), and a prefill that fails, fails silently. No stored change; about 100 B in the lazy chunk. | S | Now |
| B-C-6 | **A copy of the plan to keep away from the screen.** "Keep a copy" at the foot of the plan offers "Print my plan" and "Save a copy", dated, beside one honest line: "A printed or saved copy is outside the garden lock, so keep it somewhere private." | The plan lives only in the app. With the lock on it is sealed until unlocked, and a flat or forgotten phone has no plan at all. [Stanley-Brown](https://suicidesafetyplan.com/) asks where the plan will be kept, and [plan builders](https://www.mentalyc.com/therapy-games-and-tools/safety-plan-builder) suggest copying or printing it. The app already makes a keepable sheet for the recovery code (`lock/RecoverySheet.tsx`). | A lazy `lib/comfortPlanCopy.ts` builds `comfortPlanText(plan, now)` from the lists alone. `ComfortPlanView` adds the buttons and a print-only portal in the `RecoverySheet` pattern, and the Android app saves through `saveTextFile`'s existing path, so WP-E 1 and 2 (F-9, F-10) apply. No stored change and no new permission. | M | Next |
| B-C-7 | **For eyes closed: a way in, a way out, and a buzz where the phone can.** Start gives a 3-second "Settle in…" before the first breath, a soft two-note chime marks the end when the tone is on, and the Android app and Chrome on Android offer an opt-in buzz at each turn. | Start begins the first breath at once (the first tone within 250 ms), and only silence marks the end, since "All done" can only be seen; in [Balban et al.'s protocol](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9873947/) participants set a 5-minute timer, so the practice had a clear end. Sound is often unwelcome in a shared room; Apple Watch's Breathe guides each breath with taps ([iMore](https://www.imore.com/how-to-use-breathe-apple-watch-app)), and in vibrotactile pacing for anxiety, personalising the pattern mattered ([Miri et al., TOCHI 2020](https://hci.stanford.edu/publications/paper.php?id=384)). `navigator.vibrate` works in Chrome for Android and Android WebView only: Safari never had it, and Firefox removed it in 129 ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Vibration_API)). | `planSession` in `lib/breathPacer.ts` gains a fixed 3,000 ms "settle" lead and an "end" turn, and `lib/breathTone.ts` an end chime (D5 falling to G4) that rings out before the context closes. The buzz fires from the tick `BreathingView` already runs at each step change, offered only where `canBuzz()` finds `navigator.vibrate`; the Android app adds the install-time `VIBRATE` permission ([Android haptics](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/haptics/haptics-apis)), with the Android checks and a P-20 device check. Adds an optional `buzz` to the device's breathing-choices draft only, outside the garden and its backup. | M | Next |
| B-C-8 | **Choose who breathes with you (D-27).** A small "Breathe with" choice offers Marigold and, once it has wings, the active companion, and the chosen name follows wherever breathing is offered. The cyclic sigh's description gains the breath out through the mouth. | D-27, not yet answered, recommends "Yes: a small choice on the breathing page", but `pacerFor` in `lib/breathing.ts` still picks the companion once it has wings, and nothing explains why a caterpillar is not offered. The sigh's text omits the breath out through the mouth that [Balban et al.](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9873947/) ask for "ideally". | An optional `pacer` (`'marigold'` or `'companion'`) in `BreathChoices` and the `breathing-choices` draft, left out meaning today's rule, and `pacerFor(state, choice?)`. `SupportView`, Today's next step and `MoonlightRecap` read the same draft, so every label agrees; `data/breathing.ts` gets the new sigh copy. Device draft only: an older build drops the key and falls back to today's rule. | S | Next |
| B-C-9 | **Say why there is no sound.** At volume 0 a line beside "A soft tone at each turn" says so and points to Settings, and on iPhone and iPad the sound card and the tone choice say the sound follows the ring/silent switch. | The tone plays at the sound volume, so at 0 it shows as on and the session is silent with no word why. On iPhone and iPad, Web Audio follows the ring/silent switch ([WebKit bug 237322](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=237322)), and neither Settings' "Choose what you hear" nor the tone choice says so. [`navigator.audioSession`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/AudioSession/type) (Safari 16.4 and later) can switch to "playback", which plays in silent mode but pauses other audio such as a podcast. F-32 (WP-F 5) covers recovery after an interruption; this covers the silences the app can explain. | `BreathingView` compares `soundVolumeOf(profile)` with 0, and `followsSilentSwitch()` in `lib/sound.ts` (true where `navigator.audioSession` exists) drives the note in Settings' sound card and `SoundSwitches`. If the owner agrees to the opt-in, set `audioSession.type = 'playback'` for a session and for the soundscape's engine and return it to "auto" when they stop, checked on a device under P-20. The notes store nothing; the opt-in adds a breathing-choices draft key and an optional profile field for the soundscape (about 60 B of first load). | S | Next |
| B-C-10 | **An optional list for noticing a day is getting harder.** Under the plan's lists sits an offer, never a request, "Add a list for the signs a day is getting harder"; chosen, it works like every other list, and removing its last line puts the offer back. | In its own words the plan already covers four [Stanley-Brown](https://suicidesafetyplan.com/) steps, and B-C-5 makes room for professionals. Missing is the first, recognising the signs that tell a person when to open the plan. Safety-planning-type interventions cut suicidal behaviour (RR 0.57, NNT 16, 6 studies, 3,536 participants; [Nuij et al. 2021](https://doi.org/10.1192/bjp.2021.50), [eprint](https://eprints.gla.ac.uk/238354)), though those studies involved clinicians; the [Stanley-Brown form](https://dsamh-training.utah.gov/_documents/evidencebasedprogramming/SAFETY_PLAN_form_8.21.12.ada.pdf) is copyrighted, so the app keeps its own words. | `ComfortSection` in `types.ts` gains `'signs'`, first in `COMFORT_SECTIONS`; `checkComfortPlan` in `validateGarden.ts` learns the key, and `ComfortPlanView` shows the list only when it has lines or was just opened. Adds an optional `signs` list inside `profile.comfortPlan`. Older builds keep only known lists (`validateGarden.ts:818`), so store at version 9 only when a plan holds it, as D-15 does for the ♥ jar. | M | Later |
| B-C-11 | **"Breathe now" beside any goal about breathing.** A goal whose words include breathe, breathing, breath or breaths offers "Breathe now", as the suggested goal does. | `isBreathingGoal` (`lib/breathing.ts:39`) matches only the exact title "Take three slow breaths", so "Breathing practice" or "Slow breaths before bed" gets nothing. In a meta-analysis of randomised trials, breathwork had small-to-moderate effects on stress (g −0.35), anxiety (−0.32) and depression (−0.40) ([Fincham et al. 2023](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9828383/)), and [Balban et al.](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9873947/) found the benefit grew with the days practised. | Also accept a whole-word match, `/\bbreath(e\|es\|ing\|s)?\b/i`, on the normalised title, with a table test that rejects "breathtaking walk". English only (D-28). No stored change; lazy chunk only. | S | Later |

**Considered, and not recommended here.**

- **The plan's people or words on the support page or the lock screen.** It
  breaks the promise that "the garden never shows it anywhere else"
  (`ComfortPlanView.tsx:435`), and a locked phone would show private names.
- **A "make the surroundings safer" step** (Stanley-Brown's sixth). It belongs
  in a conversation with a clinician, and the form is copyrighted.
- **A button that dials an emergency number by itself.** Accidental emergency
  calls are a real cost; a `tel:` link that opens the dialler is enough.
- **Minutes breathed, breathing streaks or a calm score.** D-39 recommends
  keeping streaks retired; the once-a-day Sunlight stays the only mark.
- **Lock-screen and headset controls for the soundscapes (Media Session), for
  now.** Chrome shows no media notification for Web Audio without an audio
  element, and Android WebView has none; revisit after a device test.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-61.** Should "If you need support now" take Today's place among the
  three shortcuts [Chrome on Android shows](https://web.dev/articles/app-shortcuts),
  with Today kept fourth for desktop, and may the lock screen show the static
  helplines, which hold no garden data (B-C-1)? Recommended: yes to both;
  "Check in" already opens Today.
- **D-62.** With no region chosen, should the device's language region
  order the support lines, though it can differ from where the gardener is,
  and should the urgent card gain a button for the region's emergency number
  (B-C-4, with D-49)? Recommended: yes to both, since no line is hidden and
  the button is a `tel:` link that only opens the dialler.
- **D-63.** Add the `VIBRATE` permission to the Android app (B-C-7)? It
  brings no prompt, but Play lists it on the app's page. Recommended: yes,
  with the buzz off unless chosen.
- **D-64.** Offer "Play even when my iPhone is on silent", knowing it
  pauses other sound while the garden plays (B-C-9)? Recommended: yes, as an
  opt-in that is off by default.
- **D-65.** Add the optional signs list (B-C-10), and if so store it at
  version 9 only when a plan holds it, following the rule D-15 proposes,
  rather than let an older build drop it? Recommended: yes, with that rule.

### b.5 The garden, care and companions, and the shop

The garden opens on a painted scene with its name and a line for the day,
night and season, where up to twelve butterflies fly their own patterns over
eight swaying plants. Care gives each companion a pettable portrait, a
once-a-day menu and a bond meter that says what the next level brings, and the
Shop says exactly why a purchase cannot go through, so a refusal never loses
anything. The gaps show with time and at night. A walk through the production
build at Pixel 7 size, the feature digest's simulated year and the code agree
on the two worth changing first: a coral "Needs care today" dot that sits on
every companion every day, and a night line that says butterflies rest while
they keep flying (D-17). After that come lists that never page (363 companions
after a year under Keep giving), purchases that cannot be taken back, and a
"Try on" that only ever shows a monarch.

| Id | Improvement | Why it helps | How | Effort | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-G-1 | **Care marks what was given.** The coral dot on each companion chip goes, and a chip cared for today wears the small sage "Cared for today" mark the portrait already has. | The dot (`CareView.tsx:228–230`) is there on a gardener's first visit, and every day on every grown butterfly, which has no stage left to reach. So a returning gardener opens Care to a row of red dots: the "you missed this" signal that went with the streak, which D-39 recommends keeping retired. In a two-week study of a care-for-a-pet wellness device with 20 students, interviews found that ["pet neglect can convert a wellness prompt into a source of guilt"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00412). The evidence on wording is mixed (messages felt as controlling lowered autonomous motivation in [Legate and Weinstein 2021](https://selfdeterminationtheory.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/2021_LegateWeinstein_CanWeCommunicate.pdf); [Altendorf et al. 2019](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6393822/) found no difference between "should" and "could"), so the app's own no-shame rule settles it. | In `CareView.tsx`, render a `.cared-chip-mark` when `hasCaredToday` is true and add ", cared for today" to the chip's name. The sage rule in `App.css` gets its night twin (F-23). The page's "Every stage of life needs you" (`CareView.tsx:172`) and the Guide's "Butterflies never stop needing you" (`GuideView.tsx:106`) become "One act of care a day moves a stage along: three days for each stage, never needed in a row, and a bond that lasts." and "Butterflies are here to stay. Care for them whenever you like." No stored change. | S | Now |
| B-G-2 | **Butterflies settle on the flowers by night.** Whenever the night scene shows, each butterfly rests with still wings on a grown bloom, one to a flower (about four on a phone), and the rest settle low in the grass. The fireflies and moths (D-44) are all that move. A tap still opens the same card. | By night the line says "Butterflies rest through the night, folded under leaves and in tall grass", and `data/nightLines.ts` promises no line has one flying, yet Marigold and every companion keep looping. Real butterflies are active by day and hide at night under leaves, between rocks or between blades of grass ([Peggy Notebaert Nature Museum](https://naturemuseum.org/2017/01/where-do-butterflies-go-at-night/)). The loop also keeps a throttled phone's main thread 39 % busy (F-29) at bedtime, after the Moonlight recap's "Fade the garden to night and close", when [Pixel phones](https://support.google.com/pixelphone/answer/7169926) offer Night Light and Grayscale to make the screen calmer. | `lib/flightMotion.ts` gains an optional `perch` that `staticPose` uses in place of `restingPose`. `hooks/useFlightMotion.ts` reads bloom tops from the `.garden-plant` boxes (centred at `left: 50%` of the slot, as CLAUDE.md requires), picks perches at least 0.8 of a sprite apart, and takes the static path that already skips `animate()`. `GardenView.tsx` passes `settled` at night, and `garden-3.css` lifts the flight layer above the plants at night so a perched butterfly takes its own taps. No stored change. About 80–120 B gzip of first load, so it lands after WP-F 3 (F-30). | M | Now |
| B-G-3 | **Prices that say what they need and where it comes from.** "Need 4 more" becomes "Need 4 more Nectar", a refusal adds one line on where that currency comes from, and Care's empty supply links straight to it. | The aria-disabled price button is drawn at 2.6:1 by day, so its one useful number is the hardest thing on the card to read, and pressing it does not say where Nectar comes from. The Guide's Nectar and Stardust section (`GuideView.tsx:137–140`) leaves out the steadiest Stardust, 1 each evening from Moonlight (`progression.ts:42`), and Care's "Restock in the Shop →" opens Supplies unfiltered. [Recognition rather than recall](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/recognition-and-recall/) puts the answer where the question arises. | `shop/ShopItemCard.tsx` names the currency. `shop/purchaseMessage.ts` adds one source sentence per currency, built from `NECTAR_PER_SUNLIGHT` and `MOONLIGHT_STARDUST_REWARD` so it cannot drift, with no countdown. `shop.css` gets a 4.5:1 aria-disabled ink with its night twin, beside F-23's price twins. Care shows "Out of Leaf Bundle. Get more in Supplies →", which opens the Shop already searched through an in-memory `shopQuery` from `App.tsx` (about 30 B of first load). No stored change. | S | Now |
| B-G-4 | **Meet any butterfly without chasing it, and pause the scene.** Each companion card gains "Meet {name}", which opens the same card as a tap in the scene, and a small "Pause the butterflies" toggle in the hero stills everything for the visit. | Meeting a companion means catching a moving 96 px button, which is hard with a tremor, a switch or a magnifier. Butterflies outside the day's twelve (`GardenView.tsx:209–229`) cannot be met in the scene until another day, and stopping the motion means a trip into a Settings page 12,676 px long on a phone. [WCAG's Understanding 2.2.2](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/pause-stop-hide.html) recommends one control that pauses all moving content at once. | In `GardenView.tsx`, `meet()` remembers its opener so closing hands focus back. A `paused` state feeds `reduced` and sets `data-paused` on the hero, and one `garden-3.css` rule pauses `.plant-sway`. The lasting choice stays in Settings. No stored change. About 60–90 B of first load, so it lands with B-G-2. | M | Next |
| B-G-5 | **Companions easy to find at ten or at three hundred.** The Garden shows the active companion and the six newest, then "Show all 42 companions". Renaming moves behind a quiet "Rename", and Care keeps growing companions first with "Find a companion" past twelve. | A simulated year under Keep giving brings 363 companions. The Garden draws each as a card with a full sprite and an always-open rename form (`GardenView.tsx:760–818`), about 180 rows, and Care's picker is one sideways row of chips, so finding "Juniper 2" means swiping past hundreds. [Progressive disclosure](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/) keeps the common case light and the rest one step away. | `GardenView.tsx` orders by `emergedAt ?? discoveredAt` with a `showAll` state, and one `editingName` id replaces the per-card forms, so it is about byte-neutral and lighter to render. `CareView.tsx` filters with `lib/searchText`'s accent folding, through one ordering helper shared with the Shop's companion filter. The picker's tabs pattern stays with WP-D 6 (F-27). Tests use a 60-companion fixture. No stored change. | M | Next |
| B-G-6 | **Undo after buying, while the purchase is untouched.** The purchase toast offers Undo for six seconds, as a recycled jar's does, and gives back exactly what was spent. | Every purchase is final (`ShopView.tsx:228–235`), though jar recycling, goals, saved looks and field-book deletes all offer Undo. A mis-tap on ×5 can spend 15 Nectar, a full day's Sunlight, and a Stardust piece (4–10) is up to ten evenings of Moonlight. Undo keeps buying to one tap with an [emergency exit](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/user-control-and-freedom/), and [WCAG 3.3.4](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/error-prevention-legal-financial-data.html) accepts "reversible" as one way to protect a transaction. | `lib/purchase.ts` returns a `receipt` with the "bought" outcome. One `refundPurchase(state, receipt)` reducer, modelled on `restoreRecycledJar`, refunds only a piece no one wears and no look holds, supplies still all in the satchel, a pattern no one flies or a jar on no plant. Otherwise it says why: "Leaf Scarf is being worn, so it stays yours. Nothing was changed." The receipt lives only as long as the toast: no stored change. | M | Next |
| B-G-7 | **Try it on your own companion, and wear a look with Undo.** "Try on" shows the chosen companion in its current outfit with the new piece. Each saved look on Care shows a small sprite of how it would land and names any pieces left off, and "Wear" offers Undo. | The preview always shows a monarch at the piece's first stage wearing that piece alone (`shop/ItemPreview.tsx`), so a gardener cannot see the Leaf Scarf on Sol beside the crown Sol already wears. "Wear" empties every slot the look leaves empty (`lib/wardrobe.ts:241–288`), and an unsaved combination is then gone, though deleting a look has an Undo. Animal Crossing's [Able Sisters fitting room](https://nookipedia.com/wiki/Able_Sisters) lets players try clothes on their own character before buying. | `ItemPreview.tsx` takes `creatures` and `initialCreatureId` (the Shop's Companion filter, else the active one) and builds the outfit from `visibleOutfitFor` plus the piece. Focus on close is WP-D 5's (F-26). `care/OutfitLooks.tsx` draws the look through `fitsCreature` and `canWearItem`, and `lib/wardrobe.ts` adds `restoreOutfit`, a no-op if the companion has changed since. Pass pieces can be previewed but not worn while the Pass rests. No stored change. | M | Next |
| B-G-8 | **A seed tray that says which butterfly each seed could bring.** Each host plant says "Could bring a Gulf Fritillary egg, new to your garden" or "Could bring another Monarch", grouped under "New to your garden", "Familiar friends" and "Nectar plants". | The card above says "0 / 25 species welcomed", but the tray lists all 25 plants alike ("Host for Monarch and Queen"). The rule that decides who comes ("Shared host plants prefer a species you have not welcomed yet") is only in the Guide, though `nextSpeciesForPlant` already answers it for the plant panel. When seeds run out, every button goes disabled with no word in the tray. It is the same [recognition](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/recognition-and-recall/) case as B-G-3. | `garden/SeedTray.tsx` takes `creatures` and `blocker` and groups by `nextSpeciesForPlant`, in catalogue order within each group. Its first line gives `plantingBlocker`'s words when nothing can be planted, and the Guide's chosen seed says "Chosen in the Guide" in words as well as the highlight. "Could" is deliberate, since another plant may bloom first. No stored change. | S | Next |
| B-G-9 | **A butterfly's card that remembers a little.** Under the name: "With you since 3 March, as an egg · 41 days cared for", what it wears and flies, the goal it grew alongside, and "Read Sol's story". | The card shows species, stage, name and bond level and nothing more (`ButterflyCard.tsx:82–92`), while `lib/companionStory.ts` keeps much more, folded on Care under "Remembered". Meeting a butterfly is the natural moment to recall it. The evidence is modest: a small controlled study of online savouring raised positive emotion in 49 young adults ([Villani et al. 2023](https://bmcpsychology.biomedcentral.com/articles/10.1186/s40359-023-01225-z)), and a 2024 review protocol shows the clinical evidence is still being gathered ([Cullen et al. 2024](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC11020756/)). The claim is only that this costs nothing and fits. | `ButterflyCard.tsx` works out `companionStory` once when it opens and words it with `formatWelcomedOn`. `App.tsx` opens Care with a new `openStory` flag that `CompanionStory` takes as its first `open`. It never counts missed days or mentions today's care. No stored change. The card is lazy, and the wiring is about 20 B of first load. | S | Next |
| B-G-10 | **See the garden's name on a personal photo, by day and night.** The image studio's preview draws the name where the garden draws it, with a "By day / By night" switch and a one-line legibility hint that never blocks saving. | The studio advises "A little darker helps your garden's name stand out at night, a little lighter by day" (`CustomBackdropEditor.tsx:318–321`) but shows only the photo. By day the name is dark green with a cream glow, over a cream wash that fades out by mid-scene (`garden-3.css:196–221`), so a dark photo can leave it with little contrast, where [WCAG 1.4.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-minimum.html) asks 3:1 for large text over images too. [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/text-over-images/) warns that a fix that suits one image fails another, which is exactly the case with each gardener's own. | `CustomBackdropEditor.tsx` overlays a `.garden-heading` copy on `.crop-preview` and applies the night classes `GardenView` uses (D-42's wash and stars). A pure `headingLegibility(pixels, theme)` in `lib/customBackdrops.ts` tests a 48 × 16 px sample behind the heading with `lib/contrast`. The image never leaves the canvas, and D-18 is untouched. No stored change. | M | Later |
| B-G-11 | **Say once when a new scene has opened.** On the day a scene opens, at 14, 30 or 60 days, a small card under the hero shows it with "See it in the gallery" and "Put away". | `progressAppearance` adds the scenes silently (`lib/appearance.ts:177–210`), and only the scene galleries in Settings and in Shop › Backdrops, the fifth of six tabs, show them, so a gardener may never learn that Woodland Brook was waiting. That is a gap in [visibility of system status](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/visibility-system-status/), and the garden already has the right form in the welcome back and Marigold's letter. | A lazy `garden/SceneOpened.tsx` decides for itself, as `LetterCard` does, from `backdropUnlockDays` and `elapsedGardenDays` over days 0–6. Pass scenes are never announced. A put-away is kept per browser in one `localStorage` key inside try/catch, so a restore elsewhere may show the card once more. No stored garden change. About 40–50 B of first load. | S | Later |

**Considered, and not recommended here.**

- **A count or list of companions "still waiting" for care.** Care days never
  need to be in a row, and it would bring back the streak, which D-39 recommends keeping retired.
- **A confirmation before every purchase, or "days until you can afford it".**
  The first slows every deliberate buy and the second turns saving into a
  countdown. B-G-6 and B-G-3 answer both.
- **An explainer button on the header's Sunlight, Nectar and Stardust pills.**
  The header is in the first load, which has about 245 B to spare (F-30).
  B-G-3 puts the answer in the Shop and the Guide instead.
- **Stored "favourite" flags to choose which twelve butterflies fly.** It needs
  a creature field older builds would not keep, and B-G-4's "Meet" already
  reaches the butterflies outside the day's rotation.
- **Folded-wing resting art for every species.** It is new art. D-17 asks only
  whether butterflies settle, and still wings on the existing sprites answer
  it. The other art questions (D-18 to D-20) stay with the owner.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-17 (already asked).** D-17 is open: should butterflies settle at night?
  Recommendation: yes, whenever the night scene shows (B-G-2), not only in the
  evening hours, since a gardener who keeps the night theme all day already
  reads that butterflies rest.
- **D-66.** B-G-2 and B-G-4, with the wiring of B-G-3, B-G-9 and B-G-11,
  add roughly 230–310 B gzip to the first load. May they spend it from the
  ~2.4 kB that WP-F 3 frees? Recommendation: yes, after D-31's loading heading
  and 4.0's own needs, with the baseline set on purpose as D-10 and P-18 say.

### b.6 Noticed in Nature: the field book, My region and the Guide

This is where the garden meets the real world. The gardener logs a butterfly
or plant they saw, with a day, a few words and, if they like, a photo that is
redrawn on the device at 1,024 px or less, with nothing kept about where it was
taken. Privacy, photo handling, focus care and sourcing are strong, and
sightings rightly earn nothing. What holds the area back is reach. On a
Pixel 7 the field book's heading sits 2,666 px (3.2 screens) below a Journal
summary, "Butterflies welcomed", that never mentions sightings. The region
choice that turns on the native marks, the monthly "What to look for" line and
the nearby projects is 5,230 px down a 12,676 px Settings page, and onboarding
never asks for it. The photo control is the browser's bare "Choose File",
below the fold, with no camera in the Android app.

| Id | Improvement | Why it helps | How | Effort | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-N-1 | **The field book gets its own door.** Below "Field notes", the Journal shows a "Seen in the wild" card whose line names the latest sighting ("Monarch, 3 November") or "Nothing written down yet", never a tally. | Today the book opens only inside "Butterflies welcomed", after 25 species cards, and the Guide tells the gardener to look "under Seen in the wild", a heading they cannot see. A label that does not name what is behind it is not followed ([NN/g on information scent](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/information-scent/), [on accordions](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/accordions-complex-content/)), and noticing nearby nature is the part of the app with the best wellbeing evidence ([McEwan et al. 2019](https://doi.org/10.3390/ijerph16183373), [Richardson and Sheffield 2017](https://repository.derby.ac.uk/item/9457v/three-good-things-in-nature-noticing-nearby-nature-brings-sustained-increases-in-connection-with-nature)). | `JournalView.tsx`: move the lazy `FieldBook` block (lines 362–371) out of `details.field-notes` into a sibling card with its own open state, its line from `newestFirst(state.sightings)[0]`. `GuideView.tsx` gains an optional `onOpenFieldBook` ("Open your field book", with a way back). A `#/journal/field-book` address (about 40 B) waits for WP-F 3's bytes. No stored change. | S | Now |
| B-N-2 | **Ask where the gardener gardens, where it matters.** While no region is chosen, the field book and the Guide's "Where you live" offer one quiet card with the same "My region" choice as Settings, saying "the garden never asks where you are". Never a pop-up. | `profile.region` starts empty, so until it is set the monthly line, the native marks, the region-first list and the nearby project never appear. Most people never change a default ([Spool](https://archive.uie.com/brainsparks/2011/09/14/do-users-change-their-settings/)), so a feature behind a deep setting barely exists. | New lazy `components/sightings/RegionInvite.tsx`, reusing `REGION_CHOICES`. `App.tsx` passes the existing `garden.setRegion` (already given to Settings at line 1179) to the Journal and the Guide: a few bytes, to check with `scripts/check-budgets.mjs` against Android's ~245 B (F-30). No stored change: it writes `profile.region` as Settings does. | S | Now |
| B-N-3 | **Nature lines that hold true where the gardener lives.** The milkweed tip warns against tropical milkweed; the monarch's Europe note says it breeds in southern Spain, Portugal, the Canaries and Madeira; the queen, gulf fritillary and zebra longwing include Mexico; Europe's month lines say "In the UK" where their source does. | Xerces advises against tropical milkweed, which stays green through winter and builds up a monarch parasite ([Xerces](https://www.xerces.org/blog/tropical-milkweed-a-no-grow)), yet the card warns only about exotic pipevines. Monarchs breed in Iberia and Macaronesia ([Nota lepidopterologica 46](https://www.zobodat.at/pdf/Nota-lepidopterologica_46_0083-0101.pdf), [Naturalis](https://butterflies-europe.linnaeus.naturalis.nl/linnaeus_ng/app/views/species/taxon.php?id=114985)), and the file's own source starts the ranges in Mexico ([BAMONA, zebra longwing](https://www.butterfliesandmoths.org/species/Heliconius-charithonia), [gulf fritillary](https://www.butterfliesandmoths.org/species/Agraulis-vanillae)). "UK and Europe" gets only the UK's [Big Butterfly Count](https://bigbutterflycount.butterfly-conservation.org/); [eBMS's ButterflyCount app](https://butterfly-monitoring.net/ebms-app) covers Europe. | `data/regions.ts`, `data/whatToLookFor.ts` (a test: a line sourced only from butterfly-conservation.org starts "In the UK" or "In Britain"), and `regionalNatureProjects` as a list per region, with eBMS checked against the file's watching-not-catching rule. `natureProjects.release.test.ts` gives `NATURE_PROJECTS_CHECKED_ON` the freshness rule of F-35's fix. `speciesFacts.ts` keys its PMC publisher by article. Content and tests only, 0 B on the first load, no stored change. | S | Now |
| B-N-4 | **A photo step made for phones.** Near the top of the form, "A photo, if you like" offers "Take a photo" and "Choose a photo". The thumbnail shows while the gardener picks the butterfly, and a private window's "keeps no photos here" note shows at once, not after Save. | The control is now 823 px down a 1,089 px dialog in a 753 px window. In the Android app Capacitor opens the camera only for `capture` with `image/*` ([BridgeWebChromeClient](https://github.com/ionic-team/capacitor/blob/main/android/capacitor/src/main/java/com/getcapacitor/BridgeWebChromeClient.java)), and Chrome on Android 14 and 15 often hides Camera for a typed accept list ([addpipe](https://blog.addpipe.com/html-file-input-accept-video-camera-option-is-missing-android-14-15/)). Taking photos raises engagement, but not when the equipment gets in the way ([Diehl, Zauberman and Barasch 2016](https://www.apa.org/news/press/releases/2016/06/picture-enjoy)). | `SightingForm.tsx`: two label-wrapped hidden inputs, one with [`capture="environment"`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Attributes/capture) (on its own it would hide the library), each 44 px ([WCAG 2.5.8](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html)) with a `:focus-within` ring (F-22) and its text in its name (W-1). Keep `checkPhoto`'s answer, ignored today, where the trial runs (WP-C 6 skips it under the lock). Android: `GardenFilesPlugin.clearCapturedPhotos()` empties the camera's full-size JPEGs from the one folder `file_paths.xml` shares. No CAMERA permission, so Play's Data safety answers stand; the Android checks apply. Builds on F-11 and F-33. No stored change. | M | Now |
| B-N-5 | **A half-written sighting survives a lock, a reload or leaving.** Afterwards the field book, or the Guide card it began from, says "A sighting of a Monarch you began on Tuesday is kept", with "Carry on writing" and "Let it go". Only a photo being chosen is lost, and the lock screen names just that. | Once P-13 lands (D-5: lock at twice the chosen time), a garden left with the form open locks and the words go; a reader confirmed that "Lock now" from another tab loses them today. Every other place the gardener writes is a draft, or will be under WP-A 2. WCAG 2.2 accepts a timeout that would lose data when the data is kept more than 20 hours ([2.2.6 Timeouts](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/timeouts.html)). | New `lib/sightingDraft.ts` in the style of `goalEditDraft.ts`: one draft under a dated key (`sighting:` plus the day), so the seven-day pruning covers it and W-6's undated gap is not repeated. `SightingForm.tsx` writes it on change, clears it on Save and Cancel, and sets `data-lock-loses` only while a photo is chosen. Drafts are sealed and never in a backup; one drafts record stays (D-34). No stored garden change. | S | Next |
| B-N-6 | **A field book you can find your way around.** Month headings; a "Show" choice for butterflies, plants or one subject; "First this year" on a year's first sighting of each; a tapped photo opens whole; a photo that cannot be drawn says so instead of a broken image. | It is one list, ten at a time, of up to 2,000 sightings: at about three a week, last spring is a dozen presses away. Logged nectar plants have no Guide card, so this list is their only home. Photos show only cropped (`sightings.css:154`, `:178`), and `lib/sightingPhotos.ts` notes that storage can lose a Blob's bytes. "First seen this year" is a descriptive record phenology keeps ([Nature's Calendar](https://naturescalendar.woodlandtrust.org.uk/)), and headings carry the structure to a screen reader ([WCAG 1.3.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/info-and-relationships.html)). | `lib/sightings.ts`: `firstOfYear()` and `sightingsOf()`. `FieldBook.tsx`: the filter, month `h3`s over entry `h4`s, paging after the filter, and an optional starting filter from B-N-1. `PhotoImage.tsx`: an `onError` fallback; a photo viewer in the `garden-dialog` pattern with F-11's close listener. The Guide's "Seen in the wild" stamp opens the book on that butterfly, and the Journal subsection's sightings items (D-25) can use the same entry point. No stored change. | M | Next |
| B-N-7 | **"What to look for" leads somewhere, and Elsewhere gets a line too.** Where the month's line names a butterfly or plant the guide holds, the field book adds "Log a Monarch" or "Log milkweed", opening the form on that choice. Elsewhere and no region get "A way to look this month", such as "Some butterflies sip from damp ground: watch puddles and wet sand after rain." | The line stops at a sentence, and `lookForThisMonth()` returns nothing for Elsewhere even with a hemisphere set. Prompts to notice *nearby* nature carried the wellbeing effect ([McEwan et al. 2019](https://doi.org/10.3390/ijerph16183373)). It is the existing monthly line, not the daily prompt D-26 recommends against. | `data/whatToLookFor.ts`: `MonthLine` gains optional `subjects` (the monarch line from [BAMONA](https://www.butterfliesandmoths.org/species/Danaus-plexippus) among them) and a sourced `howToLook` list by month. `LookForLine.tsx` takes an optional `onLog`, used only by `FieldBook.tsx`. Tests: every subject is in `data/content`, every line has an https source and no catching wording. Species the guide lacks (comma, kahukura) get no button. No stored change. | S | Next |
| B-N-8 | **The Guide answers "which live near me, and which have I seen?"** With a region chosen, "Your collection" gains "Native where I garden" and "Seen where I garden", and "Seen in the wild by me" once the book holds anything. | The only filters are the game's own progress. The 25 cards make the Guide 25,782 px long on a Pixel 7, and a gardener in the UK and Europe has 4 native species and 2 more seen among them (in Australia and New Zealand, none native). | `SpeciesDirectory.tsx`: extend the filter with `regionMark(...)?.presence` and the seen set. Derive the hard-coded "Shared hosts" sentence from `hostPlantIds` in `data/content`, so a species pack (D-46) cannot make it wrong; a test pins today's text. No stored change. | S | Next |
| B-N-9 | **Recognise it in the list.** Each butterfly in the sighting form's list shows a 28 px still drawing and its scientific name, under "Drawings from the garden, to jog your memory." | A gardener who saw "an orange one with black veins" must now recall a name; recognising beats recalling ([NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/recognition-and-recall/)). The 25 sprites are already distinct, and each choice already carries `scientificName`. | `SightingForm.tsx`: a `ButterflySprite` (size 28, still, `aria-hidden`) and a `<small>` per choice. Check 25 sprites in the 256 px list against `e2e/perf.spec.ts`, and draw only rows in view if needed. No stored change. | S | Later |
| B-N-10 | **The day comes from the photo, where the photo says it.** When a chosen JPEG records the day it was taken, the day field moves to it, unless the gardener has set the day or it is in the future, with "The day was taken from your photo." | Many sightings are written up later from the camera roll. Android's picker and desktops usually keep `DateTimeOriginal`; iOS Safari usually strips it ([WebKit bug 207088](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=207088)), so an iPhone keeps today, as now. | `lib/imageImport.ts`: `photoTakenOn(bytes)` reads only tag 0x9003 from the header bytes already read, and never follows the GPS pointer (0x8825); a test proves it. About 0.5 kB in the lazy form. No stored change: the stored photo is still re-encoded with no metadata. | S | Later |
| B-N-11 | **Frame what you saw before the photo is made smaller.** After choosing, an optional "Frame it" lets the gardener drag, pinch or use sliders, then "Use this". The stored photo is the framed part, within the same limits. | Watched "from a little way off", a 6 cm red admiral at 1.5 m spans about 3 % of a 12 MP frame: about 127 px across in the original but about 32 px once the whole frame is reduced to 1,024 px (by arithmetic), too small to tell from a painted lady. Framing first keeps about four times the detail at the same stored size. | `prepareSightingPhoto(file, frame?)` draws a source rectangle from `coverGeometry`, decoding once to at most 3,072 px with [`createImageBitmap`'s resize options](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/createImageBitmap) where supported (WP-F 6). New lazy `PhotoFrame.tsx` reuses `CustomBackdropEditor`'s pointer handling and sliders, the single-pointer way [WCAG 2.5.7](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/dragging-movements.html) asks for. No stored change: the same Blob types, 1,024 px and 400 KB. | L | Later |

**Considered, and not recommended here.**

- **A free-text "not in the guide" sighting.** `checkSighting` keeps only
  subjects the catalogue knows, so builds 3.3 to 3.9 would drop it and the
  next save would lose it for good. Regional species packs (D-46, section (c))
  are the honest route.
- **Reading the photo's location, or the device's.** It would break "the
  garden never asks where you are". B-N-10 reads only the day.
- **A species tally, a life list or a "log ten species" goal.** Sightings earn
  nothing by design. "First this year" describes nature's timing, not effort.
- **Naming the species from the photo on the device.** A model of tens of
  megabytes, uneven for regional species, and confident wrong names. B-N-9 and
  B-N-11 help the gardener name it instead.
- **More than 50 photos, or a longer edge than 1,024 px.** Every photo rides in
  every backup and crosses the Android bridge as one string (F-10). Revisit
  after WP-E 2's chunked save.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-25 (already asked).** D-25 has a recommendation but no recorded answer, and the
  Journal subsection's sightings work (B-J-3) waits on it. Should sightings
  go into Journal search and readable copies, with one line in the letter and
  the year, and should a logged nectar plant get a Guide card? Recommendation:
  answer it before that work ships. Search and copies yes, as recommended; no
  nectar card for now, since B-N-6's "Show" choice gives nectar plants a home.
- **D-67.** Should a sighting gain two optional fields, "How many?" (1 to
  999) and "Where, in your own words" (up to 60 characters)? Counts match what
  the [Big Butterfly Count](https://bigbutterflycount.butterfly-conservation.org/)
  and [eBMS](https://butterfly-monitoring.net/ebms-app) record, and the place
  needs nothing from the device. Builds 3.3 to 3.9 keep unknown fields when
  they read a garden, but one that *edits* such a sighting rebuilds it and
  drops both. Recommendation: later, and only if that loss in a stale tab or an
  older install is acceptable. It is the one item here that changes what is
  stored.

### b.7 Keeping the garden safe: backups, copies, the move and the lock

The garden's safety tools are thorough and honest. A gardener can keep plain,
protected and shared backups, move with a file and twelve one-time words,
preview a restore, put back one of up to seven earlier copies, and lock the
garden with a passcode, a recovery code and a passkey, and the lock says
plainly that nothing opens it once both secrets are lost. But all of it starts
9,571 px down a 12,676 px Settings page on a Pixel 7, and nothing near the top
says when the last copy was kept or whether the lock is on. Two gaps matter
most. The recovery code is read row by row from a sheet written in columns, so
the gardener's own saved or printed sheet, pasted, does not open the garden
(measured with the real word list). And the restore preview never sets the
file beside the garden here, so a March backup chosen in November reads like
yesterday's.

| Id | Improvement | Why it helps | How | Effort | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-S-1 | **The recovery code reads the gardener's own sheet, and helps before the slow check.** A pasted sheet opens the garden, a quiet line counts "17 of 24 words", an unknown word gets "Did you mean “bramble”?" before Open is pressed, and the passcode gains "Show passcode". | `recoverySheet` (`lib/recoveryCode.ts:104`) writes three columns of eight and `readRecoveryCode` (`:53`) drops the numbers, so a pasted grid reads words 1, 9, 17, 2, 10, 18, and the whole sheet stops at "“the” is not one of the recovery words". After a 600,000-round check the gardener is told to check the words "in order", which they did, one tap from "Delete this garden…"; this is close to a defect and can ride with WP-A. The move code already suggests the nearest word, [WCAG 2.2's Error Suggestion](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/error-suggestion.html) asks for a correction whenever one is known, and [the text for SC 3.3.8](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/accessible-authentication-minimum.html) says showing a password "can improve the chance of success". | In `lib/recoveryCode.ts`, a `wordsOf()` folds accents ("rosé" reads as "rose", not "ros") and orders the words by their numbers when each of 1 to 24 appears once; `nearestWords` moves from `moveCode.ts` into a shared `lib/codeWords.ts`, so the lock screen never loads the move chunk. `LockScreen.tsx` shows the count and the first unknown word 400 ms after typing stops, in a `role="status"` line tied by `aria-describedby`, and `LockedCopyPrompt.tsx` and `ArriveDialog.tsx` ("9 of 12 words") reuse it. No stored change: every code ever written reads as before, and D-22's check bits, which catch the 739 word pairs one edit apart, add to it later. | S | Now |
| B-S-2 | **A restore preview that sets the backup beside the garden here.** A small table, "This backup and the garden here", and one plain line when the file ends earlier: "This backup ends on 3 March. The garden here has 41 days written after that." | The preview (`SettingsView.tsx:1333-1424`) describes the file well, but of the garden here says only that it "is kept aside, so you can undo the restore afterwards". A backup chosen by mistake reads like yesterday's, and Undo helps only if the loss is noticed. Daily copies also lose their time of day in the list (`dailyCopies.ts:217`), and [NN/g on confirmations](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/confirmation-dialog/): "Be specific and inform users about the consequence of their action." | `lib/backupPreview.ts` gains `compareWithHere(incoming, here)`: counts on both sides, the last day written, the days written here after the file's last day, a differing name and, for an earlier copy, the entries added and removed since it. `SettingsView.tsx` renders a real `<table>` and the line, `describeCopies` keeps `takenAt` for daily copies ("before your first change at 08:12"), and a new `backupPreview.test.ts` gives the module a test file of its own. No stored change; the backup format is untouched. | M | Now |
| B-S-3 | **The garden's safety at a glance.** A small card under the Settings header, "Keeping your garden safe", says when the last copy was kept, how many earlier copies are here, whether the browser has agreed to keep the garden, and whether the lock is on, each with its one button and a link to the full card. | In the walk of the production build, Backup starts at 9,571 px, Earlier copies at 10,242 px and the lock at 11,423 px, with no in-page contents, and the "time for a fresh copy" mark (`App.tsx:268-270`) opens the top of that page. `lastBackupAt` is kept on every copy but shown only once a copy is due (30 days or 50 entries); [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/visibility-system-status/): people "uninformed about the system’s current status cannot decide what to do next", and [Apple's iCloud Backup](https://support.apple.com/en-us/108366) shows the date and time of the last backup. In [Backblaze's 2024 poll](https://www.backblaze.com/blog/2024-state-of-the-backup-security-incidents-and-data-loss-on-the-rise/), only 15 % of Americans were fully certain their most important files were secure. | A new `components/backup/SafetyGlance.tsx`, rendered by `SettingsView.tsx` after the header, fed by `lastBackupAt`, the reminder already computed, the storage status and one shared `onListEarlierCopies` call; lock state comes from `useLockState` and an exported `useAutoLock`, and the buttons reuse `saveBackup` and `lockGarden(true)`. The Settings mark lands on the card, the link focuses the backup heading, and the Settings style baselines are re-recorded on purpose. No stored change; it need not wait for WP-G 1's split. | M | Now |
| B-S-4 | **Check a backup without restoring anything.** "Check a backup file…" opens a plain, protected or move file and answers "This backup opens and reads cleanly", with B-S-2's comparison and "Nothing here has changed." | Today the only way to learn whether a file opens is to start a restore, whose first words are "Restoring replaces everything currently in this browser" (`SettingsView.tsx:1301`), so a wrong passphrase is found only on the day it is needed; on Android a check would also catch F-9's broken file. New Zealand's [NCSC](https://www.ncsc.govt.nz/protect-your-organisation/implement-and-test-backups/): "Test your backups so you know you can successfully restore your data", and Trezor's [Check backup](https://trezor.io/guides/backups-recovery/general-standards/check-backup-on-trezor-safe-5) is a [dry-run recovery](https://wiki.trezor.io/User_manual:Dry-run_recovery) that changes nothing. | A `checking` flow in `SettingsView.tsx` reuses `readBackupFile`, `sealedKind` and `ProtectedRestore` with a new `next` value, `'check'` (and its line in `whatFollows.ts`), then `previewBackupFile` and `verifyGardenImages`, and never calls `replace()`. `SealedBackupPrompt.tsx` shows the envelope's `createdAt` and `appVersion` ("Saved on 3 March 2026, by version 3.8") as display only, since they are not authenticated, and a saved protected backup offers "Check that it opens…". No stored change; a check does not move `lastBackupAt`. | M | Now |
| B-S-5 | **Before deleting everything, say when the last copy was kept.** The confirmation adds "Your last backup is from 3 November" (or "There is no backup of this garden yet") and "The earlier copies kept on this device go too", with [Save a backup first] before "Yes, delete everything". | Settings' delete (`SettingsView.tsx:1620-1660`) asks only "Are you certain you want to start over?", though `clear()` takes the earlier and set-aside copies too (`gardenRepository.ts:1887-1899`). Safe mode refuses its delete until a copy is saved and WP-A 6 (F-7) gives the lock screen's one a sealed copy, so this is the one delete with no copy on offer. A confirmation should ["restate the user’s request and explain what the computer is about to do"](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/confirmation-dialog/), and [Google asks people to download their data](https://support.google.com/accounts/answer/32046) before deleting an account. | In the delete card of `SettingsView.tsx`, `formatJournalDate` on `profile.lastBackupAt` and the existing `saveBackup` (or `setProtecting('save')` while the lock is on), with focus on "Keep my garden" from WP-D 5 (F-26). Deleting stays one press away, so a shared phone is never held up. No stored change. | S | Now |
| B-S-6 | **When the garden cannot be read, offer the earlier copies that can.** For a garden withheld as malformed, the recovery screen lists the three newest earlier copies that open ("Monday 16 November, before your first change: 41 check-ins…"), each with "Look at this copy" and "Put this copy back". | `RecoveryScreen.tsx` offers the set-aside copy, a backup, Arrive and "Start a new garden instead…", but not the daily copies, which are separate records and may open when today's parts do not. For a gardener with no backup file (about one in five people never back up, in [Backblaze's 2023 survey](https://www.backblaze.com/blog/2023-state-of-the-backup-as-data-needs-grow-backups-need-to-fill-the-gaps/)), that leaves only a fresh start while yesterday's whole garden sits on the device. | `App.tsx` passes the existing `earlierCopies`, `readCopy` and `putBackCopy` to the lazy `RecoveryScreen`, which shows `EarlierCopies` without Discard and the preview through `previewGarden`; `replace()` already works while the garden is withheld. One honest line says earlier copies leave out field-book photos, which the set-aside copy keeps. No stored change; the props cost about 40 B gzip of the Android first load, so it lands with WP-F 3, and safe mode's share waits for it too. | M | Next |
| B-S-7 | **Practise opening the garden, changing nothing.** With the lock on, "Check my recovery code…" and "Practise my passcode…" answer "These words open your garden" or "That isn’t it, and that’s all right: nothing has changed", with an opt-in reminder to practise now and then. | Nobody can open the garden once both secrets are lost (`NO_WAY_BACK`), yet the sheet is checked once, two words at setup, and auto-lock defaults to Never, so the passcode may rarely be typed. [Signal](https://signal.org/blog/signal-pins/) has the same design and helps people remember with reminders that "get less frequent over time", and with spaced practice 94 % of participants learned a random 56-bit secret ([Bonneau and Schechter, USENIX Security 2014](https://usenix.org/node/184458)). It keeps WP-C 1's stronger passcodes (D-47) remembered, and is the lock's own [dry-run recovery](https://wiki.trezor.io/User_manual:Dry-run_recovery). | `lib/gardenLockActions.ts` gains `checkRecoveryCode(words)`, which opens the recovery slot and discards the key, beside the existing `checkPasscode` (`:227`); `LockChange.tsx` gains the two check kinds, and a failed recovery check offers "Make a new recovery code…" while the garden still opens. The reminder (after a week, a month, three months, then every six months; a quiet line, never on a gentle day) adds the optional `Profile.lockPracticeAt`, which older builds keep and ignore. | M | Next |
| B-S-8 | **Finish turning the lock on with the two choices that matter next.** The last step offers "Lock by itself" (Never stays chosen unless changed) and says "Backups you saved before today are not locked", with [Save a protected backup…]. | The done step (`LockSetup.tsx:154-170`) says the garden locks itself "if you choose that in Settings" but offers no choice. Fewer than 5 % of users had changed any setting in [Jared Spool's study](https://archive.uie.com/brainsparks/2011/09/14/do-users-change-their-settings/), so a gardener who locks a journal on a shared phone is then protected only by "Lock now", 11,000 px down Settings. WP-C 5's line about the kept file (F-16) belongs here too. | Extract the select from `LockSettings.tsx` into an `AutoLockChoice` that both screens render. An `onProtectedBackup` callback runs from `SettingsView` through `LockSettings` to `LockSetup`, and opens `ProtectedBackupDialog` once the lock dialog closes. No stored change: `autoLockMinutes` is already in the lock record. | S | Next |
| B-S-9 | **Firefox asks to keep the garden only when the gardener does, and the room it takes is shown in parts.** In Firefox, "Ask the browser to keep my garden" explains the prompt first; everywhere, the storage line lists the journal, photos, own images, earlier copies and set-aside copies, with one kind line near the quota. | The garden asks quietly after the first load and after meaningful actions (`usePersistence.ts:190`, `useGardenState.ts`), which is right where browsers decide silently. But [Firefox shows a permission popup](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria), so a gardener can meet it unexplained mid check-in, and a reflexive Block makes "Ask the browser again" useless. One total ("about 144 KB here") does not say what to trim. | `requestPersistentStorage({ quietly: true })` in `lib/storage.ts` returns early where `navigator.permissions.query({ name: 'persistent-storage' })` answers "prompt", and the Settings button asks without `quietly`. The breakdown sums parts with `dailyCopies.sizeOf`, `photosIn` and the existing copy sizes. No stored change; the check costs about 40 B gzip of first load, so it lands with WP-F 3. | M | Later |
