| B-S-10 | **A backup reminder that can rest.** Beside the note, a quiet "Remind me later" rests the note and the Settings mark for 30 days; then the same rule applies again. | The note and the mark stay until a copy is made, and only a gentle day hides them, so a gardener who keeps copies another way cannot quiet them. The module's own comment says "a reminder that keeps asking turns a safety net into a chore" (`lib/backupReminder.ts`). | `due()` in `lib/backupReminder.ts` reads an optional `Profile.backupReminderRestsUntil`, set by a small reducer beside `recordBackup` and dropped by `validateGarden` when malformed; older builds keep and ignore it. About 30 B gzip of first load (`isBackupDue` in `App.tsx`), so it lands with WP-F 3. | S | Later |
| B-S-11 | **A passkey the gardener can recognise, and tidy away.** New passkeys are named "The Butterfly Garden (added 17 November 2026)", removing one asks first, and where the browser can, the password manager is told it is no longer used. | Every passkey is named "My garden" (`lib/passkey.ts:114`), which says nothing among other sites, and "Remove the passkey" runs at once (`LockSettings.tsx:135`), leaving it in the manager to be offered again and fail. [Chrome's WebAuthn Signal API](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/identity/webauthn-signal-api) lets a page say a credential no longer exists (Chrome and Edge 132 and later, not Firefox), and with no server the lock record is the truth. | `lib/passkey.ts` dates `user.name` and `displayName`, and a new `forgetPasskey(credentialId)` feature-detects `PublicKeyCredential.signalUnknownCredential` and calls it after a removal or a "no-prf" add; `LockSettings.tsx` gains the confirmation. No stored change; existing passkeys keep working, and D-7's explicit `rp.id` is untouched. | S | Later |

**Considered, and not recommended here.**

- **A passcode or passphrase hint.** One in the clear weakens what is already
  the weakest way in (F-14), and one inside the sealed envelope changes the
  backup format.
- **A lockout or growing delay after wrong passcodes.** Each try already costs
  600,000 rounds, and a lockout does nothing against an offline copy; it would
  only lock out a gardener who is upset or unsure.
- **Autocompleting each recovery word, or 24 separate fields.** 195 of the
  2,048 words begin another word ("bake", "baker"), and 24 fields make pasting
  harder; B-S-1's count and suggestion give most of the help.
- **Argon2id, or the move code shown again or as a QR code.** D-23 recommends
  keeping PBKDF2 and D-21 keeping QR deferred, and the move code is one-time
  by design.
- **One entry back from an earlier copy, a "recently removed" bin, or merging
  gardens on restore.** These are new capabilities for (c); B-S-2's comparison
  by entry id is their groundwork.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-68.** Should the practice checks come with the opt-in reminder (a
  quiet line in Settings and on the glance card, spaced like Signal's PIN
  reminders), or ship as the two checks alone (B-S-7)? Recommended: both, with
  the reminder off by default. It builds on D-47 and leaves D-23 as it is.
- **D-22 (already asked).** D-22 asks whether new recovery codes should carry check bits.
  B-S-1 does not need the answer, but only check bits catch a word typed as
  another valid word. Recommended: yes, as D-22 recommends, after B-S-1.

### b.8 Settings, navigation, onboarding and updates

The gardener meets this area at the edge of every visit, and its machinery is
careful and private. The Guide, breathing and the comfort plan already return
the gardener to the exact link they pressed, the Guide's sections have their
own addresses, and iPhone gardeners get a plain card about the Home Screen.
What is missing is reach and a sense of place. In the walk of the production
build on a Pixel 7, Settings is one 12,676 px page of about 60 controls with no
contents, Backup starts at 9,571 px, and none of the places that send the
gardener there is a link; the pages it opens come back to its top with focus
lost, and What's new, which has no Back, opens by itself after an update even
over the "Check in" shortcut. Most worth doing first are a contents with an
address for each part of Settings (B-H-1) and a Back that puts the gardener
where they were (B-H-2); nearly every row costs tens of bytes of first load, so
they land with WP-F 3 (F-30).

| Id | Improvement | Why it helps | How | Effort | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-H-1 | **Settings in parts, each with its own address.** Under the heading, an "On this page" block lists the parts in four groups as the Guide's pills ("How it looks and sounds", "Your days", "Scenes", "Keeping it safe and about"), and choosing one brings its card into view with focus on its heading. | Backup is 9,571 px down and the lock 11,423 px, and none of the places that send the gardener to Settings is a link, among them the "time for a fresh copy" mark, the write-error banner "Save a backup from Settings before closing the app" (`App.tsx:829-838`), the read-only banner, the Home Screen card and two Guide sentences (`GuideView.tsx:79, :93`). The write error is the moment the last changes exist only in memory. [NN/g](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/in-page-links-content-navigation/) finds in-page links "most valuable for long-form content", and the Guide already solves the same problem with pills and `#/guide/{id}` addresses. | `lib/routes.ts` gains `#/settings/{part}`, validated in the lazy `SettingsView` as `GuideView` validates a section (an unknown part opens at the top); cards take ids from their existing `aria-labelledby` headings, the links navigate with `replace` as D-36's Shop tabs do, and the banners gain "Save a backup now" and "Restore a backup". A `scroll-padding` rule keeps a jumped-to heading clear of the header and dock ([WCAG 2.4.11](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/focus-not-obscured-minimum.html)); the block sits under B-S-3's safety card and need not wait for WP-G 1. No stored change; about 60–90 B gzip of first load. | M | Now |
| B-H-2 | **Back puts the gardener where they were.** Privacy, Open-source notices, What's new and support, opened from Settings, return to the button that opened them with focus on it, by any Back; What's new gains a Back button; the browser's Back to any page finds it scrolled as it was left. | The About card's pages return with `setView('settings')` (`App.tsx:1066-1070`) to the top of a 12,676 px page, What's new has no Back and Android's back from it goes to the garden (`backNavigation.ts:39`), and the `popstate` handler scrolls every other page to 0 (`App.tsx:340-345`). NN/g's advice is to ["save scroll position almost always"](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/saving-scroll-position/) on a return, and [Baymard's testing](https://baymard.com/blog/back-button-expectations) finds people expect Back to return them to what they perceive as the previous page. | Reuse the way-back machinery rather than add a second: `LinkPlace` in `lib/routes.ts` gains an optional `opened`, the four buttons get `data-way-back`, and `returnFrom` and `backAction` cover the four pages. `navigate()` writes the leaving page's `scrollY` into `history.state` before pushing, with `history.scrollRestoration = 'manual'`; W-7 folds in, and the new field is checked with WP-C 3's `history.state` validation (F-20). No stored change (`history.state` only); about 50–80 B gzip of first load. | M | Now |
| B-H-3 | **The garden opens as fast as it loads.** A returning gardener goes from the launch straight to the garden; the butterflies show only when reading the garden takes longer than a moment, and the very first launch keeps today's splash. | Every open waits at least 700 ms (`SPLASH_MINIMUM_MS`, `App.tsx:198`), though the constant's own comment says the garden usually loads "in a few dozen milliseconds", and at 700 ms only the monarch of the five flyers has come into view (`SplashScreen.tsx`). In the Android app it follows the system splash, the duplicate [Google's migration guide](https://developer.android.com/guide/topics/ui/splash-screen/migrate) says to remove, and [1 s is the limit for keeping a train of thought](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/response-times-3-important-limits/) on a visit that is often one check-in. | `App.tsx` replaces the minimum with a delay (about 200 ms before the splash shows) and a hold (about 400 ms once shown, so it never flickers), keeping 700 ms when there is no garden yet; WP-D 6 (F-27) puts the splash inside the theme, and B-H-3 brings that one item forward with it. Holding Android's system splash with `setKeepOnScreenCondition` can follow, measured first. No stored change; about 0–25 B gzip of first load. | S | Now |
| B-H-4 | **What's new waits for the garden, and reads easily.** It opens by itself only when a visit begins on the garden, not on a gentle day or mid-recap; dates read as the journal writes them, the newest release is open with the rest under "Earlier releases", and releases since the last look are marked. | `useWhatsNewOnce` checks focus and dialogs but not the page, so launching the "Check in" shortcut, a `#/breathe` link or a reload of the support page lands on What's new instead, and a gentle day hides the backup mark but not this. [WCAG 2.2's Interruptions](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/interruptions.html) (AAA) asks that interruptions can be postponed, and [Mark, Gudith and Klocke](https://www.ics.uci.edu/~gmark/chi08-mark.pdf) found interrupted work came with more stress and frustration. All ten releases render in full with dates like "2026-11-01", and four carry none (W-10 names 3.4 and 3.5). | `useWhatsNewOnce` takes the active page and settles to the garden's existing notice unless it is the garden on an ordinary day; `WhatsNewView` uses `formatJournalDate`, a `<details>` for older releases, and a "since" passed through `history.state` from the `lastSeenReleaseId` it replaces. `App.update.test`, which tests only the bare address today, gains `#/today/check-in` and `#/breathe`. No stored change; about 15–25 B gzip of first load. | S | Now |
| B-H-5 | **Onboarding that starts comfortable and lets the gardener step back.** The welcome offers "Text size and motion" in place, each later step reads "Step 1 of 3", and a Back returns to the step before with everything kept. | The four steps have no Back (`Onboarding.tsx`), nothing says how many there are, and text size and motion wait for Settings, since onboarding is "always at the usual size" (`App.tsx:483-487`); a gardener with low vision or motion sensitivity meets the lesson's three cards first. [Apple's iPhone setup](https://support.apple.com/111780) offers accessibility on its first screens, [NN/g's wizard guidance](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/wizards/) asks for a clear step indicator, and a Back that keeps entries honours [WCAG 2.2's Redundant Entry](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/redundant-entry.html). | `Onboarding.tsx` gains a `look` state applied live with `applyTextSize` and the motion class, Back buttons and step labels; `finishOnboarding` and `useGardenState.onboard` set the size and motion in the same pass as `createInitialState`, so the garden is saved once. F-38's lesson fix lands in the same file, and D-20 is untouched. No new stored field: it sets the existing `textSize`, `calmMotion` and `reducedMotion`; about 25–40 B gzip of first load. | M | Next |
| B-H-6 | **A page change is said, not only shown.** After a page is chosen in the header, the dock or More, a screen reader hears its name once ("Settings") while focus stays on the tab; a page opened from inside another (a Journal day, a Guide section) takes focus to its heading. | Focus stays on the tab or returns to More (`Navigation.tsx:106-118`), `aria-current` moves silently, and the title never changes (F-27 gives each page one), so a blind gardener who picks Settings hears "More, button". In [Marcy Sutton's testing with Fable](https://marcysutton.com/prototype-testing-accessible-clientside-routing/), NVDA with Firefox and VoiceOver with Safari announced nothing on client-side route changes, which led [Gatsby](https://v2.gatsbyjs.com/blog/2020-02-10-accessible-client-side-routing-improvements) and later Next.js to add a route announcer. | `App.tsx` keeps one visually hidden, always-mounted `role="status"` region and sets it to the new page's `h1` once the lazy page draws, unless the route carries a restore, so B-H-2's returns are not said twice; in-content navigation focuses `#main-content h1` instead. The wording follows F-27's titles, neutral for the comfort plan and support. No stored change; about 60–80 B gzip of first load. | S | Next |
| B-H-7 | **Carry a Safari garden to the Home Screen in fewer steps.** The Home Screen card gains "Go to Backup" (or "Move my garden…" while the lock is on), and a Home Screen copy with no garden opens with the restore choice showing: "Bringing your garden from Safari?" | Safari deletes a site's storage [after seven days of Safari use without interaction](https://webkit.org/blog/10218/full-third-party-cookie-blocking-and-more/), a Home Screen app keeps its own, and [the two do not share it](https://firt.dev/notes/pwa-ios/). Today the carry-over is one sentence (`HomeScreenGuide.tsx:66`): find Backup 9,571 px down Settings, add to the Home Screen, then spot "I have a backup" beside "Enter the garden", which by habit starts a second, empty garden. Since iOS 26 [sites added to the Home Screen open as web apps by default](https://www.macrumors.com/how-to/save-safari-bookmark-web-app-iphone-home-screen/), so this is now the usual path. | `HomeScreenGuide.tsx` takes an `onSaveBackup` that `GardenView` wires to B-H-1's `#/settings/backup`, or to the move while the lock is on, so a locked garden travels sealed; `Onboarding.tsx` starts with `restoreOpen` true when `isIPhoneOrIPad() && isInstalledApp()`, as it already does for an arrival. No stored change; Android folds `lib/install`'s checks away, and the web first load gains about 10–20 B gzip. | S | Next |
| B-H-8 | **Reminders the gardener can shape.** "A daily garden visit" gains "On these days" (every day, weekdays, or the goals' weekday chips), a choice of what the calendar shows ("Garden time 🦋", "A quiet moment", "Time for me"), and "Last added: weekdays at 8:00 AM". | There is one shape: every day (`RRULE:FREQ=DAILY`, `calendarReminder.ts:87`) titled "Garden time 🦋", which shows on lock screens and in calendars others may see, and nothing says a visit was already added, so a second tap adds a second event (F-38). Cue-based plans help, modestly: d = .65 in [Gollwitzer and Sheeran's 2006 meta-analysis](https://kops.uni-konstanz.de/entities/publication/2e749bfb-8533-437c-8203-7e788c910c5f), smaller after bias correction in a [2024 synthesis of 642 tests](https://doi.org/10.1080/10463283.2024.2334563). Lally et al. (2010) found a missed day ["did not materially affect the habit formation process"](https://jamesclear.com/new-habit), which favours chosen days over every day. | `calendarReminderText` takes optional days (`FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=…`, `DTSTART` on the first chosen day) and a title, and the eight static iPhone files stay daily; `DailyEvent.java` builds the rule from a whitelist of two-letter codes, never page text, and the Android confirmation asks the gardener to check the event has a notification, since its insert screen sets none. Adds optional `profile.reminderDays` and `profile.reminderTitle`, which older builds keep and ignore; about 25–40 B gzip of first load, plus CLAUDE.md's Android checks. | M | Next |
| B-H-9 | **Android's back gesture shows where it leads.** At the garden, with nothing open, the swipe shows Android's own preview of the home screen; everywhere else back works as it does now. | The app targets API 36, and [Android 16 turns predictive back animations on by default](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/16/behavior-changes-16) for such apps. But `@capacitor/app` registers an always-enabled `OnBackPressedCallback` (`AppPlugin.java:46`), and [Google's guidance](https://developer.android.com/guide/navigation/predictive-back-gesture) is that a root activity intercepting back this way never shows back-to-home, so the garden simply vanishes with no chance to change one's mind mid-swipe. | `lib/native.ts` gains `setBackHandled(enabled)` over `App.toggleBackButtonHandler` (in `@capacitor/app` 8.1.1), and a small lazy, Android-only module turns the handler off only at the garden with no open dialog or `[data-dismiss-on-back]` layer, and only on Android 12 and later, where [back on a root activity moves it to the background](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/12/behavior-changes-all) as `minimizeApp` does; the manifest sets `enableOnBackInvokedCallback` for Android 15. No stored change; about 15–25 B gzip of Android first load, the Android checks, and a look on a real Android 15 or 16 phone. | M | Next |
| B-H-10 | **The update offer waits for a calm moment, and says what is kept.** The toast holds back during breathing, the recap, the comfort plan, the support page or an open dialog, and reads "Anything you are writing is kept, and every open garden tab updates together." | `UpdatePrompt` slides in at the foot of whatever page is open, a breath or a hard moment included ([Interruptions](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/interruptions.html); [Mark et al.](https://www.ics.uci.edu/~gmark/chi08-mark.pdf)). It does not say that "Update now" first keeps drafts and waiting saves (`lib/appUpdate.ts`, 3 s cap), so a gardener mid-reflection has reason to press "Later" and never update, and its `role="status"` mounts with its content, which some screen readers do not announce. | `App.tsx` passes a `quiet` flag; `UpdatePrompt.tsx` keeps an always-mounted hidden status region and shows the toast only when `needRefresh && !quiet`, with an `App.update.test` for `needRefresh`, which F-42 notes is untested at app level. No stored change; web only, about 10 B gzip. | S | Later |
| B-H-11 | **"What does this do?" answered in place.** Pressing it opens the Guide's own opening sentences under the link, with "More in the Guide →" for the full section; pressing again, Escape or Android's back puts it away. | The nine links leave the page for a Guide section whose first sentence or two answers the question (`GuideView.tsx:57-130`), and on the garden the link wraps under the dock (lead's walk). [Heydon Pickering's toggletip](https://inclusive-components.design/tooltips-toggletips/) answers in place through a live region and closes with Escape, which meets [WCAG 1.4.13](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/content-on-hover-or-focus.html). | Move `guideSections` into a lazy `lib/guideText.ts` shared by `GuideView` and a new lazy `GuideAnswer`, so the words keep one source; `GuideLink` becomes a button with `aria-expanded` and `data-dismiss-on-back`, keeping its address as "More in the Guide", way back and all. No stored change; about 80–120 B gzip of first load, so it waits until WP-F 3 has reclaimed its 2.4 kB. | M | Later |

**Considered, and not recommended here.**

- **Moving Backup and the lock to the top of Settings, or a page per part.**
  Reordering upsets every gardener's sense of place and the visual baselines,
  and separate pages add taps and lose the browser's Find; B-H-1 gives the
  same reach with nothing moved.
- **A sticky contents bar, or a search box, in Settings.** A sticky bar would
  take about a fifth of a 412 × 839 px screen on every view, and about 60
  controls in fourteen named parts are served by a contents; the Guide's
  search is there for 25 sections of prose.
- **Notifications from the garden itself.** Web push needs a push server, which
  the garden promises never to have; on-device notifications in the Android
  app are a new capability for (c).
- **Silent updates, or the new release's notes in the toast.** A reload in
  mid-sentence is what the prompt and the draft flush exist to avoid, and the
  old page cannot read the waiting build's notes; What's new says the same at
  a calmer moment (B-H-4).
- **A safety card at the top of Settings, and a "For harder days" group in
  More.** B-S-3 and B-C-1 propose them; B-H-1 gives the card its addresses and
  does not repeat it.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-69.** Should Settings' scene gallery open folded to the scene in use,
  with "Show all scenes", while the full gallery stays in the Shop's Backdrops
  tab (B-H-1)? Recommended: yes; it moves Backup up by about 2,500 px on a
  phone.
- **D-70.** Which discreet calendar titles should sit beside "Garden time
  🦋" (B-H-8)? Recommended: "A quiet moment" and "Time for me", neither naming
  what the app is for.

### b.9 Accessibility and comfort across the garden

Where comfort exists, it is careful. Theme and text size apply the moment they
are chosen, the device's request for less motion is honoured, jar marks are
spoken by name, and focus goes back to whatever opened a dialog. The gaps are
at the edges of who uses the garden. Measured in the production build on a
360 px phone: Largest text with the phone's own 200 % leaves the header and dock
57 % of the screen and cuts text off past the right edge; nothing answers a
request for more contrast; a Persian phone titles the weather calendar "Aban
1405" over a November grid; Arabic and Hebrew notes show their closing
punctuation at the start; and the lock screen ignores every comfort choice.
Most worth doing first are large text that leaves room for the words (B-A-1),
and dates and words that read the right way in any language or script (B-A-2,
B-A-3).

| Id | Improvement | Why it helps | How | Effort | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B-A-1 | **Large text that leaves room for the words, and one size beyond Largest.** Text size gains "Very large" (150 %). From Larger up, or whenever the phone's own setting makes text 1.25 times the usual, the header scrolls away with the page, its three pills keep to one line, long lines wrap, and a control reached by keyboard or switch scrolls clear of the header and dock. | In the Android app the WebView multiplies the in-app size by the phone's own ([up to 200 % on Android 14](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/14/summary), [through `textZoom`](https://startdebugging.net/2026/09/fix-flutter-text-renders-off-screen-in-an-android-webview-with-font-scaling/)): at 360 × 780 px, Largest with 200 % leaves the header and dock 57 % of the height (22 % at Comfortable), and `.app-shell`'s `overflow: clip` (`theme-aurora.css:151–157`) cuts text off with no scrollbar on the Garden, Today, Care, the Guide and Settings' own size choices. Even at Comfortable, 4 of Today's 49 Tab stops sit wholly under the header or dock ([WCAG 2.4.11](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/focus-not-obscured-minimum.html)), and an iPhone Home Screen app cannot go past 125 %, because WebKit keeps 1rem at 16 px whatever Dynamic Type says ([bug 260880](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=260880); [Roselli](https://adrianroselli.com/2026/02/honoring-mobile-os-text-size.html)). [Apple's Larger Text criteria](https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/manage-app-accessibility/larger-text-evaluation-criteria) and WCAG's [1.4.4](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/resize-text.html) and [1.4.10](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/reflow.html) ask for 200 % without loss, scaling the content before the navigation. | `lib/textSize.ts` gains the 150 % entry and sets `data-text-size="device"` when the root computes 20 px or more with no in-app size; the rules (a static header, pills capped with `min()`, wrapping on `.care-stage-card`, `.toggle-row`, `.garden-stats` and the rest) live in a lazy `src/largeText.css`, and only a `scroll-padding` line for the header and dock joins the first load ([C43](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Techniques/css/C43)), shared with B-H-1. An e2e sweep at 360 px for 125 %, 200 % and 250 % checks for text past the edge, bars under 30 % of the height at 200 %, and no covered Tab stop. Adds optional `profile.textSizeBeyond: 'very-large'` beside `textSize: 'largest'`, so an older build opens at Largest; about 80–110 B gzip of first load, after WP-F 3 (F-30). | M | Now |
| B-A-2 | **Dates in the garden's English, in the gardener's own order and calendar.** A German or British gardener sees "Tuesday, 17 November 2026" and 18:05, an American "Tuesday, November 17, 2026" and 6:05 PM, and a Persian, Arabic or Thai phone the Gregorian month its grid draws, in Western digits. | Dates in 13 files (`lib/date.ts`, `MonthPlanner.tsx`, `EarlierCopies.tsx` and others) and counts in 3 more are formatted in the device's language inside English sentences (D-28), so a German phone heads an entry "Dienstag, 17. November 2026", which a screen reader reads with English rules ([WCAG 3.1.2](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/language-of-parts.html)), and counts "2.000" sightings. On a Persian phone the weather calendar is titled "Aban 1405" (the Solar Hijri calendar) over a Gregorian November grid, with the entry beneath dated in a third form; a Thai phone would title it 2569. That mismatch is a defect rather than a preference, and WP-D could carry it. | One small `lib/dateWords.ts` builds every formatter as `'en-'` plus the device's region (from `new Intl.Locale(navigator.language).maximize()`, checked with `supportedLocalesOf`, falling back to `'en'`), with `calendar: 'gregory'`, `numberingSystem: 'latn'` and the device's own hour cycle ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Intl/DateTimeFormat/DateTimeFormat)). The call sites, `lib/weatherCalendar.ts` and the readable exports switch to it; the week's first day stays with B-T-8 and B-J-10. Tests pin `en-DE`, `en-US` and `en-IR`, which also helps WP-H 3 (F-41). No stored change; about neutral on the first load. | M | Now |
| B-A-3 | **The gardener's own words sit the right way round, in any script.** An Arabic, Hebrew, Persian or Urdu note aligns right with its punctuation at the end, in the field while typing and wherever it is shown later, names in sentences such as "Dear {name}," included. The English around it is unchanged. | No element in `src` sets `dir`, so on a `lang="en"` page every text field and every displayed note runs left to right: an Arabic check-in note shows its "!" at the far right, the start of the sentence for its reader, and a Hebrew reflection shows its "?" first while it is typed. The W3C advises [`dir="auto"` on forms and inserted text](https://www.w3.org/International/questions/qa-html-dir) and [`<bdi>` for names inside sentences](https://www.w3.org/International/articles/inline-bidi-markup/); this is private writing in the gardener's own words, where direction matters most. | Add `dir="auto"` to every text input, search field and textarea (`TodayView.tsx:536, :899`, `GoalEditor`, `EarlierEntry`, `MoonlightRecap`, `ComfortPlanView`, `SightingForm`, Settings' names, Care's name field), pinned by a unit test per form. One `.their-words { unicode-bidi: plaintext; text-align: start }` class ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/unicode-bidi)) goes on displayed words outside the Journal, which B-J-7 covers with its copies, and names in English sentences get `<bdi>`. The interface itself is not mirrored, which only makes sense with a translated one (D-28). No stored change; about 10–20 B of first load. | M | Now |
| B-A-4 | **Clearer colours, chosen or asked for by the device.** The Comfort card (B-A-6) gains "Clearer colours". With it on, or when the device asks for more contrast, text reads at 7:1 or better, every field, card and button edge at 3:1 and 2 px, cards are solid rather than glass, and night becomes a true dark with light text. | No stylesheet has a `prefers-contrast` rule, so iOS's Increase Contrast ([Safari 14.5+](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/688706)) and the same request from [Chrome and Android WebView 96+](https://web-platform-dx.github.io/web-features-explorer/features/prefers-contrast) ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@media/prefers-contrast)) change nothing; a text field's only edge measures 1.34:1 by day and 1.68:1 at night, under [WCAG 1.4.11](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-contrast.html)'s 3:1, which is likely a WP-D defect in its own right. In [WebAIM's low-vision survey](https://webaim.org/projects/lowvisionsurvey2/), 51.4 % of respondents use some high-contrast mode, and 71.2 % of those who do choose light text on dark, so night needs a clear variant as much as day. | A third token layer: `.contrast-more` on `.app-shell` beside `theme-*` (`App.tsx:698`), and `@media (prefers-contrast: more)` with the same values, redefine `--ink`, `--muted`, `--edge`, `--line`, the glass tokens and `--ring-focus` for day and night in a lazy `src/contrast.css`. A unit test checks every token pair with the existing `lib/contrast.ts` (used only by tests today), and an e2e sweep under `emulateMedia({ contrast: 'more' })` runs axe's [enhanced contrast](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-enhanced.html) rule. Adds optional `profile.contrast: 'more'`; about 60 B of first load. | M | Next |
| B-A-5 | **Heard at the right moment: one sentence per tap, counts said as words.** Finishing a goal is spoken once, saying what happened in the garden ("Drink water marked done. Sunlight helped Milkweed 1 grow."), counts are read as "1 of 5 Sunlight today", and a celebration stays long enough to read, pauses under the pointer or focus, and waits its turn. | One tap can speak in two live regions at once, the Undo toast's and the celebration's (`UndoToast.tsx:40`, `Celebrations.tsx`), which [Sara Soueidan](https://sarasoueidan.com/blog/accessible-notifications-with-aria-live-regions-part-2) warns "may interfere with each other", and neither says what the care did: the growth step is shown, never spoken. Today's meter and the garden's two containers put `aria-label` on plain `div`s (`TodayView.tsx:427–430`, `GardenView.tsx:396–424`), which [ARIA 1.2 does not allow](https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#generic), so "0 / 5" is read as "zero slash five", and a celebration leaves after a fixed 5 s (`CELEBRATION_MS`) however long its sentence ([WCAG 4.1.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/status-messages.html)). | `lib/announcer.ts` gains `say(text)` with a short queue, and `Celebrations.tsx` becomes the one polite voice; `UndoToast` keeps its visible toast but speaks through `say()`, so its Undo button leaves the live region, and `gardenEvents` gains a spoken-only `plant-grew` event that adds no count on a gentle day (D-35). `Spoken` turns "0 / 5" into "0 of 5", the containers get `role="group"`, and a toast lasts `min(12000, 4000 + 60 × length)` ms. Arrival on a page is B-H-6's, though [Gatsby's tests with disabled users](https://www.gatsbyjs.com/blog/2019-07-11-user-testing-accessible-client-routing) found focusing the new heading worked best and helped magnifier users, so it is worth trying beside B-H-6's announcement. No stored change; about 120–180 B gzip of first load, so after WP-F 3. | M | Next |
| B-A-6 | **One place for comfort, and a lock screen at the gardener's size.** "How the garden looks" becomes "Comfort": theme, text size, motion, clearer colours and reading spacing, each applied at once. A line says when the device is asking for less motion, Calm stops the music button's bounce, and with the lock on an opt-in opens the lock screen at the gardener's size and colours. | Motion sits in another form and waits for "Save settings" (`SettingsView.tsx:761–791`), and when the device asks for less motion Settings still shows "Full garden motion" while the garden stands still. The lock screen is deliberately at the usual size and the device's light or dark (`LockScreen.tsx:38–44`), so a low-vision gardener types the passcode at the smallest size ([1.4.4](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/resize-text.html)), and every launch applies text size only once the garden has loaded (`App.tsx:483–487`), so pages at Larger jump. Calm leaves the music button bouncing forever once sound is on (`App.css:3083`), motion from an interaction that [WCAG 2.3.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/animation-from-interactions.html) says a gardener should be able to stop. | Move the motion fieldset into the appearance card with a `garden.setMotion` action beside `setTextSize` (`gardenActions.ts:735`), which also retires the stale-write path F-5 fixes; the device line reads `useMediaQuery('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')`. A pre-paint `lib/appearanceCache.ts` keeps the five choices in `localStorage`, as `lib/install.ts` keeps its flag, and `main.tsx` applies them to `<html>` before React renders, so nothing jumps and the splash gets its theme (F-27); under the lock it does so only with the opt-in. Onboarding's choices are B-H-5's. Motion stays in `profile.reducedMotion` and `calmMotion`; the cache is outside the garden and never in a backup; about 150 B gzip of first load. | M | Next |
| B-A-7 | **Forced colours that keep every choice visible.** In a Windows contrast theme the chosen mood, feeling, energy, Shop tab, filter chip, calendar day and companion keep a Highlight outline, the current life stage keeps its ring, and the weather pictures stay. | Forced colours drop shadows and gradient backgrounds ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@media/forced-colors)), and under emulation on a Pixel 7 the chosen mood differs from the rest by a hairline, the current Shop tab looks like every other, Care's life-stage dots vanish, and the weather pictures go. WP-D 6 (F-27) covers only the progress dots and the bond bar, and in [WebAIM's survey](https://webaim.org/projects/lowvisionsurvey2/) 30.6 % of respondents name high-contrast modes or settings among the tools they use most often (51.4 % use one at all). | One `@media (forced-colors: active)` block in B-A-4's lazy `contrast.css` gives `[aria-pressed="true"]`, `[aria-current]`, `[aria-selected="true"]`, `.selected` and `.active` a 3 px `Highlight` outline, and the decorative weather glyphs [`forced-color-adjust: none`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/forced-color-adjust). WP-H 2's forced-colours pass asserts a solid outline on each pressed or current control in Today, the Shop, Care and Journal search. CSS only, no stored change; lazy, or about 300 B in `App.css` once there is room. | S | Next |
| B-A-8 | **Reading spacing, not a special font.** Comfort gains "Reading spacing", described as "Some people find this easier to read": letters and words widen together, lines get more room, headings use the body's plain sans, nothing is italic, and eyebrows grow without spaced capitals. | Headings use Fraunces with tight tracking (`h2` at −0.035em), eyebrows are 10.9 px capitals at 0.17em (`garden-3.css:75–83`), and starred lines and "written later" marks are italic. A [2026 meta-analysis of 15 studies (N = 688)](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11881-026-00389-8) found dyslexia-friendly fonts had no effect (g = −0.04) and [OpenDyslexic gave no gain](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5629233/), while extra letter spacing helped dyslexic children read faster and more accurately ([Zorzi et al. 2012](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3396535/)), wider letter spacing without wider word spacing slowed reading ([Galliussi et al. 2020](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7188700/)), and italics read worse ([Rello and Baeza-Yates 2013](https://doi.org/10.1145/2513383.2513447)). [WCAG 1.4.12](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/text-spacing.html)'s values applied to six views at 360 px clipped nothing, so the layout already takes them. | A `.reading-spacing` class on `.app-shell`, with rules in a lazy `src/readingSpacing.css`: `--font-body` for headings, `letter-spacing: 0.12em`, `word-spacing: 0.16em`, `line-height` at least 1.5, `font-style: normal`, and eyebrows at 0.8rem or more; the Fraunces italic files then never load. A unit test for the class and an e2e clipping sweep with it on. Adds optional `profile.readingSpacing: true`; about 40 B of first load. | S | Next |
| B-A-9 | **"Describe my garden", for gardeners who cannot see it.** Under the garden's line, a button reveals a few sentences of what is drawn: "Evening in Woodland Brook. Your milkweed and aster are in full bloom. Sol, your monarch caterpillar, rests on the milkweed. Butterflies are visiting, and fireflies are out." Words, not scores. | A blind gardener meets the hero, the garden's main reward, as a row of "View Milkweed, growth 2 of 3" buttons and moving "Meet {name}" buttons, with none of the scene, season or night a sighted gardener takes in at a glance ([WCAG 1.1.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-content.html)). The line under the garden's name is already built from the garden's state, in a lazy chunk. | A pure `lib/gardenDescription.ts` beside `lib/gardenLines.ts`, from the state, theme, season, hemisphere and motion, rendered in the existing lazy `GardenLine` chunk (`GardenView.tsx:383–391`), with no numbers on a gentle day or when day counts are hidden. It builds on B-A-5's `role="group"` and says only what is drawn, so it follows D-17 (and B-G-2) whichever way that is decided. No stored change; lazy, 0 B of first load. | M | Later |

**Considered, and not recommended here.**

- **A dyslexia font (OpenDyslexic, Dyslexie).** The 2026 meta-analysis found no
  effect, and it would add font bytes; B-A-8 gives the spacing the evidence
  supports.
- **Following the phone's text size directly.** `<meta name="text-scale">`
  (Chrome 146, WebView included) would stack on the Android app's `textZoom`
  and could scale a set size twice, and `font: -apple-system-body` grows only
  body text while WebKit keeps 1rem at 16 px; revisit the first after B-A-1,
  tested on Android 14 at 200 %.
- **An accessibility overlay or toolbar.** Overlays duplicate the operating
  system's own features and are [widely criticised](https://overlayfactsheet.com/);
  the garden's settings do the same job privately.
- **Night for the browser's own pickers, here.** It is WP-D 7 (F-46). The
  study adds the detail: at night `html` and `.app-shell` compute
  [`color-scheme`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color-scheme)
  `normal`, so four date pickers, 29 selects and the scrollbar open white, and
  `paintBrowserBars` (`lib/theme.ts:128`) should set the root too.
- **W-1's spoken names, here.** It is WP-D 8. axe-core 4.14.1, injected without
  installing it, found the header pills pass ([WCAG 2.5.3](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/label-in-name.html);
  [the rule](https://dequeuniversity.com/rules/axe/4.14/label-content-name-mismatch)):
  the failures are "Try on" named "Preview {item}" (`ShopItemCard.tsx:68`, 27
  on 12 phone views), the brand button on every page at 1,280 px, Earlier
  copies' "Download as it is" and the jars' "Place", so WP-D 8 should start there.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-71.** Should dates show English words in the gardener's regional
  order (B-A-2), or keep the device's language and mark each date with its own
  `lang`? Recommended: English words, which follows D-28's recommendation and avoids
  mixed-language sentences; either way the Gregorian calendar and Western
  digits are needed so the month title matches its grid.
- **D-72.** With the lock on, may five appearance choices (text size,
  contrast, spacing, motion, theme) be kept outside the seal, so the lock
  screen opens at the gardener's size and colours (B-A-6)? Recommended: yes,
  as an opt-in, off by default; none of them reveals what the gardener wrote.

---

## (c) Major New Capabilities

Section (a) makes what exists correct, and section (b) makes it kinder. This
section asks what the garden should be able to do that it cannot do today. It
answers with the same care. Each capability was researched against three
things: the code it would touch, the platforms as they stood on
17 November 2026, and the evidence that it would help a gardener. Each comes
with a design, what it stores, a build order, its tests, its risks and the
owner's questions.

### c.0 How these were chosen, and what they cost

The candidates came from four places:

- **Gaps no fix or improvement can close.** These came from the code readers'
  notes:
  - A deleted entry cannot come back once the morning's copy has moved on.
  - The Android app makes no automatic copy at all.
  - A kept backup file pauses while the lock is on (F-16).
  - A gardener cannot keep one journal across two devices.
- **What section (b) handed on.** Its areas passed along "a new capability for
  (c)": a bin for deleted entries, one entry back from an earlier copy, and
  merging gardens.
- **Earlier plans.** These include the first Voyager's (c) list, the owner's
  unbuilt items (W-n) and the content packs of D-46.
- **What platforms newly allow.** Examples are on-device speech in Chrome 139
  and Firefox 160, widget discovery on Play, the Keystore's biometric-bound
  keys and Health Connect's mindfulness record.

Eleven candidates were researched in depth, each by its own study, and every
claim about the code was checked against it. Four more ideas came up along the
way. Those four and two of the eleven are answered in c.8 as "not
recommended". The rest are c.1 to c.7.

Every capability keeps (b)'s rules (nothing lost, gentle, private, lazy, and
the owner's decisions stand), and four more:

- **Nothing leaves the device** (invariant 8). Each works with no server,
  account or analytics. A platform may still reach out because of it: a
  speech pack downloads, or a backup lands in a cloud folder the gardener
  picked. That is named, and put to the owner.
- **No new store and no version raise.** Every design fits
  `DATABASE_VERSION` 8 and state version 8 with optional fields, so D-15's
  version 9 stays free. Each says what older builds do with what it stores.
- **After 4.0.** 4.0 is the Play submission, and nothing here goes into it.
  The groundwork that does, in (a)'s packages, is named in c.9.
- **Honest evidence.** No capability here has a trial showing it improves
  wellbeing. Where the evidence is about data loss, convenience or access, it
  says so. Where a study points the other way, it is quoted.

| | Capability | What it gives a gardener | Evidence | Recommendation | Effort | Android first load | Questions |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [c.1](#c1-recently-removed-and-one-entry-back-from-an-earlier-copy) | Recently removed | A deleted entry waits 30 days, and one entry can come back from an earlier copy without the rest | Convention and expert guidance; no trial, and some evidence for letting go | Build later, with (b)'s B-J-5 | L | About 120 B, estimated | D-73 to D-75 |
| [c.2](#c2-backups-that-keep-themselves-on-android-and-under-the-lock) | Backups that keep themselves | The Android app keeps a backup file up to date, and on every platform the file stays written, sealed, under the lock | Data-loss surveys, self-reported | Build later, once 4.0 is in production | L | +43 B, measured | D-76 to D-78 |
| [c.3](#c3-a-letter-to-open-later) | A letter to open later | Write to your future self, and Marigold brings it back on the day chosen | A keepsake; any lift is small, short and partly unreplicated | Build later, the first release after 4.0 | M | +104 B measured, plus 10 to 20 B estimated | D-79, D-80 |
| [c.4](#c4-a-garden-check-kept-copied-offline-locked-and-private) | A garden check | One look, on request: where the garden lives, the last copy, offline, the recovery code, and what the page fetched | Need indirect; the design follows studies of habituation and false reassurance | Build later, in two steps | M | 0 B, estimated | D-81, D-82 |
| [c.5](#c5-open-the-locked-garden-with-a-fingerprint-or-face-in-the-android-app) | Fingerprint or face, in the Android app | The locked garden opens with a strong biometric, through a key the phone's hardware holds | Lock usability studies; no wellbeing evidence | Build later, early 4.x | L | 0 B, estimated | D-83 to D-85 |
| [c.6](#c6-share-a-photo-to-the-field-book-and-a-field-book-that-travels) | Share a photo; a field book that travels | Share from the gallery straight into "Log a sighting", and save the field book as a CSV shaped for iNaturalist | Noticing nature: reasonable; that a share sheet helps is inferred | Build later, in three steps | L | 0 B, estimated | D-86, D-87 (and D-25) |
| [c.7.1](#c71-two-gardens-one-journal) | Two gardens, one journal | Another device's journal days and sightings come in, deletions honoured, the game untouched | Need described, not measured | Pilot | L | About 20 to 115 B, estimated | D-88, D-89 |
| [c.7.2](#c72-a-quiet-widget-for-the-android-app) | A quiet widget, in the Android app | A meadow with Check in and Breathe, and long-press shortcuts | Thin: one vendor figure and small, old studies | Pilot, on a testing track | M | 0 B, measured | D-90 |
| [c.7.3](#c73-speaking-instead-of-typing-on-the-device-only) | Speaking instead of typing | Dictation that never leaves the computer, in desktop Chrome and Firefox only | Ease and access; no wellbeing gain | Pilot, desktop web only | M | 0 B, estimated | D-91 |
| [c.8](#c8-considered-and-not-recommended) | Six ideas not recommended | Health Connect, computed observations, AI summaries, Argon2id now, passkeys in the app, notifications | Each turned down for a reason the owner can check | Do not build | — | 0 B | None new |

**The first load sets the order.** The Android build has about 245 B to spare
(a.2). The studies measured 234 B in their own scratch builds. If c.1, c.2,
c.3 and c.7.1 were all built, they would spend about 300 to 400 B. (b)'s
garden items (D-66) would spend another 230 to 310 B. WP-F 3's reclaim of
about 2.4 kB, which (a) places in 4.0, is what makes room. Each of these sets
the Android baseline on purpose under D-10. Everything else here costs 0 B on
Android, because it lives in lazy chunks or in Java.

### c.1 Recently removed, and one entry back from an earlier copy

**In one line.** A deleted check-in, reflection, recap or sighting (photo and all) waits 30 days in a quiet "Recently removed" place on this device, each one able to come back on its own, and an earlier daily copy can give back one entry without putting the whole garden back.

**Recommendation:** build later, after the 4.0 Play submission, with or just after (b)'s B-J-5 (Undo for Journal deletes), whose toast it extends · **Effort:** L · **First load:** about 120 B gzip on the Android build (`dist-android`), estimated by splicing the minified additions into today's chunks (60 B for the save guard and lazy import, 49 B for `changeEntries`, 13 B for two props). Everything else is lazy. The PWA build has about 800 B spare · **Waits on:** B-J-5, D-6 (new privacy wording), D-10 (an Android baseline set on purpose), D-73 to D-75

**Why it matters.** Today a check-in, reflection or recap has no Undo. The Journal asks "Delete this check-in for good?" (`JournalView.tsx:573`) and the entry is gone from view. A crossed-out sighting has six seconds of Undo. After that, the only way back is to put a whole daily copy back, which also undoes everything else changed since that morning, though Earlier copies says it is there for "a wrong delete". A gardener who deleted the wrong day, or deleted something in a hard moment and wants it back a week later, has no calm way to bring back just that entry. The prompt is also not true: since 3.6 the morning's daily copy keeps the entry for up to seven days (F-38). The case for a bin rests on expert guidance and convention, not trials. Nielsen Norman Group advises systems to ["go to great lengths to provide undo"](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/confirmation-dialog/) rather than lean on confirmations. Thirty days is the shared convention of [Apple Photos](https://support.apple.com/en-us/124460), [Apple Notes](https://support.apple.com/guide/icloud/mm2f42f05cb9/icloud) and [Google Photos](https://support.google.com/photos/answer/6128858), and Android's [MediaStore has its own trash](https://developer.android.com/training/data-storage/shared/media) for user content. In [24 interviews after a breakup](https://eprints.lancs.ac.uk/id/eprint/60831/) (Sas & Whittaker, CHI 2013, qualitative), some people regretted deleting on impulse and others wanted to put disposal off until they were ready. Some evidence points the other way. People who threw away the paper they had written their anger on [returned to baseline anger, and those who kept it did not](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-57916-z) (Kanaya & Kawai, 2024, paper only), and [thoughts thrown away weighed less in later judgments](https://doi.org/10.1177/0956797612449176) (Briñol et al., 2013, a lab finding). No controlled study shows a bin helps wellbeing. So "Delete for good" stays one tap away at the moment of deleting. Section (b)'s B-J-5 gives every Journal delete six seconds of Undo; this gives the thirty days after it.

**Where it works.** Nothing new is asked of any platform: IndexedDB with Blob values in the existing `snapshots` store, AES-GCM through `repository/sealedStores.ts`, the `butterfly-garden-sync` channel and `crypto.randomUUID`. There is no new permission, dependency or request. The bin is exactly as durable as the garden, no more ([MDN, checked November 2026](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria)).

- **PWA on iPhone.** From Safari 17 (iOS 17), a Home Screen web app gets the browser's origin quota ([WebKit](https://webkit.org/blog/14403/updates-to-storage-policy/)), and WebKit does not mean its 7-day cap on script-written storage to delete a Home Screen app's data ([WebKit](https://webkit.org/blog/10218/full-third-party-cookie-blocking-and-more/)). In a plain Safari tab that cap can clear the garden and its bin together. In Private Browsing a photo Blob may not store ([WebKit bug 268037](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=268037)), which the existing "not kept" note covers (D-9).
- **Android Chrome and desktop browsers.** Chromium allows an origin up to 60 % of the disk, Firefox 10 % or 10 GiB best-effort, and Safari 17 and later about 60 % ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria)).
- **The Android app.** The bin lives in the WebView's IndexedDB with the garden. Cloud backup stays excluded (`data_extraction_rules.xml`), phone-to-phone transfer carries it as it carries daily copies, and clearing the app's storage or uninstalling removes it ([Capacitor](https://capacitorjs.com/docs/guides/storage)). No `isAndroidApp()` branch is touched, so the Gradle checks are not needed, but the `android-webview` e2e project runs the flow.

**What the gardener sees.** It lives at the very end of the Journal, and in Settings' Earlier copies.

1. **Deleting in the Journal.** B-J-5 has made Delete act at once, with the shared Undo toast. Its words gain one sentence: "Your check-in for 3 November was deleted. It is in Recently removed. Undo", for six seconds, and focus moves to the day's heading. Beside Delete, the entry's own actions gain **Delete for good…**, which asks once: "Delete this check-in for good? It will not wait in Recently removed." If B-J-5 has not shipped, Delete keeps a prompt instead, with three buttons: **Delete**, **Delete for good** and **Keep**.
2. **Crossing out a sighting.** The field book's toast keeps its Undo and adds "It is in Recently removed for 30 days". The photo goes with it.
3. **Recently removed.** A plain text button after "Readable copies", with no count, opens: "Entries you deleted wait here for 30 days, then go for good the next time you use the garden. They stay on this device and are never in a backup." Each row shows only its kind and day ("Check-in · Tuesday 3 November", "Removed 5 November"). The words and photo wait behind **Show words**. Each row offers **Bring back** and **Delete for good**, with "Delete all of these for good…" at the foot. Every accessible name begins with the visible words, so W-1's label-in-name rule already passes.
4. **Bringing back.** The entry returns to its own day with its feelings, energy, star and "written later" mark, and a sighting with its photo. Focus moves to it: "Your check-in for 3 November is back." It earns nothing, because the Sunlight ledger already holds that day. If the day has a newer check-in, its words are shown to copy instead: "3 November has a check-in now, so this one cannot go back beside it." If the field book already keeps 50 photos, it offers **Bring back without its photo**.
5. **One entry from an earlier copy.** Building on (b)'s B-S-2, which compares a copy with the garden here by entry id, the copy preview gains **Bring back just one entry**: "Only this entry comes back. The rest of your garden stays as it is." It lists what the copy holds and the garden no longer has. A sighting comes back without its photo, since daily copies keep none. If the same entry is in Recently removed, the row points there instead.
6. **Under the lock**, all of this shows only while the garden is unlocked. Nothing in it is a form, so a lock loses nothing.

**What it stores, and what older builds do.**

- **Shape.** No new store. `DATABASE_VERSION` and `CURRENT_STATE_VERSION` both stay 8. Each removed entry is one record in the existing `snapshots` store: `{ id: 'removed-<entry id>', reason: 'removed', takenAt, bytes, garden }`. Its `garden` is deliberately not a garden: `{ format: 'the-butterfly-garden-removed', formatVersion: 1, gardenVersion: 8, kind, <kind>: [entry] }`, with no `version`, so `validateGarden` refuses it in every build and none can ever put it back as a whole garden.
- **The write.** The record goes in the same transaction as the save that removed the entry, after the same revision and lock checks. It is sealed before the transaction opens, as `prepareDaily` is, because a transaction [closes once control returns to the event loop](https://w3c.github.io/IndexedDB/). A conflict, a withheld garden or a garden deleted elsewhere writes neither. On a full disk `writeWithDaily` retries without it and the delete goes through as today. Riding the save means WP-B's baseline epoch guards it too (F-1).
- **Backups (invariant 6).** Unchanged. Export carries `AppState` only, so the bin is never in a backup, and a restore never fills it. Bring back places the entry in `createEmptyState()` at its `gardenVersion` and runs `migrateState`, so an older entry is migrated forward. One from a newer version is refused ("It was removed in a newer version of the garden") and left in place.
- **Older builds.** 3.6 to 4.0 read past a `removed` record: `describeCopies` lists only `daily` and `restore`, `snapshots()` filters `restore`, and `keepDaily` prunes only the `daily-` range. Their lock change opens and seals bin records like any snapshot, which is why the words live inside `garden`. 3.3 to 3.5 could reach one only after a clock went backwards, and then say it could not be read. No older build empties the bin. The first newer build to make a change does.
- **The lock.** `snapshots` is already in `SEALED_STORES`, so `sealGarden` seals the entry under its collection's label and a photo goes into `blobs`. Expiry needs no key, since `takenAt` is in the clear, but runs only inside an unlocked, checked write.
- **Two tabs.** The existing `garden-saved` message is enough, and an open list re-reads on the new garden identity. Bring back deletes `removed-<id>` in its own save, without reading it first. A second tab bringing back the same entry finds it present and changes nothing, as `restoreGoal` does.
- **Limits.** A record expires 30 days after `takenAt`. It is pruned in `keepDaily` with the day's first change, and hidden from the list at once. Caps of 100 entries, 50 photos and about 20 MB drop the oldest first, never the entry just removed (D-75).

**Privacy and safety.** Nothing leaves the device. The bin is not in backups, readable exports (Markdown, print, CSV), journal search, On this day, the weekly letter, the year or the postcard, and nothing resurfaces it unasked. It does go wherever "everything stored" goes, as daily copies do today: safe mode's "Download what's stored" (`lib/storedCopy.ts`), the moved-address banner's stored copy, and Android's phone-to-phone transfer. "Delete all local data" removes it with the garden (invariant 9). Data kept only on the device is [not "collected" for Play's Data safety form](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10787469), so the Play declarations (P-4) do not change. Under the lock, the kind, day, words and photo are all inside the seal, and the key carries only a random id from `lib/id.ts`. `takenAt` stays readable: one more fact for the privacy policy's "what stays readable" list (D-6). Without the lock, anyone holding the unlocked device can read the bin, as they can the Journal. So it stays quiet, with no count and the words hidden, much as [Apple puts Recently Deleted behind Face ID](https://support.apple.com/en-us/124460). Expiry is described gently, never as a countdown. There are no badges or reminders, and bringing back or deleting never earns or costs Sunlight.

**How to build it.**

1. Ship the honest wording first. It is already WP-A 10 (F-38): the prompts in `src/components/JournalView.tsx` say earlier copies keep the entry for up to a week, and `JournalView.test.tsx:100` changes with them. It needs no bin.
2. `src/repository/gardenRepository.ts`: add `'removed'` to `SnapshotRecord['reason']`, export `REMOVED_PREFIX`, and add a lazy `recentlyRemoved` import beside `dailyCopies`. In `save()`, after `copyBeforeToday`, one guard: when a collection's length differs, `aside()` takes the day's `PreparedDaily` and returns one of the same shape, so `withDaily` and `writeWithDaily` stay as they are. `replace()` does not change. (A hook the lazy module registers would cost 19 B, not 60 B, but a removal from a view that never loaded it would skip the bin.)
3. New `src/repository/recentlyRemoved.ts` (lazy): `KEEP_DAYS = 30` and the caps; `aside()`, which diffs ids per collection, seals removed entries with `sealGarden` and turns returned ids into deletes; a `letGo` set that "Delete for good" fills first; `pruneRemoved()`, by requests alone; and `openRemoved()`, which validates through `migrateState`.
4. `src/repository/dailyCopies.ts`: `keepDaily` also prunes the `removed-` range, and `describeCopies` returns the removed entries, with their bytes kept apart from the copies' storage line. In `src/repository/sealedStores.ts`, `SnapshotSealedError` names Recently removed too.
5. New `src/lib/recentlyRemoved.ts` (lazy, pure): `bringBack()` returns a garden or a reason (a day already holding that kind, a full field book, an id already present), reuses `restoreSighting` and never calls `awardSunlight`. `missingFrom(copy, now)` serves the earlier-copy list, and the words use `formatJournalDate`.
6. `src/hooks/useGardenState.ts`: `changeEntries()` beside `changeSightings`, keeping only moods, reflections, recaps and sightings from its result. `src/App.tsx` passes it and `onListEarlierCopies` to `JournalView`, and `onChangeEntries` to `SettingsView`.
7. `src/components/JournalView.tsx`: B-J-5's toast words, **Delete for good…** beside Delete (or the three-button prompt, if B-J-5 has not shipped), and a lazy section after `<JournalExport>`. New `src/components/journal/RecentlyRemoved.tsx`, styled in `journal.css`, deletes for good through the existing `discardSnapshot(id)`. `src/components/sightings/FieldBook.tsx` gets its toast's new words.
8. New `src/components/backup/CopyEntries.tsx` (lazy) in the copy preview of `src/components/SettingsView.tsx`, and one line in `src/components/backup/EarlierCopies.tsx`.
9. Words and docs: a concern-table row and notes under invariants 6 and 9 in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `public/privacy.html` (for approval under D-6), the Guide's backup section and `src/data/releases.ts`. Then run `npm run build`, `npm run build:android` and `npm run budgets` on both, and set the Android baseline on purpose (D-10).

**How it is tested.** A new `src/repository/recentlyRemoved.test.ts` pins the record written in the save's own transaction, none written on a conflict, a withheld garden or a delete elsewhere, and `migrateState` refusing the record both plain and sealed. Tests on the paths older builds share (`describeCopies`, `snapshots()`, `keepDaily`'s range, `gardenSwitch.test.ts`) show none of them drops or unseals a bin record. `src/lib/recentlyRemoved.test.ts` checks that an entry returns whole and that the Sunlight ledger, Nectar, Stardust and rhythm are identical before and after. `twoTabs.test.ts` brings the same entry back from two tabs, and component tests cover focus and accessible names. The e2e specs (`app`, `lock`, `nature`, `android`) delete, open, bring back, repeat under the lock after a reload, and run axe on the open section. One more test pins that every reducer that drops an entry shortens its collection.

**Risks, and what keeps them small.**

- **First-load room.** About 120 B of the 234 B the Android first load had spare in this research's build (a.2 measured 245 B), which 4.0's P-7, P-8 and P-23 may need first. Building after 4.0, once WP-F 3 has freed about 2.4 kB, and with a baseline set on purpose (D-10), keeps it small.
- **Letting go.** A 30-day bin may blunt a deliberate act of letting go. "Delete for good" in the prompt, hidden words, no counts and no resurfacing soften it but do not remove it, and with no analytics there is no way to measure how it lands.
- **"For good" that keeps a week.** Unless "Delete for good" also clears the entry from daily and pre-restore copies (D-74), WP-A 10's wording must stay.
- **Detection by length.** The guard fires only when a collection's length changes. A reducer that removed one entry and added another in one change would skip the bin, so a test pins that none does.
- **A damaged sealed record.** It makes a lock change refuse with `SnapshotSealedError`. The new build lists the record and can discard it, but a 3.6 to 4.0 build points to Earlier copies, where it is not listed. It needs ciphertext damage, which is rare.
- **Storage.** Up to about 20 MB beside the daily copies' about 24 MB. On a full disk the delete goes through without its bin record, and the list should say so for that session.
- **Older builds and two tabs.** An old install never empties the bin. Tabs share one bin, as they share one drafts record (D-34), and carry the same delete-all gaps as the save (D-32, D-33).
- **Scope.** Goals keep their six-second Undo. A deleted goal releases its companion (`releaseGoal`), which a bin cannot undo honestly.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-73.** Build Recently removed after the 4.0 submission, for check-ins, reflections, recaps and sightings, at the end of the Journal only, spending about 120 B of the Android first load under a baseline set on purpose (D-10), with new privacy wording approved afresh (D-6)? *Recommendation:* yes, with or just after B-J-5's Undo. Goals keep their Undo.
- **D-74.** Should "Delete for good" (and "Delete all of these for good") also remove the entry from the daily and pre-restore copies, so "for good" is literally true? Or should the prompt keep WP-A 10's words, that earlier copies keep it for up to a week? *Recommendation:* either is honest. The research does not lean, and this is the owner's call.
- **D-75.** Keep entries 30 days, as Apple and Google do, with caps of 100 entries, 50 photos and about 20 MB, oldest first? And is it always on, or on by default with a Settings switch, "Keep deleted entries for 30 days" (an optional profile field that older builds write back whole)? *Recommendation:* 30 days and those caps. A switch is optional, and if it exists it starts on.

### c.2 Backups that keep themselves, on Android and under the lock

**In one line.** Bring the desktop's backup file kept up to date to the Android app through Android's own save screen, and on every platform keep that file written while the lock is on, as the sealed copy safe mode already makes, instead of pausing it.

**Recommendation:** build later, once 4.0 is in production (P-22): the desktop sealed-under-the-lock half first, then the Android twin · **Effort:** L · **First load:** +43 B gzip on the Android build, measured in a prototype (234 B spare when measured; a.2 found about 245 B); 0 B on the web build · **Waits on:** D-6 (new privacy wording), D-10 (spending first-load bytes on purpose), D-76 to D-78

**Why it matters.** On Android a journal has no automatic copy anywhere. The app turns Android's cloud backup off on purpose (`android/app/src/main/res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml`, `backup_rules.xml`), so uninstalling, "Clear storage", a WebView storage fault or a lost phone takes the garden with it unless the gardener made a backup by hand. The only help is the monthly reminder (`lib/backupReminder.ts`). On desktop the gap is quieter: a gardener who keeps a file and then turns the lock on is left with a plain copy from before the lock, going stale (F-16). WP-C 5 only names that file; this closes it. The evidence is about data resilience, not wellbeing. A CHI 2025 survey of 1,423 people in Germany, the UK and the USA found 46 % had lost data; of the 656 who recovered, 42 % used backups, but over half of those backups were outdated or incomplete, and recovery was most distressing for exactly those people ([Wunder et al.](https://doi.org/10.1145/3706598.3714202)). The authors recommend backups that run by themselves, with a reminder only where none is active ([author manuscript](https://strathprints.strath.ac.uk/92011/1/Wunder-etal-ACM-CHI-2025-Achieving-resilience-data-loss-and-recovery.pdf)). That is self-reported and cross-sectional, and the recommendation is untested. A vendor survey points the same way and is indicative only: in 2022, 10 % of US computer owners backed up daily ([Backblaze and The Harris Poll](https://backblaze.com/blog/the-2022-backup-survey-54-report-data-loss-with-only-10-backing-up-daily/)).

**Where it works.**

- **The Android app** (Capacitor 8.5.3, minSdk 24, targetSdk 36). The Storage Access Framework needs no permission and no manifest change. Its files stay on the device after the app is uninstalled, and a persisted grant lets the app keep writing one file ([Android Developers](https://developer.android.com/training/data-storage/shared/documents-files)). Play keeps All files access for apps that cannot use it ([Play Console Help](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10467955)). The grant survives restarts and updates. It is lost on uninstall, "Clear storage", a moved or deleted file, and a device-to-device transfer. The WebView has had `showSaveFilePicker` since Chrome 132, January 2025 ([New in Chrome 132](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/new-in-chrome-132)), but only where the host app handles the chooser ([blink-dev](https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/x3IcFv2jY6c/m/LUOy9WHHBQAJ)). Capacitor 8.5.3's `BridgeWebChromeClient` does not, and P-9's minimum WebView is 111, so the twin goes through `GardenFilesPlugin`, as one-off saves do.
- **Desktop browsers.** Chrome and Edge keep the file today (since 86), with "Allow on every visit" since Chrome 122 ([Chrome for Developers](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/persistent-permissions-for-the-file-system-access-api)). Firefox and Safari have no `showSaveFilePicker` as of November 2026 ([Can I use](https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_window_showsavefilepicker)), and their positions are negative and opposed ([web-features](https://web-platform-dx.github.io/web-features-explorer/features/file-system-access)). Only the lock half changes anything there.
- **Android Chrome (the PWA).** `canKeepBackupFile()` is a feature check, so today's setting very probably already shows there, outside the policy's "on a computer" and on content URIs that "do not support atomic writes" ([blink-dev](https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/x3IcFv2jY6c/m/HxuEfzBHAAAJ)). Not checked on a phone. It belongs on P-20 either way.
- **The PWA on iPhone and iPad.** Nothing, through Safari 27.x. The gardener keeps manual backups, Share backup and the reminder.

**What the gardener sees.** Settings, Backup and restore, the existing "Keep a backup file up to date" card (`AutoBackupSetting.tsx`), now in the Android app too. `AutoBackupKeeper.tsx` writes it while the garden is open.

1. Nothing chosen, on Android: "Choose a file once, and the garden writes a fresh backup to it after each day's changes and as you leave the app. It writes only while the app is open." Button: **Choose a backup file…**
2. Android's save screen suggests `butterfly-garden-backup.json`. Backing out changes nothing and says nothing. Choosing writes at once, and the card names the file Android actually made, since a clash gets a number added ([Android Developers](https://developer.android.com/training/data-storage/shared/documents-files)): "butterfly-garden-backup (1).json, in Downloads on this phone, was last written today at 2:05 pm."
3. A cloud place adds one line: "It is in Google Drive, so each fresh copy goes there too, and Drive keeps earlier versions for up to 30 days." A place that will not keep the grant: "That place does not let the garden keep writing to a file, so nothing was kept. A folder on this phone, such as Downloads or Documents, works well."
4. No prompts after that. It is written on a later day's first open, and as the app goes to the background at most every 10 minutes (`HIDDEN_WRITE_GAP_MS`), following both `visibilitychange` and the app's own state, as `LockWatch.tsx` does.
5. Trouble is said once, with one way forward: "Android no longer lets the garden write it: it may have been moved, or the app's storage cleared." with **Choose the file again…**, or "The last copy was cut short as the app closed, so it is written again now."
6. Turning the lock on with a file kept, on every platform, the setup's last step says: "Your backup file, butterfly-garden-backup.json, is sealed as soon as the lock is on: it will open with your passcode or recovery code." The card's "Automatic backups pause…" becomes: "While your garden is locked, this file is sealed too. It opens with your passcode or recovery code, here or on a new device. A sealed file is only as hard to open as your passcode is to guess, so a longer passcode keeps it safer if the file is ever copied."
7. After a new passcode or recovery code the file is rewritten at once. If it cannot be: "butterfly-garden-backup.json still opens with your previous passcode or recovery code." with **Write it again now**. Turning the lock off writes an ordinary backup at once.
8. Restoring needs nothing new. Restore, "I have a backup", recovery and safe mode already recognise a sealed copy, and `LockedCopyPrompt` asks for the passcode or recovery code.

**What it stores, and what older builds do.** Only the drafts-store record at `BACKUP_FILE_KEY` changes, by optional fields: `form?: 'sealed'` (absent means plain, which is what every existing record means), `lockStamp?`, `startedAt?` (Android: a write begun and not yet confirmed), and on Android a `{ kind: 'document', name, uri, place? }` handle in place of a `FileSystemFileHandle`. `DATABASE_VERSION` stays 8. No state version, garden field or `validateGarden` change. The record stays outside every backup and daily copy, and `repository/gardenSwitch.ts` already carries it unsealed across a lock switch.

- **Invariant 6.** The plain file is the unchanged export envelope. The sealed file is the existing `the-butterfly-garden-raw` copy (the lock record, meta and the collection stores), the form `MovedBanner`'s `lockedCopy()` and safe mode already write and `lib/lockedCopy.ts` reads. No new shape. Every build since `145557e` (27 October 2026) opens it. Older builds refuse it as not a backup: a refusal, never a silent loss.
- **Older builds and the record.** Web tabs of 3.6 to 4.x keep pausing under the lock. With the lock off they write a plain file and rebuild the record without `form`, which correctly reads as plain. The 4.0 Android app has no keeper and ignores the record.
- **The lock's sealing.** The copy is read raw after `gardenRepository.settled()` and never opened: the data key is never used for the file. It is made only while this tab has the garden unlocked, so the record's write passes the existing `lockIs` check. With the lock "locked" or "unknown" the keeper pauses, and a copy is refused while `persistence.writeError` is set, as `MovedBanner` refuses.
- **Cross-tab.** Writes still take `FILE_LOCK` and the shared `<database>-switch` Web Lock. Before committing, the writer re-reads the lock and aborts if its id or stamp moved (on Android before the target is opened, since a content URI has no atomic write).

**Privacy and safety.**

- Nothing new leaves the device through the app. The native code writes only the one document the gardener chose; the CSP, manifest and permissions are unchanged. Play counts neither on-device processing nor a transfer the gardener starts and expects as collection or sharing ([Play Console Help](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10787469?hl=en)).
- A cloud place uploads every write. The card names the places it recognises, and Drive's history, where a plain file later sealed can linger for 30 days or 100 versions ([Google Drive Help](https://support.google.com/drive/answer/2409045?hl=en)). Sealing does not erase earlier plain bytes, and the card says so once, when the lock goes on.
- **The honest limit.** The sealed file carries the passcode's way in. At OWASP's 600,000 rounds ([OWASP](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Password_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html)), one RTX 4090 tries about 14,800 passcodes a second ([hashcat benchmark](https://gist.github.com/Chick3nman/32e662a5bb63bc4f51b847bb422222fd)): every 6-digit passcode in about 70 s, offline (F-14 gives a faster card's figure: under a minute). Safe mode's copy and the moved-address file carry the same risk today, but once. A kept file, perhaps in a cloud folder, stands. WP-C 1's advice sits beside it, and D-76 asks whether to go further.
- **Guards.** `writeKeptDocument` refuses any URI not among `getPersistedUriPermissions()` with write access. A re-chosen file is written over only if it is empty or already a garden file (the first-256-bytes test `sealedKind` uses). SECURITY.md's promise that a new secret opens nothing written since is kept by the rewrite at once, and the status says when it is not.
- Free for everyone, outside the Garden Pass. No notifications, counts or "days since your last backup".

**How to build it.**

1. `android/.../KeptDocument.java` (new, plain Java, a JVM test like `DailyEvent`): problem kinds from exceptions (`ENOSPC` is full), place words from a URI authority, the persisted-grant check, the garden-file check, and the write modes in order: `"wt"`, then `"rwt"`, then `"w"` with an explicit truncate. That is WP-E 1's rule, since `"w"` need not truncate on Android 10 and 11 ([DuckDuckGo PR #4371](https://github.com/duckduckgo/Android/pull/4371)).
2. `GardenFilesPlugin.java`: `chooseKeptDocument` (`ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT` with the persistable flags, then `takePersistableUriPermission`, answering `{ uri, name, place }` or "refused"), `reopenKeptDocument`, `writeKeptDocument`, `checkKeptDocument` and `forgetKeptDocument`. The text arrives in WP-E 2's pieces and is held whole before the target is opened, then written off the main thread, flushed and its size checked. No manifest change.
3. `src/lib/keptDocument.ts` (new, Android only, behind an inline `VITE_PLATFORM` check and `inAppPlugin('GardenFiles')`), mapping each answer to `BackupFileProblem`.
4. `src/lib/sealedGardenCopy.ts` (new): `MovedBanner`'s `lockedCopy()` moved here unchanged, returning the lock id and stamp it carries. `MovedBanner.tsx` imports it, so the two files are one form by construction.
5. `src/lib/autoBackup.ts`: the Android handle, the optional fields, a pure `sealDue(record, lock)`, and the new status words. `src/lib/autoBackupFile.ts`: `canKeepBackupFile()` true in the Android build, Android calls routed to `keptDocument.ts`, `locked()` meaning "locked" or "unknown", the sealed copy under "unlocked" with `form` and `lockStamp` recorded, and `startedAt` around Android writes.
6. `AutoBackupKeeper.tsx`: pause on read-only, a write error, or "locked"/"unknown"; write at once on `sealDue` or a leftover `startedAt`; follow `onAppActiveChange`. `App.tsx`: widen the keeper's existing lazy import to the Android build (the 43 B).
7. `SettingsView.tsx` drops the Android exclusion and releases the grant in delete-all. `AutoBackupSetting.tsx` gets the words and actions. `LockSetup.tsx` and `LockChange.tsx` name the file; on desktop they ask the browser's permission inside the "Turn on the lock" tap, before the derivation. `gardenLockActions.ts`'s `lockGarden` writes a behind file, sealed, after `flushDrafts`, through a hook the keeper registers.
8. Docs: ARCHITECTURE's "desktop backup file" row, SECURITY.md's two passages, `public/privacy.html`'s two passages (for approval under D-6), `docs/play-store/README.md` (P-4, P-20) and `src/data/releases.ts`.
9. Ship it as two runs, the desktop half (steps 4 to 8 without Android) first, each with CLAUDE.md's four checks, the budgets and knip, and the Android checks for the second.

**How it is tested.** Unit: `sealDue`'s table; under "unlocked" the file satisfies `sealedKind(text) === 'copy'` and `readLockedCopy(text).ok` and holds no journal words; nothing is written under "locked" or "unknown"; a lock change between making and committing aborts; a round trip opens with the passcode and with the recovery code into the plain export's garden, photos included; the moved-address file is byte-for-byte unchanged. The existing "paused while locked" tests are rewritten on purpose, not deleted. JVM: `KeptDocumentTest` (exception mapping, the grant check refusing any other URI, the garden-file check, the mode order). E2e: `android.spec.ts` with the `withGardenFiles` stand-in, axe in each state; `keep.spec.ts` from plain to sealed, restored in a fresh context through "I have a backup", and plain again; `lock.spec.ts` for "Lock now". `npm run budgets` on `dist-android`. Then the owner's devices under P-20: an Android 14+ phone and an older one; Downloads, Documents, a memory card and Google Drive; a reboot, a reinstall, "Clear storage" and a device transfer.

**Risks, and what keeps them small.**

- **A standing sealed file guessed offline.** Gardeners may take it as strongly protected. The card's words, WP-C 1's advice, and D-76.
- **Torn files on Android.** Content URIs have no atomic replace, and Android 14+ freezes a cached app 10 s after it is cached ([AOSP](https://source.android.com/docs/core/perf/cached-apps-freezer)). Whole text first, explicit truncation, a size check, and `startedAt` to redo a cut-off write at the next launch. A kill mid-write can still tear it until then.
- **Providers vary.** Some refuse persistable grants, move files or upload lazily; Drive is unverified until P-20. Grants are capped at 512 from Android 11, by a secondary source ([CommonsWare](https://commonsware.com/blog/2020/06/13/count-your-saf-uri-permission-grants.html)), and this holds one, released on Stop.
- **A file only an old secret opens.** The status says so until it is rewritten.
