**How it is tested.** Unit: `sightingsCsv` with the notes choice (BOM, CRLF, the header in order, newest first, no "spp." left for any plant, notes only when asked, `=HYPERLINK` and a line break then `+1` guarded, no id, `createdAt`, base64 or `blob:`, geoprivacy "obscured"); `JournalExport` (the button only with sightings, the box unticked, Android's "Saved." and "Nothing was saved."); `shareTarget.test.ts` against a fake worker scope (GET ignored, the 303, the file only to its client, refusals answered "none", caches and IndexedDB never touched); `sharedPhotoWeb`; the manifest's `share_target`; `SightingForm`, `FieldBook` and `JournalView` (prepared on mount, the 50-photo line, "The photo was not kept.", a conflict). JVM: `SharedPhotoTest` refuses `file://`, this app's own authority, a missing grant, an unlisted type and a stream over 10 MB. E2e: `nature.spec.ts` posts a multipart JPEG to `share-photo` against the real worker on the preview build, saves it with an axe sweep, and repeats offline; a photo with GPS EXIF comes back out of a backup with no GPS marker; `lock.spec.ts` shares into a locked garden; `android.spec.ts` stubs `takeSharedPhoto`, and the budgets show 0 B on `dist-android`. Outside CI: a two-row CSV uploaded to the owner's own iNaturalist account, and on P-20's phones a share from Google Photos and from the camera, warm and cold, lock on and off.

**Risks, and what keeps them small.**

- **The worker reaches every installed copy.** A fault in the imported script could break offline launch for everyone. One POST-only listener, `generateSW` unchanged, and e2e of the offline and update flows.
- **Version skew.** The manifest could gain `share_target` before the new worker is active, and the gardener would meet GitHub Pages' error page. The handler ships a release first.
- **Uneven reach.** No share target on iPhone, iPad, Safari or Firefox, and no sign WebKit will add one. No copy may promise sharing "from any phone".
- **A share reloads an open PWA window.** An open form that is not a draft is lost, as on any navigation. Saved data is safe, since every change is written at once (invariant 4).
- **Confused-deputy URIs**, a real class of Android bug. `SharedPhoto`'s rules and their JVM tests, the app's own FileProvider included.
- **Memory.** A 10 MB photo crosses the bridge as about 13 MB of base64, the pressure F-10 describes. Test on P-20's older phone; WP-E 2's pieces are the pattern if it struggles.
- **Large and HEIC photos.** Until WP-F 6 (F-33) lands, a photo over 24 MP is refused, as from the file input. HEIC (some Samsung defaults) is not declared, matching what the web pipeline decodes.
- **After Save**, Back leaves through `leaveApp()`, which usually shows the gallery again. This varies by device.
- **Overselling the CSV.** Casual records, no grid references, mostly North American species. The words say so plainly.
- **iNaturalist changes its columns.** The header rests on users' reports. Check it before each release, as the nature-project links are (`NATURE_PROJECTS_CHECKED_ON`).
- **Process.** Inside 4.0 it would reopen P-4 and D-6 just before the closed test. Hence 4.1.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-25** (open, not new). Should readable exports include sightings? This CSV is such an export, and (b)'s B-J-3 asks the same for search and the Markdown and printable copies, and its "Field book as CSV" is this CSV: one button and one function, not two. The recommendation recorded there: "Search and exports: yes."
- **D-86.** Build it after 4.0 is submitted, in order: the Android share in 4.1, the CSV with B-J-3 (after D-25), then the PWA share target through `generateSW`'s `importScripts` in two releases, rather than an `injectManifest` migration at W-3's January 2027 review? Approve the one privacy-policy sentence above under D-6's process, and ship the share-sheet entry without a Settings switch, since a switch would need a stored setting? *Recommendation:* yes to all three.
- **D-87.** The CSV's defaults: notes left out unless ticked, geoprivacy "obscured", tags blank; and a two-row trial upload from the owner's own iNaturalist account, to confirm the columns and the date-only format, before release? *Recommendation:* yes. Prefilling the day from a photo's own capture date (read on the device; location still never read) and accepting HEIC through native transcoding are later, separate questions.

### c.7 Three pilots: two gardens one journal, a quiet widget, and speaking instead of typing

#### c.7.1 Two gardens, one journal

**In one line.** A second way to use a backup: instead of replacing this garden, bring in the check-ins, reflections, evening recaps and sightings that another device's garden holds and this one lacks, honour what was deleted on either side, and leave this garden's butterflies, plants, goals, currencies and settings exactly as they are.

**Recommendation:** pilot, after 4.0 is live (P-22), in two stages: deletion markers one release ahead with no new screen, then the merge behind a build-time flag on the owner's devices and Play internal testing, opened to everyone only once those hold · **Effort:** L · **First load:** about 20 to 115 B gzip on both builds (140 to 215 B if deletions are written into the reducers), estimated, not measured: a revision check in `gardenRepository.ts` (35 to 50 B), a merge mode in `usePersistence.ts` (15 to 25 B), and the recording of deletions (−30 to +40 B through one narrow hook, 90 to 140 B if written into the reducers). The merge itself is lazy. Android has about 234 B spare in the studies' builds (a.2 found about 245 B) until WP-F 3 frees about 2.4 kB · **Waits on:** D-88 and D-89; D-6 (one new policy sentence, approved afresh); D-10 (the bytes spent on purpose)

**Why it matters.** A gardener who checks in on a phone in the morning and writes the evening recap on a laptop keeps two journals. Restore can only replace one with the other ("Restoring replaces everything currently in this browser", `SettingsView.tsx:1302`), so whichever side has the newer writing loses it, and Move my garden is for leaving a device, not for keeping two. A merge gives them one journal with no server and no account, so search, On this day, Marigold's letters (`lib/letters.ts`) and the year look back read all of it. The need is well described but not measured here. In interviews with 27 people who use several devices, managing information across them was the hardest part ([Dearman and Pierce, CHI 2008](https://www.dgp.toronto.edu/~dearman/papers/2008-MultiDeviceComp-CHI.pdf)), and local-first software names "your work is not trapped on one device" as an ideal, noting that file sync leaves concurrent edits to be merged by hand ([Kleppmann et al., 2019](https://www.inkandswitch.com/essay/local-first/)). Journaling's own benefit is small to moderate (20 trials, a 5 % greater fall in symptom scores, high heterogeneity: [Sohal et al., 2022](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8935176/)), and no study measures what a merge adds. With no analytics, nobody knows how many gardeners use two devices. The gain is fewer lost words: plausible, not shown.

**Where it works.** Wherever Restore works today, as of 17 November 2026: the same file input, WebCrypto for protected backups and move files, IndexedDB and BroadcastChannel. `showOpenFilePicker` is not used, since Safari and Firefox lack it ([Can I use](https://caniuse.com/mdn-api_window_showopenfilepicker)). The gardener carries the file by Share where it exists (Safari on iOS 12.2+, Chrome for Android, most desktops) or as a download in desktop Firefox, which has no Web Share ([Can I use](https://caniuse.com/web-share)). Since Safari 17 a garden left in a plain Safari tab can be evicted ([WebKit](https://webkit.org/blog/14403/updates-to-storage-policy/)), so a merge rescues it only from a backup. Where Blobs are refused, in Safari Private Browsing (D-9, W-11), incoming photos are checked with `lib/photoCheck` first. In the Android app the WebView's file input goes through `onShowFileChooser` ([Android Developers](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/webkit/WebChromeClient#onShowFileChooser(android.webkit.WebView,%20android.webkit.ValueCallback%3Candroid.net.Uri[]%3E,%20android.webkit.WebChromeClient.FileChooserParams))), as Restore's does, and the file it carries away is saved by `GardenFilesPlugin`, so WP-E 1's truncating save and WP-E 2's pieces come first: a backup that kept an old file's tail would be refused on the other device. No new permission, plugin or manifest entry. Clock skew, iPhone Files and the Android save screen can only be seen on the owner's devices (P-20).

**What the gardener sees.** In Settings' Backup and restore card, beside "Restore from a backup", in both builds, and not while saving is paused: (1) **Bring in writing from another garden…** opens the same chooser under a calm note in place of Restore's red warning: "Choose a backup from your other device. Everything here stays as it is; only its journal days and sightings come in." A protected backup or a locked copy asks for its passphrase or code first. (2) A preview, "What comes in from {gardenName}", counts what is new and lists it by date and kind only, each day with "Leave this day out". Where both gardens have a check-in on one day, both notes show with "Keep the one written here" and "Keep the one from {other}", nothing preselected. "You deleted 4 entries the backup still holds. They stay deleted." is a count, never their words. "Your butterflies, plants, goals, Nectar, Stardust, settings and images stay as they are here." (3) **Bring these in** keeps the garden as it was aside, then says "The writing from {other} is in your journal now." with **Undo bringing it in**, and one next step: "To keep the same journal on your other device, save a backup here and bring it in there."

**What it stores, and what older builds do.** Two optional fields, no new store, no version raise. `Profile.letGo?: { id: string; on: string }[]` holds each deleted check-in, reflection, recap or sighting's random id and the local day it was let go, never a word, at most 500 with the oldest dropped. It sits in the profile, not in `META_FIELDS`, because an older build writes meta field by field and would drop it, but carries the profile through whole, as it does `comfortPlan`. `Sighting.updatedAt?` lets the newer edit win, falling back to `createdAt`. `DATABASE_VERSION` and `CURRENT_STATE_VERSION` stay 8, which leaves D-15's version 9 free, and invariant 6's envelope is untouched. The merge never leaves two check-ins, reflections or recaps on one day, because `migrateRecaps` in `validateGarden.ts` silently skips a second recap on a date, in every build. It writes through `replace` with a restore snapshot (invariant 5, `SNAPSHOT_LIMIT` 2) and a new `ifUnchanged` check that aborts with `GardenConflictError` if another tab wrote since: a restore may win over another tab, a merge must not (invariant 2). Under the lock, `replace` seals every part, markers included, and `garden-saved` brings other tabs along. Game state comes wholly from this garden, and brought-in entries earn nothing. A 3.x build restores a merged backup normally, but its own deletions write no marker.

**Privacy and safety.** Nothing leaves the device: the gardener carries the file as with any backup, and the step suggests a protected one. Play's Data safety answers do not change, since processing on the device is not collection ([Play Console Help](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10787469?hl=en)). The markers do show someone reading a plain backup that something was deleted on a given day. Under the lock they are sealed with the profile, they sit inside a protected backup's ciphertext, and the policy approved under D-6 needs one sentence saying so. The preview never shows the words of an entry that stays deleted and never brings one back unasked: digital traces can upset people who want to forget, and intentional forgetting has value ([Sas and Whittaker, CHI 2013](https://eprints.lancs.ac.uk/id/eprint/60831/4/Design_for_Forgetting_final.pdf)). A backup from a different garden (another `profile.createdAt`) is named plainly, so nobody folds in a friend's journal by accident. Nothing compares the two devices, no Sunlight moves, and the Pass and purchases are untouched (invariant 7).

**How to build it.**

1. Stage A, a release ahead: `Profile.letGo` and `Sighting.updatedAt` in `src/types.ts`; a lazy `src/lib/letGo.ts` with a defensive reader, a writer capped at 500 and a remover for Undo; `lib/sightings.ts` stamps `updatedAt`, and the field book's remove and Undo add and drop a marker.
2. Also in Stage A, route `deleteMood`, `deleteReflection` and `deleteRecap` through one narrow hook in `useGardenState.ts`, in the manner of `changeSightings`. Measure it on `dist-android` against writing markers in `gardenActions.ts`' reducers, and keep the smaller.
3. Stage B, behind a build-time flag: a pure, lazy `src/lib/gardenMerge.ts`. `planMerge(here, there)` lists additions, newer-wins replacements, same-day pairs and deletions on either side; `applyMerge` replaces collections (invariant 3) and returns `here` itself when nothing changes.
4. In `gardenRepository.ts`, `replace(…, { ifUnchanged })` compares the stored revision with `persistedRevision` inside `writeAllParts`' transaction, after WP-B has put that baseline behind one queue. In `usePersistence.ts`, `importGarden` gains a merge mode that skips `recordBackup` and adopts the stored garden on a conflict.
5. `previewMerge` in `lib/backupPreview.ts`, built on (b)'s B-S-2 comparison by entry id, a lazy `components/backup/MergePreview.tsx`, the Settings button, the Guide's "Two devices, one journal", `releases.ts`, ARCHITECTURE and, once approved, the policy sentence. Then CLAUDE.md's four checks and `node scripts/check-budgets.mjs --dist dist-android`, moving the baseline only on purpose (D-10).

**How it is tested.** `gardenMerge.test.ts`: new entries come in by id; a marker on either side keeps an entry out; two entries on one day never both survive, and `validateGarden` of the merged garden repairs nothing; game state is this garden's own objects; merging twice equals merging once, and A into B then B into A gives both the same journal; more than 2,000 sightings is refused before anything changes, and photos past 50 stay out and are counted. `twoTabs.test.ts` pins `ifUnchanged` against another tab's write, `lockedGarden.test.ts` that every part is sealed and Undo puts back the sealed garden, and a 3.9 backup fixture merges cleanly. `e2e/merge.spec.ts` makes two gardens in two browser contexts and brings one into the other, with axe on the step and the preview, and the same flow runs on `android-webview`.

**Risks, and what keeps them small.**

- **Lost words.** Device clocks disagree, so "newer wins" can keep the older edit ([Kingsbury](https://aphyr.com/posts/299-the-trouble-with-timestamps)). A same-day choice lets one entry go, kept only in the file and in an aside copy that a third restore pushes out. A bug that left two recaps on one date would lose the second silently in every build. Every replacement is listed in the preview, the one-a-day rule is pinned by test, and Undo is offered.
- **Resurfaced deletions.** Entries deleted before markers existed, or in an older build, can be offered again. A deletion always wins, as in a 2P-Set ([CRDT summary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conflict-free_replicated_data_type)), and markers a release ahead, the day list and Undo shrink the gap but cannot close it. A merge also goes one way, so the after-step and the Guide say plainly that it is done in both directions.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-88.** Pilot the merge after 4.0 is live: deletion markers and `Sighting.updatedAt` one release ahead with no new screen, then the merge behind a build-time flag on the owner's devices and Play internal testing; with one new privacy-policy sentence (the garden keeps the id and day of each deleted entry, never its words) approved beyond D-6, and about 20 to 115 B of the first load set on purpose under D-10? *Recommendation:* yes to each.
- **D-89.** The merge's choices: settle a same-day pair by a choice with nothing preselected (not "keep both"); keep game state wholly from this garden; keep an entry deleted here but changed there deleted, with "Bring it back" unticked; leave photos beyond the 50 a garden keeps in the file; allow a different garden's backup with a plain note; offer it from Settings only? *Recommendation:* yes to each, as worded.

#### c.7.2 A quiet widget for the Android app

**In one line.** A native home-screen widget in the Android app that shows the garden's own meadow painting and two gentle ways in, Check in and Breathe, with the long-press shortcuts the installed PWA already has; it reads and stores nothing from the garden, so the lock has nothing to hide.

**Recommendation:** pilot, as a 4.x update on a Play testing track after 4.0 is live (P-22), kept only if testers find it gentle; the opt-in picture of the gardener's own garden is not built unless the owner chooses it · **Effort:** M · **First load:** 0 B on both builds, measured: the widget, shortcuts and routing are Java and resources in the APK, and the `dist-android` first load stays at 169,011 of 169,245 B in the study's build (a.2 found about 245 B spare) · **Waits on:** D-90

**Why it matters.** The garden sends no notifications by design; the calendar does the reminding. A widget is a pull cue, not a push: it sits on the home screen, can be ignored, and one tap opens Today at its check-in question or breathing with a butterfly, which saves steps on a hard day. Android gardeners would also get the long-press shortcuts that installed-PWA gardeners have (`src/pwaManifest.ts`: Check in, Today, Journal), since `AndroidManifest.xml` declares none. The evidence is thin. Its headline is a vendor case study: the Gratitude journal reports "25% higher retention for widget users", with no definition, time window or comparison, among people who chose to add a widget, so it is a self-selected correlation, and retention is not wellbeing ([Android Developers Blog, May 2026](https://android-developers.googleblog.com/2026/05/how-gratitude-widgets-boosted-user-retention-25-percent.html)). The real precedent is UbiFit Garden, a garden on the phone's background screen: in a 3-month field experiment with 28 people over the winter holidays, those with the display kept up their physical activity while the others' fell ([Consolvo et al., UbiComp 2008](https://doi.org/10.1145/1409635.1409644)). It is small, from 2008 and about exercise. A glanceable entry on the unlock screen was rated less intrusive than reminders (1.77 against 2.22 of 5; 24 people, 18 days: [Zhang et al., CHI 2016](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5553980/)). With no analytics, only testers can say whether it helps.

**Where it works.** The Android app only. Classic `AppWidgetProvider` and `RemoteViews` widgets work on every version from minSdk 24; in-app pinning needs API 26 and a launcher that pins, and previews and the system corner radius need API 31 ([AppWidgetProviderInfo](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/appwidget/AppWidgetProviderInfo)). Jetpack Glance 1.2.0 is stable (26 August 2026) but needs Kotlin and the Compose compiler, and its 1.3 line AGP 9.2 ([release notes](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/glance)), against P-11's held Gradle 8 and AGP 8.13, so the widget is plain Java with no new dependency. Play has shown a widget badge and filter since March 2025 ([Android Developers Blog](https://android-developers.googleblog.com/2025/03/google-play-enhances-widget-discovery.html)), for widgets that meet its quality tiers ([tiers](https://android-developers.googleblog.com/2025/03/introducing-widget-quality-tiers.html)). There is no web widget on iPhone, where widgets need a native WidgetKit extension ([Apple](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/widgetkit/creating-a-widget-extension); [firt.dev, October 2026](https://firt.dev/notes/pwa-ios/)), nor in Android Chrome, whose team called WebView-based widgets "not an option" ([W3C minutes, 13 November 2025](https://www.w3.org/2025/11/13-pwa-widgets-minutes.html)). Windows 11 has Edge-only, experimental PWA widgets ([Microsoft](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-edge/progressive-web-apps/how-to/widgets)). They are not worth building: the service worker would have to move to `injectManifest` against vite-plugin-pwa's hold, to show the same plain widget the shortcuts nearly match.

**What the gardener sees.** Android's widget picker, the icon's long press, and one card in Settings (Android only, beside the calendar reminder): (1) The picker lists "The Butterfly Garden": "A quiet meadow with two gentle ways in. It never shows what you write." The Settings card, "A garden on your Home screen", has one button, **Add to Home screen**, and is the only place the app mentions it: no prompts, ever. (2) The widget shows the Sunlit Meadow painting (its night one in dark theme), the name and two rounded buttons, **Check in** and **Breathe**. It resizes from 2×1 to 4×2, has 48 dp targets, follows the phone's font size, and looks the same every day: no counts, streaks or "today" state. (3) Check in opens `#/today/check-in` with nothing chosen; Breathe opens `#/breathe`, and Back goes to the garden. With the lock on, "Your garden is locked" comes first, then the chosen page. A long press on the icon offers Check in, Today and Journal, each still behind the lock.

**What it stores, and what older builds do.** Nothing. No store, field or version changes (`DATABASE_VERSION` and the state version stay 8), `validateGarden` is untouched, backups keep their shape (invariant 6), and nothing joins the lock's sealed stores. Cross-tab sync does not apply: the app has one WebView, and the bundler folds `VITE_PLATFORM`, so the PWA gets no widget code. The route arrives as an ordinary hash change, which `lib/routes.ts` and `App.tsx`'s `popstate` listener already follow. Play blocks downgrades; on a rolled-back build without the receiver, Android removes the widget and no garden data is touched. The receiver's name, `io.github.dionnedeh.butterflygarden.widget.GardenWidgetProvider`, is permanent once shipped: Gratitude's package refactor removed every placed widget (the same case study), so a test pins it.

**Privacy and safety.** Nothing leaves the device: no network code, no WorkManager, alarms or services (`updatePeriodMillis` 0), and it never wakes `GardenBillingPlugin`, keeping P-23's promise about when Play is asked. The exposure is the screen. A home screen is visible to anyone using the unlocked phone, and since Android 16 QPR2 Pixels can put widgets on a lock-screen hub ([9to5Google](https://9to5google.com/2025/08/20/android-qpr2-lock-screen-widgets/)). So the widget shows nothing from the garden (no names, words, moods, counts, jar letters or photos) and declares `WIDGET_CATEGORY_NOT_KEYGUARD` from a `res/xml-v36` copy, a hint that hosts may ignore. Its labels still hint at a wellbeing app on a shared phone (D-90). `MainActivity` is already exported, so any app could send the route extra: only fixed routes are honoured, the hash is built from string constants and never from intent text, and a relaunch from Recents is ignored. The Data safety answers do not change for the plain widget. A picture of the garden would change them, since Play counts an on-device transfer to another app as sharing unless the user starts it ([Play Console Help](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10787469?hl=en)).

**How to build it.**

1. `android/app/src/main/java/io/github/dionnedeh/butterflygarden/widget/WidgetRoute.java`, pure and JVM-tested like `DailyEvent.java`: a `ROUTE` extra (`check-in`, `breathe`, `garden`, `today`, `journal`) maps to a fixed hash, and unknown values and relaunches from history map to nothing.
2. `MainActivity.java`: a `WebViewListener` on the bridge builder, and an `onNewIntent` that runs `evaluateJavascript` with that hash once the page has loaded (`BridgeActivity.load()` covers a cold start). No routing code joins the first load.
3. `widget/GardenWidgetProvider.java` with `res/layout/garden_widget.xml` (and `layout-v31`), each button an explicit `PendingIntent` to `MainActivity` with `FLAG_IMMUTABLE`; `res/xml/garden_widget_info.xml` and its `xml-v36` copy; day and night `widget_meadow.webp` from a new `scripts/generate-widget-art.py` beside `generate-android-icons.py`; colours checked at 4.5:1 over both paintings.
4. `AndroidManifest.xml`: the receiver, and `res/xml/shortcuts.xml` mirroring `src/pwaManifest.ts`, including "If you need support now" if D-61 adds it to the PWA's shortcuts (B-C-1).
5. The Settings card inside `SettingsView.tsx`'s Android block, through `src/lib/widget.ts` (an inline `VITE_PLATFORM` check) and a small `requestPin()` plugin method. Then ARCHITECTURE, `docs/android-play-plan.md`, the P-20 checklist in `docs/play-store/README.md`, the four checks and the full Android checks.

**How it is tested.** JVM `WidgetRouteTest`, beside `DailyEventTest`: each route maps to its hash, anything else to nothing, and the statement holds only whitelisted constants whatever the extra says; another JVM test pins the receiver's name. A Vitest reads `shortcuts.xml` and checks its routes against `pwaManifest.ts`. `e2e/android.spec.ts` sets the hash as `MainActivity` does: Today opens at its check-in with nothing selected, Breathe's Back returns to the garden, and with the lock on the lock screen comes first, each with axe. `lintRelease` and the merged manifest show no new permission (P-4). On a recent Pixel and an older Samsung (P-20): cold and warm taps, a Recents relaunch, the night art, TalkBack, the largest font, resizing, and whether Pixel's lock-screen hub honours `not_keyguard`.

**Risks, and what keeps them small.**

- **A benefit that cannot be shown.** One self-selected vendor figure and small, old studies, in an app with no analytics. It stays only on testers' word, and costs 0 B of first load if it goes.
- **Shared phones and native upkeep.** Even the plain widget tells an onlooker the app is there, and a cue can feel like a duty, so it exists only if the gardener places it and never shows state. A receiver name that can never change, OEM launchers, and routing that leans on Capacitor internals (`addWebViewListener`, `load()` calling `onNewIntent`) are caught by the e2e and device checks on each upgrade.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-90.** Pilot the plain widget and long-press shortcuts as a 4.x update on a testing track after 4.0 is live, with no picture of the gardener's garden (that needs a new policy item under D-6, the Data safety answers checked again under P-4, and 53 B of the Android first load, measured), no Windows Edge widget, and no in-app prompt beyond one Settings card? And on a shared phone: keep "Check in" and "Breathe" or offer a quieter pair, and include Journal among the shortcuts? *Recommendation:* yes to the pilot, plain only, no Edge widget, no prompts. On the labels and Journal the research does not lean.

#### c.7.3 Speaking instead of typing, on the device only

**In one line.** An opt-in Speak button beside the check-in note, the reflection and the evening recap's two written answers, using only a recogniser that runs on the device and never falling back to a cloud one, offered in desktop Chrome and Firefox only.

**Recommendation:** pilot, desktop web only, after 4.0 is submitted and its closed test is under way (P-22); not in the Android app; do not build if the owner declines the policy passage or the speech-pack download · **Effort:** M (L with an Android plugin) · **First load:** 0 B on both builds, estimated: Today, the recap and Settings are already lazy pages, the speaking chunk (about 2 to 3 kB gzip) loads only once speaking is set up, and the Android build folds the whole web path away · **Waits on:** D-91; D-6 (a new policy passage, approved afresh); D-28 (English only)

**Why it matters.** Some gardeners find typing hard work: motor impairment, RSI, dyslexia, or the fatigue of a low day, when three typed sentences feel like too many. In a scoping review of 8 studies and 5 reports, speech-to-text improved the writing of adolescents with learning difficulties, but inaccuracy was the main challenge ([Matre and Cameron, 2022](https://doi.org/10.1080/17483107.2022.2149865)), and a review of 2008 to 2018 found mixed results against keyboard and handwriting ([NCEO Report 414](https://nceo.umn.edu/publications/OnlinePubs/report414/default.html)). Nothing says speaking is better for wellbeing. Across 146 disclosure trials (average r = .075), the mode, whether handwriting, typing or talking, did not change the effect, and talking to a recorder came out at r = .048 against .106 to a person, from 6 studies and not significant ([Frattaroli, 2006](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17073523/); [full text](https://www.gwern.net/doc/psychology/2006-frattaroli.pdf)). So the claim is ease and access, not a better outcome. What only the garden's own button can promise is where the voice goes: Gboard's Rambler is "temporarily processed by Google" ([Gboard Help](https://support.google.com/gboard/answer/17468539?hl=en)), though iPhone keyboard dictation stays on the phone in many languages ([Apple](https://support.apple.com/guide/iphone/dictate-text-iph2c0651d2/ios)).

**Where it works.** As of 17 November 2026. The spec's `processLocally = true` means recognition "MUST be performed locally", and with no local recogniser `start()` errors with `service-not-allowed` instead of going to a server ([Web Speech API, Community Group draft of 27 October 2026](https://webaudio.github.io/web-speech-api/)). **Desktop Chrome** on Windows, macOS and Linux since 139 (5 August 2025: [release notes](https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/139)), with a dictation quality hint in 150 ([notes](https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/150)) and inferred punctuation in 151 ([notes](https://developer.chrome.com/release-notes/151)). **Desktop Firefox** since 160 (10 November 2026), on-device only in 40 languages, with no cloud recognition at all ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Firefox/Releases/160)); it is a week old. **Not the iPhone or iPad PWA:** WebKit has only the prefixed `webkitSpeechRecognition`, whose audio "may be sent to Apple" ([Apple](https://www.apple.com/legal/privacy/data/en/ask-siri-dictation/)). **Not Android Chrome:** "Android & ChromeOS currently do not support on-device Web Speech" ([blink-dev, 6 May 2026](https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/P8P-x7AnC6I); [compat data](https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/api/SpeechRecognition.json)). Not desktop Safari, nor Edge stable, whose local model is behind a flag in Canary and Dev ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-edge/web-platform/speech-recognition-api)). **Not the Android app:** its WebView offers a recogniser that is not local ([Chromium source](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/android_webview/lib/aw_main_delegate.cc)), so the web path is compiled out, not feature-detected; a native `createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizer` needs API 31, and Android gives no explicit promise that the maker's on-device service stays off the network ([SpeechRecognizer](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/speech/SpeechRecognizer)). Phones keep their keyboard's own microphone in every field.

**What the gardener sees.** (1) In Settings' "This garden stays on this device" card, and only where this browser can listen on the device itself, "Speaking instead of typing" says the voice "is heard by this browser on this computer only: the garden never records it, keeps it or sends it anywhere"; that "the browser fetches its English speech pack once from its maker. Nothing you say or write goes with it."; and "Speak where it feels private: words said aloud can be overheard." **Set up speaking** fetches the pack, then "Speaking is ready." (2) On Today and in the recap a **Speak** button sits after each field, named "Speak your note" or "Speak your reflection". Pressing it shows "Listening… press Stop when you're done."; Stop or Escape ends it, and words heard so far show faintly, hidden from screen readers. (3) Final words are added to the field for the gardener to read and change, never saved by speaking alone: "Stopped. Your words are in the box to read and change." If anything fails: "Speaking isn't ready on this device just now. Your words are safe to type." Listening also stops after five minutes, when the tab is hidden and when the garden locks.

**What it stores, and what older builds do.** Nothing new in the garden. Dictated words are ordinary text in the existing drafts (`mood-note:{day}`, `reflection:{day}` and the recap's two) and entries, so `validateGarden`, `CURRENT_STATE_VERSION` and `DATABASE_VERSION` (8), the backup (invariant 6) and every older build see typed words. The one new item is a per-device switch, `butterfly-garden:speaking` (on, and the language tag), in `localStorage` behind try/catch, as `lib/install.ts` keeps the Home Screen guide's flag: never in the profile, meta, a backup or a move file, since it describes this browser's speech pack, not the garden. Under the lock, drafts holding spoken words are sealed like typed ones; locking unmounts the button, `abort()` drops unfinished words, and `data-lock-loses="What you were saying"` lets the lock screen name them. A `storage` event shows or hides the buttons in other tabs, and drafts behave as they do today (D-34).

**Privacy and safety.** The central risk is audio reaching a cloud recogniser, and the CSP cannot stop it: `connect-src` governs the page's own requests, not the browser's speech service ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy/connect-src); an inference that no source tests directly). So the guard is `processLocally`, in layers: only the unprefixed constructor, and only with `processLocally`, `available()` and `install()` present; one function in `src/lib/dictation.ts` sets it to `true`, reads it back, and starts only after `available()` answered "available" for that language; any error ends the session, with no retry and no second recogniser. MDN's own example falls back to `processLocally = false` after that error ([MDN](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_Speech_API/Using_the_Web_Speech_API)), which is exactly what must never be copied, so lint rules forbid it. The page never calls `getUserMedia` and holds no audio. The one-time pack download is a request to Google or Mozilla that carries nothing of the garden, but the garden causes it, and the README says "nothing is ever requested from a third party" (D-91). The W3C TAG closed its review "satisfied with concerns" ([#1038](https://github.com/w3ctag/design-reviews/issues/1038)). In the Android app, Capacitor grants the page the microphone once `RECORD_AUDIO` is held ([`BridgeWebChromeClient`](https://github.com/ionic-team/capacitor/blob/main/android/capacitor/src/main/java/com/getcapacitor/BridgeWebChromeClient.java)), and the permission would show on the Play listing for everyone ([Play policy](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/16558241)): one more reason to keep it out, as the camera is kept out today (D-21).

**How to build it.**

1. Once D-91 is answered, `src/lib/speaking.ts`: the switch and its `storage` subscription on `lib/install.ts`'s model, and the language (the first English tag in `navigator.languages`, else `en-US`, as D-28 recommends).
2. `src/lib/dictation.ts`, the only module allowed to name `SpeechRecognition`, all inside the literal `import.meta.env.VITE_PLATFORM !== 'android'` check as `lib/native.ts` does: `onDeviceSupport()`, `prepare()` with a ten-minute "not ready" timeout (Brave's `install()` once never resolved: [issue #55414](https://github.com/brave/brave-browser/issues/55414)), `isReady()`, and `listen()` with the five-minute cap.
3. A lazy `src/components/SpeakButton.tsx` and `speak.css`, after the note and reflection in `TodayView.tsx` and in steps 2 and 3 of `MoonlightRecap.tsx`, through `setMoodNote`, `setReflection`, `setWentWell` and `setSettingDown`, clipped at 280, 4,000 and 400 characters.
4. A lazy `src/components/SpeakingSetting.tsx` in `SettingsView.tsx`'s privacy card, rendering nothing where support is "none"; and in `eslint.config.js`, rules forbidding `webkitSpeechRecognition`, any `processLocally` but the literal `true`, and `SpeechRecognition` outside `dictation.ts`.
5. The passage in `public/privacy.html` once approved, the Guide, `releases.ts`, a line under invariant 8 in ARCHITECTURE, then the four checks and the budgets on both builds.

**How it is tested.** Unit: a fake recogniser whose `processLocally` accessor records every write sees only `true` through every error, with one recogniser per press; `start()` never runs unless `available()` said "available"; a never-settling `install()` is "not ready"; with `VITE_PLATFORM` set to `android` nothing is constructed, and `dist-android` holds no `processLocally` string. Component: no Speak button by default, so the visual baselines are unchanged; names start with the visible word (W-1's label-in-name); focus returns to the field; `abort()` runs on unmount. `e2e/speak.spec.ts` injects a fake recogniser and walks set-up, Speak, Save and the Journal with axe; a Safari-shaped fake and the `android-webview` project show no Speak anywhere.

**Risks, and what keeps them small.**

- **The promise rests on browsers.** The spec's MUST is a Community Group draft, the CSP cannot catch a browser that ignores it, and Brave once exposed the API with nothing finished behind it. The guards hold only while the lint rules and source tests are kept.
- **Narrow reach, real exposure.** It runs on desktops, where typing is easiest, not on phones, where it would help most. Words said aloud can be overheard and feeling words misheard, so it is opt-in and never nudged, and words land in the field to be checked, never saved, counted or rewarded by speaking alone.

**Questions for the owner.**

- **D-91.** Pilot speaking on desktop web only, after 4.0's closed test is under way: approve a "Speaking instead of typing" passage in `public/privacy.html` beyond D-6; accept that setting it up makes the browser fetch its speech pack once from Google or Mozilla; keep the microphone out of the Android app until Android documents an on-device guarantee or the WebView supports `processLocally`; English only, as D-28 recommends; and the four fields named here, with five-minute sessions? *Recommendation:* yes to each. If the owner declines the passage or the download, do not build, and give the Guide a line saying the keyboard's own microphone works in every field, and that where it sends a voice is the keyboard's choice.

### c.8 Considered, and not recommended

**In one line.** Six ideas were researched and are not recommended: breathing written to Health Connect, computed lines pairing acts with the weather, on-device AI summaries, Argon2id now, passkeys inside the Android app, and notifications; each has a smaller, later or gardener-led path instead.

**Recommendation:** do not build any of the six as researched, in 4.0 or after it. Revisit Argon2id when browsers ship it natively, and passkeys in the app once D-7 gives the garden a domain of its own · **Effort:** none now. As researched, Health Connect was L and the observations M; the Journal filter that replaces the observations is M and sits in (b) · **First load:** 0 B, since nothing here is built. The filter is 0 B on both builds, estimated, because `JournalSearch` is already in the lazy Journal chunk · **Waits on:** nothing, to say no. D-7 before passkeys in the app are looked at again; D-23's recommendation (keep PBKDF2) holds

A no is a finding too. Each idea below was researched as c.1 to c.7 were: the code it would touch, the platforms as they stood on 17 November 2026, and the evidence that it would help a gardener. Each is turned down for a reason the owner can check, and each says what would change the answer.

**(1) Breathing sessions in Health Connect: do not build.** In the Android app, an opt-in would write each finished breath as a `MindfulnessSessionRecord` of type breathing, start and end time only, write-only. The benefit is convenience in another app's dashboard: [Google Health](https://support.google.com/googlehealth/answer/14237928) counts mindfulness minutes and "active mindful days". The evidence is for the breathing itself, which the garden already offers ([Fincham et al. 2023](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9828383/): 12 trials, 785 adults, g = −0.35 on stress; [Balban et al. 2023](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9873947/) for cyclic sighing). No study was found showing that logging breathing into a health store helps, and measuring an activity can make it feel like work ([Etkin 2016](https://doi.org/10.1093/jcr/ucv095)).

- **Cost.** "All access requests for health & fitness and body sensor permissions will be subject to review" ([Play Console Help](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/12991134)), the declaration may be reviewed again with each version, and until it is approved users meet a "can't access" dialog, so it cannot ship dark ([Android Developers](https://developer.android.com/health-and-fitness/guides/health-connect/publish/declare-access)). Data safety counts a transfer to another app on the device as sharing, unless it follows a user-initiated action or prominent disclosure ([Play Console Help](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10787469)). The privacy policy would need a new section beyond what D-6 approved.
- **Privacy.** The record leaves app-private storage for a shared store that any app the gardener allows can read, copy and upload. The lock cannot seal it, and it outlives an uninstall ([Android Help](https://support.google.com/android/answer/12201872?hl=en)).
- **Reach.** Android 16 has the record in the platform ([reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/health/connect/datatypes/MindfulnessSessionRecord)); Android 14 and 15 only once Health Connect's module reaches U-extension 15. The stable `connect-client` 1.1.0 has no path below Android 14, and only its 1.2.0 alphas add one ([release notes](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/health-connect)). About 56 % of Android devices ran 14 or later on 1 October 2026 ([Composables, from Google's figures](https://composables.com/android-distribution-chart)). Neither HealthKit nor Health Connect has a web API ([Apple](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/healthkit/hkcategorytypeidentifier/mindfulsession)), so no iPhone, desktop or PWA gardener could have it.
- **Instead.** Nothing now. If gardeners ask once 4.0 is live, the research's minimal design is ready: the platform `HealthConnectManager` called from Java, `WRITE_MINDFULNESS` only and never a read, no new library, 0 B of first load (estimated). Never the Jetpack route, whose 1.3 MB AAR declares minSdk 26 and brings Guava, coroutines and protobuf ([Google Maven](https://dl.google.com/android/maven2/androidx/health/connect/connect-client/1.1.0/connect-client-1.1.0.pom)).

**(2) Computed observations pairing acts with the weather: do not build.** "On days you breathed, your weather was more often Bright" sounds kind. At a garden's scale it is mostly untrue. A simulation of 5,000 months per case (six kinds of act, no real link between any act and the weather, 12 to 28 check-ins in 30 days, re-run for this report) found that a plain rule (at least 5 days on each side, 20 points more often Bright or Radiant) invents a pattern in about 38 to 60 % of months. An honest rule (one-sided Fisher exact test, Holm-corrected at 5 %) invents one in 1 to 2 %, but then catches a large real effect (45 % to 70 % Bright on breathing days) in only about 1 to 6 % of months, and about 30 % even after 85 check-ins in 90 days. Picking whichever pairing looks strongest is [the garden of forking paths](https://stat.columbia.edu/~gelman/research/unpublished/forking.pdf). Real data is worse: weather follows weather, people do more on days they already feel bright, and some avoid entering hard moods at all ([Schueller et al. 2021](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8387890/)).

- **Harm.** 56.5 % of students with a diagnosed mental illness had felt their tracked data reflected something negative about them ([Kelley et al. 2017](https://microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2017/02/Self-Tracking4MentalWellness-CHI2017.pdf)). Personal data can tip reflection into rumination ([Eikey et al. 2021](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00779-021-01573-w)), and self-focus goes with negative affect most strongly in clinical samples ([Mor & Winquist 2002](https://cris.huji.ac.il/en/publications/self-focused-attention-and-negative-affect-a-meta-analysis/)). Feedback helped depression when a clinician gave it weekly, in person ([Kramer et al. 2014](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3918026)); in routine care it added nothing ([ZELF-i](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9650062)), and a feedback loop in bipolar disorder trended, not significantly, toward more depression ([MONARCA I](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26220802/)). On a stormy day, "on days you walked, you felt brighter" reads as blame.
- **Promises.** The Journal says "without scores or analysis" (`JournalView.tsx:261`), the Guide "nothing you write is scored or analyzed", and the letter "No scores and no advice". An inferred pattern is also a calculation a gardener cannot easily check, which [the MHRA](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/digital-mental-health-technology-qualification-and-classification) counts as high functionality. With no analytics, a harm would never be seen. Voyager I's c.11 reached the same answer.
- **Instead.** "Days when I…", which (b) carries as B-J-8 (D-59): chips for "I breathed", "I noticed nature" and "I closed the evening" beside the weather filter in `components/JournalSearch.tsx` and `lib/journalSearch.ts`, and one line that says only what was found: "Showing 9 days when breathing counted as care." The gardener looks and draws their own conclusions. The words say "counted as care" because a breath on a day whose Sunlight is already full leaves no record today.

**(3) On-device AI summaries with Chrome's built-in Gemini Nano: do not build.** A summary of the week's entries, written by a model on the device, would reach almost no gardener, and it would be analysis of their words.

- **Where it runs.** Chrome's [Summarizer API](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/ai/summarizer-api) is stable from Chrome 138, but only on Windows 10 or 11, macOS 13 or later, Linux and Chromebook Plus, with at least 22 GB free on the profile's volume and more than 4 GB of video memory, or 16 GB of RAM and 4 cores. Chrome for Android and iOS are not supported. The [Prompt API's intent to ship](https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/iR6R7-nQeHI) (1 April 2026, desktop in Chrome 148) leaves Android for later and says "this API is not available in WebView". So: no iPhone, no Android phone, not the Android app, and no Firefox or Safari.
- **What it would break.** A hosted model would reach everyone, but only by sending the journal off the device, which invariant 8 rules out. On the device, it is the same inference (2) declines, with the same promises broken, and a generated sentence can be wrong about how someone felt.
- **Instead.** The weekly letter keeps saying what the week held in counts (`lib/summaries.ts`), and Moonlight's "What went well today?" lets the gardener say it in their own words.

**(4) Argon2id now: no, as D-23 recommends.** D-23 recommends keeping PBKDF2 at 600,000 rounds, because Argon2id in a browser needs a WebAssembly module, a new dependency. That is still true.

- **Where it stands.** Node 24.8.0 has Argon2id natively in its Web Crypto, and `SubtleCrypto.supports()` since 24.7.0 ([Node.js](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v24.x/api/webcrypto.html)). Browsers do not. The algorithm is in WICG's [Modern Algorithms in the Web Cryptography API](https://wicg.github.io/webcrypto-modern-algos/), a draft community report dated 23 October 2026, and Chrome's [June 2026 experiment](https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/TF8y175J3YU) covers ML-KEM, ML-DSA, ChaCha20-Poly1305 and X-Wing, not Argon2.
- **A trap, and an open door.** CI runs Node 24, so a unit test can see an Argon2id that no gardener's browser has; any future check must be tested both present and absent. Each lock slot already names its `kdf` (`lib/gardenLock.ts`), and a locked copy with any other name is refused as "newer" (`lib/lockedCopy.ts`), so a later Argon2id slot needs no format change now. The stored lock record would need the same guard first.
- **Instead.** WP-C 1's passcode advice is the lever: four unrelated words take about 30 GPU-years (F-14). Revisit D-23 when `SubtleCrypto.supports?.('importKey', 'Argon2id')` is true in stable Safari, Chrome and Firefox.

**(5) Passkeys inside the Android app: not before D-7.** The PWA already offers passkeys where the browser has PRF (`lib/passkey.ts`). The app could too, but not yet.

- **Possible.** Android WebView supports WebAuthn from androidx.webkit 1.12 through `WebSettingsCompat.setWebAuthenticationSupport`, with androidx.credentials and its Play services module ([Android Developers](https://developer.android.com/identity/sign-in/credential-manager-webview)).
- **Why not now.** It needs Digital Asset Links with "a website that your app owns". The app is served from `https://localhost` (`capacitor.config.ts`), and the PWA still shares `dionnedeh.github.io` (F-13, D-7). It would also bring Google's credential libraries into the app, and a synced passkey leaves the phone.
- **Instead.** c.5's fingerprint slot, sealed by the Android Keystore, gives the same ease without either. Revisit once D-7 is settled and WP-C 7 sets an explicit `rp.id` on the garden's own domain.

**(6) Notifications: the garden deliberately sends none.** The README says so: a daily visit goes into the gardener's own calendar as an event file (`components/ReminderSetting.tsx`), and the calendar does the reminding.

- **Platforms.** A web page cannot schedule its own notification without a server. Chrome's Notification Triggers "has ended" ([Chrome for Developers](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/web-platform/notification-triggers)), and Web Push needs a server to send through, which invariant 8 rules out. The Android app could post local ones, but only by adding a nudge the garden has promised not to send.
- **Evidence.** In a field study of 24 people over 18 days, reminder notifications were rated more intrusive than a glanceable phone surface (2.22 against 1.77 on a 5-point scale) and led to fewer entries ([Zhang et al. 2016](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5553980/)). It measured logging, not wellbeing.
- **Instead.** Keep the calendar, and let c.7's widget be the quiet way back: something the gardener looks at, not something that calls them.

**Questions for the owner.** None new. Each answer here follows a decision already made, a recommendation already put to the owner, or a promise the app makes in its own words.

- Confirm the Journal's "without scores or analysis" promise stands, so neither (2) nor (3) is built, and (b)'s filter (B-J-8, D-59) answers the need.
- Health Connect: revisit only if gardeners ask once 4.0 is live. D-39 (the streak) bears on whether the garden should feed another app's "mindful days".
- D-23 (open, recommended: keep PBKDF2) and D-7 (to be talked through) stay as they are, with the two revisit triggers above written into `docs/plans/INDEX.md` (WP-H 5).

### c.9 What to build, and when

Nothing in (c) goes into 4.0. What 4.0 carries from (a) is the groundwork
these capabilities stand on. The versions follow a.6 and b.10, and the owner
sets the dates.

| When | Capabilities | What must come first |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **4.0**, the Play submission | None | (a)'s groundwork: WP-B's one baseline (F-1), WP-E 1's truncating save (F-9) and WP-E 2's pieces (F-10), WP-A 7's dialogs (F-11), WP-C 1's passcode advice (F-14, D-47), and WP-F 3, which frees the first-load bytes |
| **4.1**, beside (b)'s everyday release | c.3, the letter. c.6's Android share | Room left after 4.0 (D-10). WP-C 6 (F-20) before the share takes a photo under the lock |
| **4.2** | c.1, Recently removed, with or just after B-J-5's Undo. c.6's CSV, as B-J-3's "Field book as CSV", once D-25 is answered | WP-A 10's honest delete words (F-38) and B-S-2's comparison by entry id (c.1). WP-E 1's truncating save (F-9) for the CSV on Android |
| **Once 4.0 is in production** (P-22), a gate rather than a version | c.2, the desktop half and then the Android twin. c.4 in its two steps: first where the garden lives, copies and the recovery-code check; then offline, and what the page fetched. c.5, fingerprint or face. c.7.1's Stage A, deletion markers with no new screen | WP-C 5 names the kept file (F-16). B-S-3's glance card, B-S-7's checks, B-H-1's Settings parts and WP-H 1's privacy fixture (F-39) for c.4. Device runs under P-20, and P-14's kind of matrix for c.5 |
| **Pilots**, on a Play testing track or behind a build-time flag | c.7.1's merge (Stage B), c.7.2's widget, c.7.3's speaking on desktop | Each is kept only if testers find it gentle. Each has a clean way out: the flag, the testing track, or 0 B of first load |
| **After c.6's Android share** | c.6's PWA share target, in two releases: the worker's handler first, then the manifest entry | Nothing from W-3: it uses `generateSW`'s `importScripts`, so it need not wait for the January 2027 review of the vite-plugin-pwa hold (D-86) |
| **Not now** | c.8's six | Three have a trigger written in `docs/plans/INDEX.md` (WP-H 5): Argon2id in stable browsers, D-7 for passkeys in the app, and gardeners asking for Health Connect |

Each step keeps CLAUDE.md's four checks green, with its tests in the same
change, and runs `npm run budgets` on both builds. c.2, c.4, c.5, c.6 and
c.7.2 touch `android/` or code behind `isAndroidApp()`, and c.7.3 changes what
the Android build drops, so those six also run the Android checks.

### c.10 Questions for the owner

Each question is set out in full, with its reasons, in its capability's own
part. They continue section (b)'s numbering, from D-73. Two questions already
asked bear on (c) too: D-25 (sightings in exports) gates c.6's CSV, and D-24
(when the garden's day turns) sets the hour a letter opens.

| Id | Capability | The question, in short | Recommendation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-73 | c.1 | Build Recently removed after 4.0, for check-ins, reflections, recaps and sightings, spending about 120 B of the first load? | Yes, with or just after B-J-5. Goals keep their Undo. |
| D-74 | c.1 | Should "Delete for good" also clear the entry from the daily and pre-restore copies? | Either is honest. The owner's call. |
| D-75 | c.1 | Keep removed entries 30 days, with caps of 100 entries, 50 photos and about 20 MB; always on, or with a switch? | 30 days and those caps. A switch, if there is one, starts on. |
| D-76 | c.2 | Should a kept sealed file carry both ways in, or the recovery code only? | Both, with the passcode advice beside it. |
| D-77 | c.2 | Build it once 4.0 is in production, the desktop half first, with new privacy and SECURITY.md wording and about 43 B of the first load? | Yes. |
| D-78 | c.2 | Should the card name the places it recognises, Google Drive's 30-day history included? Should P-20 check whether today's setting already shows in Chrome on Android? | Yes to both. |
| D-79 | c.3 | Build the letter after 4.0, free for everyone, with one privacy-policy bullet and about 114 to 124 B? | Yes. |
| D-80 | c.3 | Are these the right limits? A month, a season, a year, or a day up to five years ahead; 2,000 characters; 24 kept; held on a gentle day; left out of the readable copies; nothing earned. | Yes to each. |
| D-81 | c.4 | Build the check as its own Settings card, in two steps? Hide "days written since" when day counts are hidden or on a gentle day? Link B-S-3's card to it? | Yes to all three. Never call it a "health check". |
| D-82 | c.4 | Include "What this page has fetched", with its limits always shown? | Yes, in the second step. Leave it out if its limits would ever have to be softened. |
| D-83 | c.5 | Build fingerprint or face as an early 4.x release: off by default, `BIOMETRIC_STRONG` only, Android 11 and later, with new wording approved? | Yes to each. |
| D-84 | c.5 | Ask for the passcode after seven days without it? Should the prompt open by itself, or only on a tap? | Seven days. Only on a tap. |
| D-85 | c.5 | When it is turned off, delete the phone's key and drop the slot, or also re-seal the garden? Confirm no Keystore "pepper" on the passcode. | Either is safe. No pepper. |
| D-86 | c.6 | Build in this order: the Android share in 4.1, the CSV with B-J-3 after D-25, then the PWA share target through `importScripts`? Approve one privacy sentence, and leave out a Settings switch? | Yes to all three. |
| D-87 | c.6 | The CSV's defaults: notes left out, geoprivacy "obscured", tags blank. And a two-row trial upload before release? | Yes. |
| D-88 | c.7.1 | Pilot the merge: deletion markers a release ahead, then a build-time flag, with one privacy sentence and about 20 to 115 B? | Yes to each. |
| D-89 | c.7.1 | The merge's choices: a same-day pair settled with nothing preselected; game state from this garden; deletions win; photos past 50 left out; another garden allowed, with a note; offered from Settings only. | Yes to each, as worded. |
| D-90 | c.7.2 | Pilot the plain widget and shortcuts on a testing track: no picture of the garden, no Edge widget, one Settings card? On a shared phone, which labels, and should Journal be a shortcut? | Yes to the pilot, plain only. The labels are the owner's call. |
| D-91 | c.7.3 | Pilot speaking on desktop web only? That means a privacy passage, the browser's one-time speech-pack download, no microphone in the Android app, and English only. | Yes to each. If the passage or the download is declined, do not build. |
